**Storage.** `.notesapp/bookmarks.json`, in the format of Obsidian's `.obsidian/bookmarks.json`: `{"items": [...]}`, where an item is a `file` (with an optional `subpath` such as `#Heading` or `#^blockid`), `folder`, `search` or `group` (with nested `items`), plus Obsidian's `ctime` and optional `title` (API.md §21). Obsidian's other types (`url`, `graph`) and any unknown field, at the item or the top level, are kept: the server keeps items as JSON trees (`ObjectNode`) and `BookmarkCodec` only checks the fields it knows. The file is written pretty-printed with two-space indents, as Obsidian writes it, in the order the items have.

**`BookmarkService`.**

- **Read on every request.** The file is small, and the watcher ignores `.notesapp/`, so reading it each time is what lets a hand edit, a pull from the git mirror or a history restore show at once. Items that break the codec's rules are left out (logged). A file that is not a JSON object reads as no bookmarks; the next save first copies it to `bookmarks.json.bak-<timestamp>`.
- **No file yet.** The bookmarks of `.obsidian/bookmarks.json` are served when Obsidian left one (read only; `.obsidian/` is never written, §4.1), otherwise none, with `version` set to the blob id of the document as it would be written. The first change writes NotesApp's own file. A vault moved over from Obsidian therefore keeps its bookmarks without an import step.
- **Save** (`PUT /api/bookmarks`) follows the settings (§5.12): validate, take the service's lock (`tryLock(5 s)`, else `503 busy`), read the disk, and serialize the new document; equal bytes return `200` without writing; a `baseVersion` that differs from the disk's blob id is `409 version-conflict` with the file's JSON as `currentContent`; otherwise `prepareWrite`, `atomicWrite`, `markDirty` (committed with the next batch, like the settings) and `bookmarks.changed` with the request's `X-Client-Id` as origin, published before the lock is released.
- **Following targets** (re-keying), always server-side so every device sees the same list:
  - `MoveService` calls `moved(from, to, type)` after both commits of a move (§5.6 step 8), still under the vault write lock: a file's bookmarks (headings and blocks included) take the new path; for a folder, its own bookmark and every bookmark whose path is under it take the new prefix (the rest of the path is kept segment by segment). Matching ignores case, like link resolution, unless an entry with the bookmark's exact path still exists (a case-variant duplicate keeps its bookmark).
  - `TrashService` calls `restored(originalPath, target, type)` when an entry comes back at another path (an explicit `to`, or a free name): bookmarks of the original path follow it, but only when nothing exists at the original path, so a bookmark whose path a new note has taken keeps pointing at that note.
  - A delete leaves bookmarks alone, exactly as it leaves links: the client shows them as missing until the target comes back (§6.23). Moves and deletes made outside the app are not followed, as for links (§5.9).
  - A re-key writes the file under the bookmarks lock (taken after the vault lock; the `PUT` path never takes the vault lock, so the order is fixed), marks it dirty with the mover's identity, and publishes `bookmarks.changed` with `origin: null`. The calling client's `MoveResult` says nothing about bookmarks, so the event must reach it too; a failure is logged and never fails the move.
- **Why a whole-list `PUT`** rather than per-item endpoints: Obsidian's items have no ids, the list is small, and the settings already prove the version protocol. Clients apply each change as a function of the latest list and re-apply it after a `409` (§6.23), so concurrent changes from two devices both survive.

### 5.22 Heading links after a heading is renamed (ROADMAP item 21)

`HeadingRenameService` (`POST /api/notes/rename-heading`, API.md §22). The client renames the heading in the note and saves it, then asks for the links to follow; Obsidian has no equivalent.

1. **Check.** `from` and `to` are one line each and differ beyond case and spacing (the comparison heading targets use, §6.4). Under the vault write lock the note is reconciled (an external edit not seen yet is indexed first), and it must have a heading `to` and no heading `from` any more: while a heading still reads the old text, links to it are not broken, and rewriting them would send them elsewhere. Otherwise `400 bad-request`.
2. **Plan.** The sources are the note's backlink sources plus the note itself (same-note `[[#Old]]` links). Each source is read from disk (from the index in a dry run) and scanned; a link qualifies when it resolves to the note (an empty target is the source itself) and a `#` segment of its subpath names `from` (block ids, `^…`, never do). Only the **subpath span** is spliced: the wikilink's text after its first `#` up to `|` (or `\|`) or `]]`, or a markdown destination's text after `#`. Target, display, style, embed mark, angle brackets and title stay as they are. The new segment is the heading verbatim in a wikilink and an angle-bracket destination, and `encodeMdPath`-encoded in a plain destination (`Next%20Steps`). A heading a wikilink cannot hold (`|`, `#`, `[`, `]`) or an angle destination cannot hold (`<`, `>`, `#`) makes that link `not-rewritable`; a frontmatter link is written for its scalar's quoting, as in a move (§5.6).
   - **Documents** (§6.8; added when items 4, 5 and 21 met). A backlink source that is not a note (a `.tasks` list, an `.excalidraw` drawing) is never scanned as Markdown: the links it was indexed with say whether one names `from`, and its `DocumentIndexer` then rewrites the file as a whole, as for a move, with a `LinkRewriter` whose `markdown` applies the rules above to each fragment (scanned with `scanFragment`) and whose `path` changes nothing. The new text is written under the same lock and listed with the number of links changed; an indexer that cannot rewrite (a drawing, or a list the task API would not write) leaves the file, reported as `not-rewritable`. A note an indexer supplements (an `.excalidraw.md` drawing) is read from disk even in a dry run, because its indexed text is more than the file, and a supplement link that names `from` makes it `not-rewritable` too.
3. **Write** (not in a dry run). Pending edits are committed first, then each note is spliced from the end backwards, with the move's guards: a link that no longer reads as planned skips the note (`span-mismatch`), invalid UTF-8 is never rewritten (`not-utf8`), and a frontmatter that would stop parsing or change its links skips it (`not-rewritable`). The BOM is kept. One commit, `Update links after renaming heading “Old” → “New” in Plan.md (3 notes)`, then `file.changed` per rewritten note (with the caller as origin; the caller applies `linkUpdates` like a move's), then heading bookmarks of the note that name `from` take `to` (`BookmarkService.headingRenamed`, `bookmarks.changed` with `origin: null`).

### 5.23 Obsidian vault import (ROADMAP item 24)

An existing Obsidian vault is imported by copying it into the vault directory before the first start (DEPLOY.md *Import an Obsidian vault*, CASAOS.md). The package `obsidian` makes the result behave like the vault did in Obsidian, without ever writing `.obsidian/`, so the vault stays usable there.

- **`.obsidian/` is read, never written.** `ObsidianConfigReader` reads `app.json`, `daily-notes.json`, `templates.json`, `graph.json`, `core-plugins.json` (an object of switches, or the older array; `core-plugins-migration.json` as a fallback) and `community-plugins.json` with each enabled plugin's `plugins/<id>/manifest.json` for its name. It does not follow symbolic links, skips files over 1 MiB, accepts a BOM, and reports unreadable files instead of failing. The folder stays hidden from the tree, the index and the API (§4.5: `403 reserved-path`), and is versioned except `workspace*.json` (§4.6).
- **First start: the settings come from Obsidian.** `SettingsService.load()` creates a missing `settings.json` from the defaults passed through every `SettingsSeed` bean; `ObsidianSettings` is the one seed, and a seed whose result fails validation is ignored with a WARN. The mapping:

| Obsidian | NotesApp |
|---|---|
| `app.json` `attachmentFolderPath`: `""` or `/`, `./`, `./sub`, `Folder` | `files.attachmentLocation` `root`, `current`, `subfolder` with `attachmentSubfolder: sub` (a single name), `folder` with `attachmentFolder` |
| `newFileLocation` (`root`, `current`, `folder`) and `newFileFolderPath` | `files.newNoteLocation`, `newNoteFolder` (`folder` with an empty path is the root) |
| `newLinkFormat`, `useMarkdownLinks` | `files.newLinkFormat`, `useWikilinks` (inverted) |
| `alwaysUpdateLinks: true` | `files.autoUpdateLinks: true` (`false` means "ask" in Obsidian; NotesApp's Move dialog always shows the updates, so it is left alone) |
| `userIgnoreFilters` | `files.excludedPaths` (paths only; `/regex/` entries are reported, not used) |
| `strictLineBreaks`, `readableLineLength`, `defaultViewMode` (`source`, `preview`) | `editor.strictLineBreaks`, `readableLineLength`, `defaultMode` (`edit`, `read`) |
| `daily-notes.json` `format` (empty is `YYYY-MM-DD`), `folder`, `template` (`.md` added), `autorun` | `dailyNotes.format`, `folder`, `template`, `openOnStartup` |
| `templates.json` `folder`, `dateFormat`, `timeFormat` (empty is the default) | `templates.*` |

  Folders are NFC-normalized and stripped of slashes; a value NotesApp cannot use (a dot-folder, a nested subfolder, an unknown enum value) is reported and the current setting kept. A later start never re-reads them: an existing `settings.json` wins.
- **The compatibility report** (`GET /api/compat`, API.md §11.5, `CompatService`). Computed on request, nothing stored: what `.obsidian/` holds; one row per Obsidian setting with the NotesApp setting, its current value and Obsidian's (`same`, `different`, `unsupported`), and `proposedSettings` with `settingsVersion`, which the client saves with `PUT /api/settings` to apply them to an existing installation; the graph options of `graph.json` the graph view understands (§6.28); the enabled core and community plugins with what NotesApp offers instead (`ObsidianPlugins`, a table that roadmap items update); and the findings of `CompatScanner`, which runs over a snapshot of the index (`VaultIndex.noteViews()`) outside its lock:

| Category | Findings |
|---|---|
| Syntax | Dataview (```` ```dataview ````, ```` ```dataviewjs ````, inline `` `= …` ``), Tasks queries, embedded searches (```` ```query ````), other plugin blocks (```` ```ad-… ````, ```` ```chart ```` …), Templater `<% … %>`, block references (`#^id`), `obsidian://` links, HTML the sanitizer removes, Kanban boards (`kanban-plugin` property) |
| Links | Embeds and attachment links that lead nowhere; links to missing headings (matched as the client does, §6.4) or missing `^block-ids`; links to notes that do not exist yet (informational: following them creates the note) |
| Files | Names the index skips (`/api/status` `vault.skipped`), notes over the text limit, invalid UTF-8, frontmatter that does not parse, notes with `excalidraw-plugin` properties not named `.excalidraw.md` (which open as Markdown), canvases, bases |

  Callouts, Mermaid and math render since ROADMAP item 3 (§6.7.1), and `.excalidraw`/`.excalidraw.md` drawings open in the drawing editor (item 5), so they are not findings; the plugin table likewise reports bookmarks, Excalidraw, the Tasks plugin and QuickAdd by what items 12, 5, 4 and 7 built.
  Fenced code and code spans are skipped, so a note that *shows* such syntax is not reported. Each finding has a severity (`error`: data NotesApp cannot use; `warning`: shown wrong or not at all; `info`: works differently), the roadmap item that will handle it, an exact count, and at most 200 occurrences (path, 0-based line, text). The rules for rendering that a roadmap item adds (callouts, Mermaid and math with item 3) are removed when it lands.

### 5.24 Transcription (ROADMAP item 33)

Voice notes are recorded in the browser and saved as attachments (§6.30); the server's only part is the optional transcription, `POST /api/transcriptions` (API.md §20), in the `transcribe` package. It is **off by default**: nothing is configured, and nothing runs, until `notes.transcription.url` (`NOTES_TRANSCRIPTION_URL`) names a speech-to-text service. DEPLOY.md *Voice notes* and CASAOS.md *Voice note transcription* set up the sidecar.

**The service** is a separate container, never part of the image: [whisper-asr-webservice](https://github.com/ahmetoner/whisper-asr-webservice) (MIT; faster-whisper on the CPU, ffmpeg for any input format) as the optional `whisper` service of `deploy/docker-compose.yml` (profile `transcription`) and the second CasaOS app `deploy/casaos-whisper-compose.yml` (which joins NotesApp's network). Neither publishes a port: only the app reaches it, from the Tailscale container's network namespace, by its service or container name. It keeps no audio. A different engine works through the second protocol.

**`HttpTranscriptionEngine`** (the `TranscriptionEngine` bean; tests replace it) sends the file as `multipart/form-data`, streamed from disk, named `recording.<ext>` (the service needs the format, not the vault's name), over HTTP/1.1, with no redirects followed:

| `notes.transcription.api` | Request | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `whisper-asr` (default) | `POST <url>/asr?task=transcribe&output=json&encode=true&vad_filter=true[&language=…]`, the file in `audio_file` | `{"text", "language", "segments"}` |
| `openai` | `POST <url>/v1/audio/transcriptions` (the URL is used as is when it already ends in `/audio/transcriptions`) with `model`, `response_format=json`, `[language]` and `file`; `Authorization: Bearer <key>` when `api-key-file` names a file | `{"text"}` |

The `openai` protocol covers whisper.cpp's `whisper-server` (`--convert --inference-path /v1/audio/transcriptions`), speaches, LocalAI and OpenAI's own API. A key is read from its file (in `/data/secrets/`, outside the vault, §4.1) before every request, so it can be rotated without a restart, and never appears in logs, the status or errors. `vad_filter` drops silence before decoding, where Whisper would otherwise invent text. A non-2xx answer becomes an error naming the status and a one-line excerpt of the service's message (FastAPI's `detail`, OpenAI's `error.message`).

**`TranscriptionService`**:

- **Checks.** The path is opened like a raw read (`FileService.openRaw`: path rules, reserved paths, symlinks, `404`); only `audio` and `video` kinds (§4.2) are sent (`415 not-audio`), up to `max-size`, 25 MiB by default, about 50 minutes at the app's 64 kbit/s (`413 too-large`).
- **One at a time.** The service is a CPU on the same small server, so a fair semaphore lets one recording through; others wait up to the timeout.
- **Retries are cheap.** A request for the version (path, size, mtime) of a file that is being transcribed joins that run; the last 32 transcripts stay in memory, so the phone retrying after it slept through the answer gets the text at once (`millis: 0`). A changed file is transcribed again; failures are not kept. Nothing is stored on disk: the client puts the text into the note (§6.30), which makes it searchable like anything typed, versioned and synced.
- **Errors** are `503 transcription-failed` with the reason (`cannot connect to http://whisper:9000`, `no answer within 600 s`, the service's own message), and `409 transcription-off` without a URL. Never `502` or `503 busy`: clients take those for the proxy during a deploy (API.md §3) and would show the app as offline. A failure is logged as `TRANSCRIBE_FAILED` (not an app-error alert marker, §5.18.1: the user sees it at once); a success as `TRANSCRIBE <path> <bytes> in <ms>`, never the text.

`GET /api/transcription` reports `enabled`, the service (protocol, scheme, host and port only), the configured language and `maxBytes`; the client shows it in Settings › This device and uses it to decide whether a new recording is transcribed.

### 5.25 Ask your vault and summaries (ROADMAP item 18)

Questions about the notes answered by Claude with citations, summaries of a note, a folder, a list of notes (this week's daily notes) or a selection, and suggested links, tags and tasks for a note. The wire contract is API.md §24; the code is the `ai` package (§5.2) and `AiController`. The client side is §6.31.

**Two providers** (`AiProviders`, `settings.ai.provider`). The model call sits behind `ClaudeGateway`:

- **Claude Code** (`claude-code`, the default; `ClaudeCodeGateway`): the user's Claude Pro or Max subscription, through the Claude Code CLI that the image ships (`/opt/claude/bin/claude`, `notes.ai.claude-command`, §7.1). One headless run per answer: `claude -p --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --model <id> --system-prompt <the app's> --tools "" --permission-mode dontAsk --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence --disable-slash-commands`, the prompt on stdin. **Every tool is disabled**, so Claude cannot read the vault or anything else: it sees only the notes the app retrieved, and excluded folders stay out. The process gets a fresh, empty home and working directory under `notes.ai.claude-work-dir` (default `<tmp>/notesapp-claude`), deleted afterwards, so no `CLAUDE.md`, setting, hook, MCP server or session is loaded or kept, and a **clean environment** (nothing inherited) holding only `HOME`, `PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1` and `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, the token from `claude setup-token` read from `notes.ai.claude-token-file` (default `/data/secrets/claude-token`). The token is never on the command line. The CLI takes one user message, so earlier turns become a transcript (`<earlier_turns>`) before the new message. The app reads the JSON lines: `stream_event` text deltas go to the browser, `assistant` messages are the fallback for a CLI without partial messages, and `result` gives the stop reason, the usage and `total_cost_usd` (the API value of the run, which the spend guard counts) or the error (`is_error`: a refused token becomes `auth`, a plan's usage limit `rate-limited`). A run that outlives `notes.ai.timeout` or whose browser leaves is killed with its process tree (`ProcessHandle.descendants()`). Usage counts against the subscription's limits; it is for the user's personal use under their plan's terms. Effort, `max_tokens`, the server-side fallback and prompt-cache breakpoints are not controllable through the CLI (Claude Code caches on its own).
- **Anthropic API** (`api`; `AnthropicGateway`, below): an API key, billed per token.

**Off by default, three locks.** A request is refused (`409 ai-unavailable`) unless (1) `settings.ai.enabled` is on (a vault setting, off in the defaults), and (2) the server has the provider's credential (and, for Claude Code, the CLI); and nothing is sent until (3) someone asks. There is no background use: no indexing, embedding or summarizing without a request.

**The credentials** (`ApiKeyFile`: the Claude Code token as above, or the API key) are read the same way. The API key is read from `notes.ai.api-key-file` (`NOTES_AI_API_KEY_FILE`), by default `<notes.secrets-dir>/anthropic-key` (`/data/secrets/anthropic-key`, outside the vault, §4.1): CasaOS uses that path; the plain-compose deploy mounts a compose secret at `/run/secrets/anthropic_key` (§7.2, `deploy/ai/no-key` is its empty placeholder), like the mirror token. The file is read again whenever its size or modification time changes, so a new key needs no restart. Its content must be one printable line; problems are described without quoting it (`"no API key: anthropic-key is empty"`). The key is never in the vault, the settings, `.env`, an environment variable, a log line or an error text, and `ApiKeyFile.Loaded` and the gateway's request records leave it out of `toString()`.

**Privacy rules.**

- Notes under `settings.ai.excludedPaths` (default `Private`) or `settings.files.excludedPaths` (the search exclusions) are never read for a request: retrieval skips them, a folder summary skips them (and counts them, so the panel can say so), an earlier turn's sources that became excluded are dropped, and naming one explicitly (a note, a folder, a selection's note) is refused with `403 ai-excluded` instead of being skipped silently. The suggested tags come only from tags used by notes that are not excluded.
- The server keeps no conversation and no question. Conversations live on each device (§6.31); `.notesapp/cache/ai-usage.json` holds monthly counts only.
- The log line has counts, never content (§5.18).
- Note text inside the prompt is data: the system prompt says never to follow instructions found in a note, notes sit inside `<note>` elements whose closing tag cannot be forged from inside (`</note` is broken up), and the answer is rendered with the reading-view sanitizer (§6.7). An answer cannot write to the vault: the API has no tool use, and the panel only displays text.

**Retrieval** ("BM25 from the existing index; embeddings come later"). `QueryTerms` tokenizes the question exactly like the search index (§5.5.10) and drops common English words and single letters. `VaultIndex.rankAny` ranks every note containing **any** of the terms by the index's BM25 summed over the terms present (the search endpoint ANDs its words, which would find nothing for most questions), through `SearchIndex.anyCandidates` and the existing `score`. The open note (`activePath`) goes first when it is allowed. Up to 8 notes are sent; each is at most `maxContextChars / 4` (2,000 to 20,000 characters).

**Cutting long notes** (`Excerpts`). A note that fits is sent whole. Otherwise the paragraphs (blank-line separated) with the most occurrences of the terms are kept, in document order, with `[…]` for what was left out; with no terms (summaries) or no matching paragraph, the beginning is kept. The result depends only on the text, the terms and the budget.

**The prompt** (`PromptBuilder`). The system prompt (stable; it changes once a day with the date) sets the rules: answer from the notes, say when they do not contain the answer, cite with wikilinks made from each note's `link` attribute (`[[Projects/Plan]]`, the path without `.md`, which the client resolver resolves from the root), Markdown for a narrow panel, the language of the question. Each user turn carries its notes first, `<notes><note path=… link=… modified=2026-10-14 [truncated]>…</note></notes>`, then the question or the task. Summaries of folders send up to 60 notes, newest first, each at least 1,500 characters and together at most `maxContextChars`; `suggest` adds the titles (only) of up to 20 related notes, found with `rankAny` over the note's most frequent words, and up to 60 of the vault's tags.

**Conversations and the prompt cache.** The device sends earlier turns back as `history`: what was asked, the answer, and the paths the server reported in `start`. `PromptBuilder.rebuild` sends those notes again **as they are now**, with the same rules (the same terms, the same per-note budget, the same count), so an unchanged note gives byte-for-byte the same turn. The last user block carries `cache_control: ephemeral`; the next request's prefix (system, earlier turns) is then read from Anthropic's prompt cache for five minutes, at a tenth of the input price. A new question does not send again a note already in the conversation, and says so to the model when nothing new matched. The newest 12 turns are used; when they outgrow `maxContextChars` (with 10 % slack), the oldest lose their notes first, and every older one with them, so the prefix only changes once.

**The API call** (`AnthropicGateway`, the `api` provider, through `com.anthropic:anthropic-java` 3.0.0). `client.beta().messages().createStreaming(...)`: the model from `settings.ai.model` (default **Claude Sonnet 5.5**; Opus 5.5 and Haiku 5.5 selectable), `max_tokens` 16,000 (`notes.ai.max-output-tokens`), `output_config.effort` `medium` (`notes.ai.effort`), adaptive thinking (the models' default, so nothing is sent), and, for Sonnet and Opus, the server-side refusal fallback (`fallbacks: "default"`, beta `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`; Haiku 5.5 has none). One OkHttp client is kept per key, built with the key alone (no `fromEnv()`: no environment variable can redirect requests or substitute credentials) and `maxRetries(2)` for 429 and 5xx before the stream starts. While streaming, text deltas go to the browser as they arrive; a `fallback` content block means another model answers from the start (`restart` event); `message_delta` carries the stop reason, the refusal category and the cumulative usage, per model when a fallback ran (`usage.iterations`). Thinking blocks are not shown (they arrive empty by default) and never sent back: earlier turns are text only. API errors become `auth`, `rate-limited`, `overloaded`, `bad-request` or `unavailable` with a short text (the API's `error.message`, scrubbed of key-shaped strings and capped at 200 characters).

**Streaming to the browser.** `AiController` checks the request and builds the prompt before answering (so refusals are problem documents), then returns an `SseEmitter` and writes the answer from a virtual thread: `start`, `delta`…, `restart`, `done` or `error` (API.md §24.5). When the browser closes the stream (Stop, a closed tab), the next write fails, the exception leaves the gateway's stream loop, and closing the SDK stream cancels the upstream request, so nothing more is generated or paid for. At most `notes.ai.max-concurrent` (2) answers are written at once; another gets `error: busy`.

**Spend guard** (`UsageLedger`, `AiModels`). Each request's cost is estimated from the usage the API reports at list prices (Sonnet 5.5 $2/$10, Opus 5.5 $4/$20, Haiku 5.5 $0.10/$0.50 per million input/output tokens, cache reads and writes priced separately; a fallback hop at its own model's price, an unknown model at the highest price) and added to the calendar month (server time zone) in `.notesapp/cache/ai-usage.json`: inside the volume, so it survives restarts and is in the backups, but excluded from git (§4.6), so it is never history or mirrored. A request whose stream the browser closed is counted too, estimated at four characters per token. Once the month reaches `settings.ai.monthlyBudgetUsd`, requests are refused with `429 ai-budget-exceeded` until the next month or a higher budget. The cap is a guard, not a hard limit: two requests in flight can each finish past it. Losing the file resets the month to zero.

**Per-request caps.** The question (4,000 characters), the context (`maxContextChars`, 60,000 by default, about 15,000 tokens), the history (50 turns sent, 12 used, answers of at most 100,000 characters), a selection (200,000 characters), the output (`max_tokens` 16,000) and the time (`notes.ai.timeout`, 5 minutes).

**Development** (`notes.ai.fake=true`, `FakeGateway`): answers with canned Markdown that cites the notes the prompt carried, streamed word by word, with token counts estimated from the characters; no key and no network. The status says `fake: true` and the panel says so. Never set in production.

**Configuration** (`AiProperties`, `notes.ai.*`): `claude-token-file` (empty: `<secrets-dir>/claude-token`), `claude-command` (`claude`; the image sets `/opt/claude/bin/claude`), `claude-work-dir` (empty: `<tmp>/notesapp-claude`), `api-key-file` (empty: `<secrets-dir>/anthropic-key`), `fake` (false), `max-output-tokens` (16000), `effort` (`medium`), `timeout` (5m), `max-concurrent` (2).

**Alternatives considered.** Embeddings (a vector index needs an embedding provider, since Anthropic has no embeddings API, and its storage and refresh; BM25 over the existing index answers personal-scale questions well, and the roadmap defers embeddings). Server-side conversations (they would keep questions and answers on the server and need their own retention; the device keeping them matches "conversation history per device"). Tool use to let Claude search the vault itself (more calls and cost per question, and harder to keep excluded folders out). Opus as the default (the roadmap's first choice; Sonnet 5.5 answers note questions well at half the price, and Opus is one setting away).

**Tests.** `ClaudeCodeGatewayTest` with the CLI replaced by a fake process (the locked-down command line and environment, the stream-json lines, an older CLI without partial messages, a refused token, a usage limit, a crash with its stderr scrubbed, cancellation and the timeout); `AiServiceTest` (the providers, availability, retrieval, exclusions, caps, the byte-for-byte rebuild of earlier turns, summaries, suggestions, the stream of events, the spend guard, a browser leaving part way) with a recording gateway; `AnthropicGatewayTest` against a local HTTP server that replays recorded Messages API streams (the request's shape, the key as a bearer token, the fallback, refusals, error mapping without the key, stopping on a listener's exception); `ExcerptsTest`, `QueryTermsTest`, the settings tests, and `AiIntegrationTest` (the HTTP contract with `notes.ai.fake`). No test calls Anthropic.

### 5.34 Flashcard review records (ROADMAP item 34)

Sections 5.34 and 6.34 (and 5.35 and 6.35) are numbered after their ROADMAP items, so branches built in parallel do not collide.

Cards are written in notes and parsed by the client (§6.34); nothing is ever written into a note. What the notes do not hold, each card's answers and the flashcard settings, lives in **`.notesapp/srs.json`**, owned by `flashcards.FlashcardService` (API.md §34). It is a vault file like `settings.json`: committed with the vault's history (batched like autosaves, author from the identity), hidden from the tree, and never served by the file API.

```json
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "config": {
    "newPerDay": 15
  },
  "cards": {
    "c1k1sjm8rzcy": {
      "note": "Cards/Biology.md",
      "front": "The [...] is the powerhouse of the cell.",
      "state": {"due":"2026-10-21T16:58:20.316Z","stability":0.212,"difficulty":6.4133,"scheduledDays":0,"learningSteps":0,"reps":1,"lapses":0,"phase":"learning","lastReview":"2026-10-21T16:57:20.316Z","n":1},
      "reviews": [
        "2026-10-21T16:57:20.316Z again 3082"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

- **The answers are the truth; the state is a cache.** A card's answers are a set keyed by their instant, so merging two devices' answers is a union: it commutes, and a retried request changes nothing. The FSRS state is what the answering client computed; the server keeps it only while it covers every answer (`n` and `lastReview`), and otherwise stores `null` for clients to recompute by replaying the answers (§6.34). Replaying every card on every load would cost about 30 µs per answer on a desktop (a second for 2,000 cards of 20 answers), so the cache matters on the phone.
- **Server-owned operations, not file saves.** Every change (an answer, an undo, a prune, a settings change) is applied under one lock to the file as it is on disk, so two devices reviewing at once never meet a version conflict, and a client sends a few hundred bytes per answer instead of the whole file. The settings change field by field for the same reason. A write that changes nothing (a duplicate) is not written, committed or announced.
- **Layout.** Written by hand for stable bytes: cards sorted by id, one answer per line, a card's state on one line, the settings sorted by key with only the fields that were set. An answer adds one line and changes one, so git diffs stay readable. Fields this build does not know are kept at every level; an entry that cannot be read is skipped with a WARN.
- **Reads.** The parsed file is cached with its blob id and read again when the bytes on disk differ (a hand edit, a history restore). `flashcards.changed` (API.md §34.7) carries the changed records, so an open page on another device updates without a refetch.
- **Source notes.** `GET /api/flashcards/notes` takes the notes from the index: by tag (`TagIndex.notesWithTag`, nested tags included), or, with folder decks, every note with a card marker. Only the client parses them: it alone schedules, and one parser keeps the syntax in one language.
- **Why not in the notes.** The Spaced Repetition plugin writes `<!--SR:!2026-10-25,3,250-->` after each card. Writing into notes from a review on the phone would put an open copy of the note on another device into conflict while the user types there, and those numbers are SM-2's, not FSRS's. Such comments in an imported vault are ignored (and hidden from the cards); their schedule is not imported.

### 5.35 Habits (ROADMAP item 35)

Habits keep no data of their own on the server: a habit is a note, and its check-ins are in its frontmatter (`entries`, the shape of Obsidian's Habit Tracker 21 plugin) or in daily notes' frontmatter (`exercise: true`). `habits.HabitService` only reads the index, for `GET /api/habits` (API.md §35): the habit notes (in `Habits/` at any depth, or tagged `#habit`; not under the templates folder) with their frontmatter as parsed, and, in the same answer, the simple frontmatter values of every note under the daily-notes folder. One request then serves a page whatever the number of daily notes, and the server does not need the daily-note date format (a moment.js format, which only the client parses, §5.11).

- **Why notes, not a store.** Check-ins in plain frontmatter are diffable, show in Obsidian (Habit Tracker 21 reads `entries`; Dataview and Tracker read daily-note properties), and need nothing new for history, trash, sync or conflicts: they are note edits (§6.35).
- **Item 4 later.** ROADMAP item 35 depends on "4 or 13": this builds on daily-note properties (13's territory) and works without tasks. A habit note may later name a recurring task (`task: <id>`) whose completions count as check-ins, read by the same endpoint; nothing here would change.

---

## 6. Frontend design

### 6.1 Stack

These versions were verified on 2026-10-16. The lockfile pins them.

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Build | Vite 8.3.4 (Rolldown/Oxc), `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.2 |
| Language | TypeScript `~6.0.3`. TS 7 is not used yet, because typescript-eslint 8.71.2 requires `<6.1.0`. |
| UI | React 19.3.1 and react-dom 19.3.1; lucide-react 1.58.0 icons; hand-written CSS with variables (no CSS framework) |
| Routing | `react-router` 8.4.0 in **declarative mode** (`<BrowserRouter>`). Never install `react-router-dom`. |
| Server state | `@tanstack/react-query` 5.104.3 |
| UI state | `zustand` 5.0.15 |
| Editor | CodeMirror 6: state 6.7.6, view 6.43.14, commands, language, lang-markdown 6.5.2, language-data, lang-yaml, autocomplete, search, merge 6.12.2; `@lezer/markdown` 1.8.0, `@lezer/highlight`, `@lezer/common` |
| Reading view | markdown-it 15.0.3 (bundled types), `@mdit/plugin-tasklist` 1.1.3, `@mdit/plugin-footnote` 1.1.2, `@mdit/plugin-mark` (same @mdit release line; exact version pinned at scaffold), dompurify 3.4.17. Rich Markdown (ROADMAP item 3, §6.7.1), pinned exactly: `@mdit/plugin-tex` 1.1.2 (math parsing only), katex 0.19.0, mermaid 12.2.0 |
| Utilities | dayjs 1.11.23 (+ plugins), fuzzysort 4.0.2 (always `threshold: 0`), `yaml` 2.9.1 for client-side frontmatter merge |
| Flashcards (ROADMAP item 34) | `ts-fsrs` 5.4.2 (MIT, no dependencies), pinned exactly, in the flashcards page's lazy chunk (§6.34) |
| Drawings (ROADMAP item 5) | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` 0.18.1 (MIT; peer React 17–19; a lazy chunk with self-hosted fonts, §6.8.1), `lz-string` 1.5.0 (MIT) for Obsidian Excalidraw's compressed-json blocks. Both pinned exactly. |
| Tasks (ROADMAP item 4, §6.22) | `chrono-node` 2.10.2 (MIT, no dependencies) for dates in quick add; `rrule` 2.8.1 (BSD-3-Clause, with `tslib` 2.8.1, 0BSD) for `every …` and readable rules. Both are pinned exactly and load only in lazy chunks. |
| Spreadsheets | `hyperformula` 3.4.0, pinned exactly; GPL-3.0-only with `licenseKey: 'gpl-v3'`; a lazy chunk (§6.21). `@glideapps/glide-data-grid` 6.0.4-alpha24, pinned exactly (MIT), the grid of the `sheet` view's lazy chunk (§6.21.5) |
| Tests | Vitest 5.0.5, jsdom 30.1.3, Testing Library (react 16.3.3, dom 10.4.2, user-event 14.6.8, jest-dom 7.0.1), `fake-indexeddb` (dev only; version pinned at scaffold) for the drafts store |
| Lint | ESLint 10.13 flat config generated by `npm create vite@latest frontend -- --template react-ts --eslint` (typescript-eslint 8.71.2, react-hooks 7.1.1, react-refresh 0.5.7) |

The `package.json` dependency set is exactly the research list plus `"yaml": "^2.9.1"`, `@mdit/plugin-mark` and (dev) `fake-indexeddb`. Drawings (ROADMAP item 5) added `@excalidraw/excalidraw` and `lz-string`, and spreadsheets (item 6) `hyperformula` and `@glideapps/glide-data-grid` (§6.21), pinned exactly. The graph view (item 16, §6.28) added `d3-force` 3.0.0 (ISC; with `d3-dispatch`, `d3-quadtree` and `d3-timer` 3.0.1, ISC) and (dev) `@types/d3-force` 3.0.10 (MIT). `package.json` declares `"license": "GPL-3.0-only"`. No virtualization, DOM-morphing or IndexedDB wrapper library is added; the small amounts of code they would replace are written by hand (§6.7, §6.9, §6.15). The scripts are `dev`, `build` (`tsc -b && vite build`), `lint` (`eslint .`), `test` (`vitest run`) and `preview`.

**Lockfile.** `package-lock.json` is generated from a clean tree (delete `node_modules` and any lockfile, then `npm install`). Vite 8's Rolldown and Oxc are native binaries installed through optional per-platform packages, and npm can otherwise keep only the platform packages of the machine that wrote the lockfile (npm/cli#4828). CI asserts that the Linux binding is present (§7.5).

`vite.config.ts`:

- Proxy `/api` to `http://127.0.0.1:8080`. Use the explicit IPv4 address, because Node on Windows may resolve `localhost` to `::1`, while the backend binds 127.0.0.1. Set `changeOrigin: false` so the backend's Host and Origin checks see the host the browser used.
- `build.sourcemap: true`.
- The build reads `VITE_BUILD_SHA` (set by the Dockerfile; `dev` otherwise) into `import.meta.env`, for the version check of §6.18.
- Vitest config through `/// <reference types="vitest/config" />` with the jsdom environment.

`index.html`:

- `<html lang="en" data-theme="dark">`: the default theme is set statically, so the first paint is already themed.
- Viewport meta: `<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover">`. Do not add `interactive-widget`.
- `<meta name="theme-color" content="#1e1e1e">` and `<meta name="color-scheme" content="dark light">`.
- The installable app (§6.26): `<link rel="manifest" href="/manifest.webmanifest">`, `apple-mobile-web-app-capable`, `apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style: black-translucent`, `apple-mobile-web-app-title`, `mobile-web-app-capable`, and `apple-touch-icon.png`.
- `<script src="/theme-boot.js"></script>` in `<head>`: a tiny **classic, non-deferred** script from `public/` (allowed by `script-src 'self'`). It runs before first paint, applies the stored or system theme, and updates `theme-color` and `color-scheme` (§6.17). A module script would be deferred, so the browser could paint before `data-theme` is set.

### 6.2 Folder structure

```

├── main.tsx                  createRoot; QueryClientProvider; BrowserRouter; theme bootstrap; installViewportVars
├── App.tsx                   <Routes> + <AppShell>
├── api/
│   ├── types.ts              VERBATIM copy of API.md §17 (single source of truth for DTOs)
│   ├── client.ts             apiFetch(): adds X-Client-Id + Accept (+ Idempotency-Key when given); JSON; reads X-Notes-Build;
│   │                         throws ApiError(problem) | TransientError (network, 502/504, non-problem 503, busy, non-JSON body)
│   ├── endpoints.ts          one typed function per endpoint (getTree, readFile, saveFile, ... )

│   ├── recent.ts             recently opened files, per device (localStorage), with times and a subscription (§6.23)
│   ├── bookmarks.ts          bookmark item helpers (Obsidian format) and BookmarkWriter (§6.23)
│   ├── bookmarkStore.ts      the page's bookmarks: query + unsaved edits, useBookmarks(), toggle actions
│   ├── mirror.ts, largeFiles.ts  the mirror's warning, summary and "push now"; the large-file threshold (§6.11, §6.18)
│   ├── ai/                   Ask your vault (§6.31): register.ts, actions.ts, run.ts, aiClient.ts, sse.ts, conversations.ts
│   │                         (eager, small); AiChat.tsx, AiAnswer.tsx, AiPanel.tsx, AiPage.tsx, ai.css (lazy)
│   ├── voice/                voice notes (§6.30): recorder.ts (MediaRecorder, formats, level, wake lock), voiceNotes.ts
│   │                         (the panel's state, saving, transcripts)
│   └── paths.ts              join, folderOf, basename, extension, encodeRoutePath, decodeRoutePath
├── tasks/                    structured tasks (ROADMAP item 4, §6.22): register.ts (the extension points), api.ts, store.ts,
│                             queries.ts, mutations.ts, sync.ts, model.ts, recurrence.ts (lazy), convert.ts, quickadd/
│                             (dialog + lazy parser), board/ (the generic board + the task columns), ui/ (pages, file
│                             view, embed, panel, detail sheet, overlay host), tasks.css
├── components/
│   ├── layout/               AppShell (hosts the app-level file input), TopBar, Ribbon, LeftSidebar, RightSidebar, StatusBar,
│   │                         MobileToolbar, Drawer, BottomSheet, Resizer, WorkspaceScroll
│   ├── VirtualList.tsx       fixed-row-height windowing for the tree, tags and long panel lists
│   ├── explorer/             FileExplorer, TreeRow (long-press menu), RowMenu, MoveDialog (dry-run preview)
│   ├── panels/               SearchPanel, TagsPanel, BookmarksPanel, RecentPanel, BacklinksPanel, OutlinePanel, HistoryPanel (lazy)
│   ├── overlays/             QuickSwitcher, CommandPalette, PromptDialog, ConfirmDialog, TemplatePicker, Toasts
│   ├── capture/              CaptureForm, CaptureDialog, CapturePage (/capture), CaptureSettings (§6.27)
│   ├── ConflictBanner.tsx, ConnectionIndicator.tsx, SaveStatus.tsx, ErrorBoundary.tsx, OfflineBanners.tsx
│   └── pages/                HomePage, SearchPage (mobile), TrashPage, SettingsPage (lazy), NotFoundPage
├── styles/                   tokens.css (themes), app.css, editor.css, reading.css, mobile.css; rich.css (loaded with the reading-view chunk)
└── test/setup.ts
```

### 6.3 State management and cache invalidation

**TanStack Query** holds all server state.

| Query key | Endpoint | staleTime | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `['tree']` | `GET /api/tree` | Infinity | Patched by SSE. Its `select` builds `byPath`, `byLowerPath`, the notes list and the alias items. This is the quick-switcher and autocomplete list. |
| `['file', path]` | `GET /api/files` | Infinity | Initial content only; live content lives in `DocumentSession`. **Removed** (`removeQueries`) on `file.changed`, `file.deleted` and `entry.moved` for that path, and replaced with `setQueryData` (saved content and version) on an own `WriteResult`, so reopening a closed note never shows a stale copy. |
| `['meta', path]` | `GET /api/notes/meta` | 30 s | `[[Note#` heading completion for **other** notes. Headings of the open note (Outline panel, `[[#`) come from its live Lezer tree (§6.6). |
| `['backlinks', path, unlinked]` | `GET /api/notes/backlinks` | 0 | Refetched when the panel is visible |
| `['search', q, sort, fuzzy]` | `GET /api/search` | 10 s | Input debounced 200 ms; a `400` is not retried |
| `['savedSearches']` | `GET /api/searches` | Infinity | Invalidated by `searches.changed`; replaced from each `PUT` response |
| `['tags']` | `GET /api/tags` | 30 s | |
| `['history', path]` | `GET /api/history` | 0 | |
| `['historyContent', commit, path]` | `GET /api/history/content` | Infinity | Immutable |
| `['vaultHistory', 'log' \| 'changes' \| 'deleted', filter]` | `GET /api/history/log`, `/changes`, `/deleted` | 0 | The vault history page (§6.11.1); all invalidated by `history.committed` |
| `['trash']`, `['settings']`, `['me']`, `['status']`, `['templates']` | | 60 s / Infinity / Infinity / 10 s / 60 s | |
| `['tasks', 'view', params]`, `['tasks', 'lists']`, `['tasks', 'task', id]` | `GET /api/tasks…` (API.md §20) | Infinity | Load into the task store (§6.22); the data is only the response's metadata. Invalidated by the resync like every query, and by the task feature's own refetch rules. |
| `['bookmarks']` | `GET /api/bookmarks` | Infinity | Refreshed on `bookmarks.changed`; this tab's unsaved edits are laid over it (§6.23). Read through the page's own client, so components outside a provider can show stars. |
| `['compat']` | `GET /api/compat` | 0 | The compatibility page only (ROADMAP item 24, §6.29) |
| `['graph']` | `GET /api/graph` | Infinity | Graph views only (ROADMAP item 16, §6.28): refreshed with `since` at most every 800 ms after `['tree']` or `['settings']` change while one is mounted |

There is no `['resolve']` query: link resolution runs on the client over the tree (§6.6).

**Zustand** (`state/ui.ts`) holds UI-only state:

- sidebar open flags and widths (persisted to localStorage);
- the active left tab (`files | search | tags`) and right tab (`backlinks | outline | history`);
- the desktop search panel's query (desktop search never changes the route, §6.4);
- overlay flags (switcher, palette, prompt, confirm, template picker);
- the mobile drawer and sheet state;
- the SSE connection status and the `caughtUp` flag;
- the global save summary.

Per-device preferences (theme, spellcheck, autocorrect, remote images) live in `state/prefs.ts` (§5.12).

**`DocumentSession`** (one per open text file, outside React, in a ref-counted registry) holds the editing state. Its state machine is in §6.10. Views use `useTextDocument(path)`.

- **Source of truth.** The session keeps CodeMirror's `Text` (inside the cached `EditorState`) as the live content. Strings are produced only for saves and drafts (throttled), never per keystroke. React subscribes to a change counter and the status, not to the text, so typing in a large note re-renders nothing; the status bar's word and character counts are computed debounced (300 ms).
- **Base text.** The session also keeps `baseText`, the content at `baseVersion`, for 3-way merges (§6.8).
- **Reopening a note.** `EditorState`s are cached by path for the page's lifetime, so undo survives switching notes. When a session is opened, it compares the cached version with the tree entry's version, which SSE keeps current. If they differ, it fetches the file and applies it to the cached state with `applyRemoteText` (undo kept); if the cached state is dirty, it enters conflict instead. A note closed on this device and changed on another is therefore never shown stale, and the first save never hits a surprise 409.

**SSE event to cache action** (`sync/applyEvents.ts`):

| Event | Cache action |
|---|---|
| `hello {resync:true}`, `resync` | **Resync with buffering** (API.md §16.5): set `buffering`; refetch the tree; invalidate every other query; buffer events that arrive meanwhile; when the tree arrives, drop buffered events of the same boot whose `seq` is at most the tree's `eventId` seq, apply the rest in order, and clear `buffering`. Then every open `DocumentSession` runs `revalidate()`: it compares versions and acts like `file.changed`. |
| `file.created` | Insert the entry into `['tree']`. Invalidate `['tags']`, `['search']` (lazy), and `['templates']` if it is under the templates folder. |
| `file.changed` | Replace the entry in `['tree']` (aliases may have changed). Call `session.onRemoteChanged(entry)` if the file has a session, and in every case refresh `['file', path]` (removed, or refetched while an embed observes it): sessions live for the page's lifetime, and `![[Note]]` embeds read the query. Invalidate `['meta', path]`, `['backlinks']`, `['tags']`, `['search']` (lazy; only active queries refetch). |
| `file.deleted` | Remove it from `['tree']` and `removeQueries(['file', path])`. Call `session.onRemoteDeleted(trashId)`. Invalidate `['backlinks']`, `['tags']`, `['search']`. |
| `folder.created` / `folder.deleted` | Patch the `['tree']` folders. `folder.deleted` also removes descendant files, removes their `['file']` queries and notifies their sessions. |
| `entry.moved` | Re-key the entries in `['tree']` (for a folder, every descendant). Record `from → to` in the client move map (`sync/moves.ts`). Call `session.onRemoteMoved(to)` for affected open docs and re-key their drafts. `removeQueries(['file', from])`. If the active route is affected, `navigate(newRoute, {replace:true})`. Invalidate `['backlinks']`. |
| `trash.changed` | Invalidate `['trash']`. |
| `history.committed` | Invalidate `['history', p]` for each `p` in `paths`, `['vaultHistory']`, and `['status']`. |
| `settings.changed` | Invalidate `['settings']`. |
| `bookmarks.changed` | Invalidate `['bookmarks']`. Sent with `origin: null` after a move or restore, so the mover refetches too. |
| `searches.changed` | Invalidate `['savedSearches']`. |
| `status.changed` | Invalidate `['status']`. |
| `ping` | Nothing; it only feeds the watchdog and the Live indicator (§6.18). |
| Names registered with `registerEventHandler` (§6.8) | The registered handler. |

**Own mutations.** The server does not echo a client's own content events back to it (API.md §16.4). The originating client therefore patches its caches from its HTTP responses, and **runs the same invalidations as the matching remote event**, so panels and completions reflect the user's own edits: a new heading or tag shows up in `[[Note#` completion, the tags pane and search right after the save.

| Response | Cache action |
|---|---|
| `WriteResult` | Upsert `file` and `createdFolders`; `setQueryData(['file', path])` with the saved content and version. Invalidate `['meta', path]`, `['tags']`, `['backlinks']`, `['search']`. |
| `AttachmentResult` | Upsert `file` and `createdFolders`. |
| `TrashEntry` | Remove the entry (and, for a folder, its descendants) and their `['file']` queries. Invalidate `['backlinks']`, `['tags']`, `['search']`, `['trash']`. |
| `FolderResult` | Add the folders. |
| `MoveResult` | Re-key the `moved` entries and add `createdFolders`. Record the moves in the client move map and re-key drafts. For each `linkUpdates` entry, set the tree version and call `session.onRemoteChanged` (the session is clean because of the pre-move flush, so it re-fetches and applies silently) or `removeQueries(['file', path])`. Invalidate `['backlinks']`, `['tags']`, `['search']`. |
| `RestoreResult` | Add the files and folders. Invalidate `['backlinks']`, `['tags']`, `['search']`, `['trash']`. |
| `HistoryRestoreResult` | Upsert the file; the open session adopts the restored content. Invalidate `['meta', path]`, `['tags']`, `['backlinks']`, `['search']`. |

**Startup order.** This closes the gap between the snapshot and the stream.

1. `GET /api/tree` returns `eventId` (read by the server before its snapshot, §5.10).
2. Open `EventSource('/api/events?clientId=…&lastEventId=' + eventId)`.
3. The server replays anything that happened in between. `hello.replayed` tells the client how many replayed events follow; `caughtUp` becomes true after that many (at once when it is 0). Queued offline saves are retried only after `caughtUp`, so a replayed `entry.moved` or `file.changed` is applied first.
4. Offline (§6.26): when the tree has not answered after 2.5 s, the device's snapshot stands in and the stream starts without an id (so `hello` resyncs). After `caughtUp`: offline-opened sessions revalidate, offline copies follow the tree, closed tabs' drafts are replayed, and queued captures are sent (§6.27).

### 6.4 Routing and URL scheme

| Route | View |
|---|---|
| `/` | `HomePage`. If `dailyNotes.openOnStartup`, open today's note. Otherwise open the last-opened path (`localStorage notesapp:lastPath`) if it still exists, then `Welcome.md`, then an empty state with "New note" and "Today". |
| `/n/<path>` | `FileView`. The registry picks the view. `#<heading text>` scrolls to the heading. `?line=N` scrolls to 0-based line `N` (a search hit), flashes it, and highlights the terms of `&q=` (words, phrases and regular expressions). For a PDF, `?page=N` opens the viewer at that page. `?mode=read` forces the reading view. |
| `/search?q=…` | Search page, **phones only**. On desktop the search panel keeps its query in zustand and never changes the route, so typing in it never navigates away from the open note. |
| `/trash` | Trash page |
| `/settings`, `/settings/<section>` | Settings page (lazy) |
| `/x/<route>` | A page registered with `registerPage` (§6.8). ROADMAP item 4 adds `/x/tasks` (all lists), `/x/tasks/today`, `/x/tasks/upcoming`, `/x/tasks/completed`, `/x/tasks/filter?tag=&priority=&list=&status=` and `/x/tasks/list/<list id>` (§6.22). A `.tasks` file at `/n/<path>` shows its list (`?view=source`: its JSON; `#^<task id>`: that task). |
| anything else | `NotFoundPage` |

**Path encoding.**

- Encode with `encodeRoutePath(p) = p.split('/').map(encodeURIComponent).join('/')`, so `Daily Notes/2026-10-16.md` becomes `/n/Daily%20Notes/2026-10-16.md`.
- Decode **from `location.pathname`** segment by segment with `decodeURIComponent`. Do not use `useParams()['*']`, so the client controls the decoding exactly.
- These are deep links: they work from bookmarks, shared tailnet URLs and reloads. The server's `SpaController` serves `index.html` for `/n/**`, `/search`, `/trash`, `/settings/**` and `/x/**`.
- Browser back and forward are the app's navigation history, as in Obsidian.
- A remote move of the open note uses `replace`, so the stale URL does not stay in history.
- **Overlays on touch devices** (drawer, bottom sheet, switcher, palette, dialogs) push a history entry when they open, so Safari's edge swipe or the back button closes them instead of leaving the note.
- Pages registered by extensions (`registerPage`, §6.8) live under the reserved prefix `/x/`, for example `/x/tasks/today`.

**Heading targets** (`[[Note#Heading]]`, `#H1#H2`, route `#…`): take the last subpath segment (`H1#H2` gives `H2`), compare it case-insensitively with whitespace collapsed against the note's headings in document order, and use the first match. The editor uses the Lezer tree's headings; the reading view stamps each heading with `data-heading` for the same comparison. `^blockid` subpaths open the note at the top in v1.

#### 6.4.1 Search panel (ROADMAP item 22)

`SearchView` serves the desktop panel and the phone page.

- **Results.** Notes show a note icon; PDF hits a PDF icon and badge, and each match its page (`p. 2`). A note match opens `/n/<path>?line=N&q=…`; a PDF match opens `/n/<path>?page=N`, which `PdfView` turns into the viewer's `#page=N` fragment (the iframe is keyed by its URL, because a fragment change alone does not move the viewer).
- **Highlighting.** `features/searchQuery.ts` parses the API.md §12.1 grammar exactly as the server does; `highlightTerms` takes the positive words and phrases and the positive regular expressions (converted to JavaScript: case-insensitive unless `(?-i)`, skipped when JavaScript cannot compile them). The editor and the reading view highlight both.
- **Typo tolerance.** When the response has `corrections`, a line reads "Including similar words: ~~recieve~~ → receive. Exact words only"; the link repeats the query with `fuzzy=false` (the query key is `['search', q, sort, fuzzy]`). The replacements are appended to the `q=` of the hit route, so the opened note highlights them too.
- **Errors.** A `400` (an invalid or runaway regular expression) is shown in place of the results with its `detail`, and is not retried.
- **Saved searches** (`['savedSearches']`, staleTime Infinity, invalidated by `searches.changed`). With the field empty, the panel lists them above the syntax help; a click fills the field and the sort. A toolbar button saves the current query and sort under a name (prompted; the query is the default) or, when that query and sort are already saved, removes it after confirmation. Rename and delete sit on each row. Every change goes through `updateSavedSearches(change)`: it applies the change to the latest list and `PUT`s it with the cached version, and on `409` fetches the list and re-applies the change (three attempts), so two devices editing at once both keep their change.

### 6.5 Component tree

```
<App>
 └ <AppShell>                                    (CSS grid; phone/tablet/desktop variants §6.16; hosts the hidden app-level file input)
    ├ <Ribbon/>               desktop: full ribbon; tablet: collapsed icon rail. Switcher, Search, Today, Template, New note,
    │                         Command palette, Trash, Settings
    ├ <LeftSidebar>           tabs: <FileExplorer/> | <SearchPanel/> | <BookmarksPanel/> | <TagsPanel/> | <RecentPanel/> + registered left panels
    │                         (phone: inside <Drawer>, whose footer holds Settings, Trash and the theme toggle)
    ├ <Workspace>
    │   ├ <TopBar/>           phone/tablet: ☰, title (+ connection dot), switcher/search button, mode toggle, ⋯ menu
    │   └ <WorkspaceScroll>   THE scroll container: the title and properties scroll away with the text, as in Obsidian
    │       ├ <ConflictBanner/>  (sticky; only in conflict/deleted states)
    │       └ <Routes> → <FileView path> | registered pages
    │             └ registry view, e.g. <MarkdownView>
    │                  ├ <NoteHeader>  <InlineTitle/> (edit = rename) · <SaveStatus/> · mode toggle · ⋯
    │                  │               (phone: only <InlineTitle/>; the TopBar already has the rest)
    │                  ├ <PropertiesTable/> (reading mode, if frontmatter)
    │                  └ <NoteEditor/> (CodeMirror sized to its content) | <ReadingView/>
    ├ <RightSidebar>          tabs: <BacklinksPanel/> | <OutlinePanel/> | <HistoryPanel/> (lazy) + registered right panels
    │                         (Ask, §6.21)
    │                         (phone: <BottomSheet>)
    ├ <StatusBar/>            desktop: <ConnectionIndicator/> · save summary · history warning · words/chars · backlink count
    ├ <MobileToolbar/>        touch devices; always mounted, shown while the editor is focused or the toolbar is in use
    └ overlays: <QuickSwitcher/> <CommandPalette/> <TemplatePicker/> <PromptDialog/> <ConfirmDialog/> <MoveDialog/> <Toasts/>
```

### 6.6 Editor: CodeMirror 6 extension list

`noteExtensions()` in `editor/editorSetup.ts` follows the verified research sketch. No wrapper library is used: React owns the host `<div>`, and CodeMirror owns everything inside it.

| # | Extension | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `history()` | Undo history, per note. `EditorState`s are cached by path for the page's lifetime, so undo survives switching notes; on reopen the session revalidates the cached state against the tree's version (§6.3). |
| 2 | `drawSelection()`, **desktop only** | iOS uses the native caret and handles. |
| 3 | `highlightActiveLine()`, `indentOnInput()`, `bracketMatching()`, `closeBrackets()`, `highlightSelectionMatches()` | Editing basics |
| 4 | `search({top: true})` | In-note find and replace panel (`Mod-F`) |
| 5 | `EditorView.lineWrapping`, `placeholder('Start writing…')` | |
| 6 | `yamlFrontmatter({content: markdown({base: markdownLanguage, codeLanguages: languages, extensions: [ObsidianSyntax, MathSyntax], completeHTMLTags: false})})` | GFM, frontmatter, lazily loaded fence grammars, and the WikiLink, Embed, Hashtag, Highlight (`==x==`) and Comment (`%%x%%`) nodes. The tag and wikilink rules use the shared patterns of `features/syntax.ts` (API.md §15.6), including `\|` as the alias separator. `MathSyntax` (`editor/mathSyntax.ts`, ROADMAP item 3) adds InlineMath (`$x$`, and `$$x$$` inside a paragraph) and BlockMath (`$$` blocks) with the reading view's delimiter rules, so `$5 and $10` stays text and nothing inside math parses as a link, tag or emphasis. |
| 7 | `syntaxHighlighting(obsidianHighlight)` + `lineClassPlugin` | Styled source mode: heading sizes, faint `#`/`**`/backtick marks, code-block and quote backgrounds, a highlight background for `==x==`, faint `%%comments%%`, math in a monospace accent colour with faint `$` marks, and a bold callout title line (`> [!type]`). Code tokens use `features/codeTokens.ts`, which the reading view's highlighter shares (§6.7.1), so fenced code looks the same in both modes. Nothing is hidden. |
| 8 | `autocompletion({override: [wikilinkSource, headingSource, tagSource], icons: false})` | **`[[`** gives notes and aliases, pre-ranked with fuzzysort, with `filter: false` and **no `validFor`**: CodeMirror honours `validFor` only when it filters itself, so with both set the list froze after the first keystroke. The source re-runs on every keystroke and returns at most 50 options. The inserted text is `linkText(P, source, settings.newLinkFormat, style)` (§5.5.7), so two notes with the same name get a path-qualified link. With `useWikilinks: false`, the typed `[[query` is replaced by a Markdown link `[display](encodedPath)`. **`[[#`** and **`[[ThisNote#`** give headings from the open note's live Lezer tree; **`[[Other#`** uses `['meta', path]`. **`#`** gives tags. Completions use a custom `apply` that swallows the auto-inserted `]]`. |
| 9 | `clickNavigation(nav)` + `unresolvedLinks` | `Mod`+click follows a link or tag (Shift adds nothing in v1, because there are no panes). A `ViewPlugin` marks wikilinks and internal Markdown links that the client resolver cannot resolve with `cm-unresolved` (faded), as Obsidian does. |
| 10 | `pasteAndDrop(uploader, notePath)` | Image or PDF paste and drop, inserted at the **current selection** (which CodeMirror keeps after blur). While a file uploads, an "Uploading name…" **widget** marks its place: a position in the editor state, never document text, so autosave, drafts and other devices never see a placeholder and a tab killed mid-upload leaves nothing in the note. `AttachmentResult.markdown` (already in the configured link style) is inserted at the widget's mapped position, in the files' order, or the widget is removed on failure. Uploads in flight count as unsaved work (the `beforeunload` prompt, the new-build reload). Uploads send an `Idempotency-Key`. Internal explorer drag (`application/x-notesapp-path`) inserts a link built with `linkText`. |
| 11 | `EditorView.contentAttributes` | `spellcheck` and `autocorrect` from the per-device preferences (§5.12), `autocapitalize=sentences`, `enterkeyhint=enter` |
| 12 | `smartPunctuationGuard` | An input transaction filter for iOS Smart Punctuation, which WebKit reportedly ties to `spellcheck` (to be confirmed on the device, smoke item 22). Inside frontmatter, fenced or inline code, and for `--`/`---` at the start of a line, it reverts the substitutions: `—` back to `--`, curly double quotes to `"`, curly single quotes to `'`. Otherwise iOS would silently break frontmatter delimiters, YAML strings and `"[[Link]]"` property values. |
| 13 | `EditorView.scrollMargins` + `cursorScrollMargin` | Keeps the caret clear of the sticky banner and the toolbar |
| 14 | `EditorState.readOnly` / `EditorView.editable` | For `lossy` files |
| 15 | Keymap, highest first | `Prec.highest`: `Mod-Alt-ArrowLeft` / `Mod-Alt-ArrowRight` (navigate back and forward; CodeMirror's `defaultKeymap` binds `Alt-ArrowLeft/Right` to syntax-wise cursor moves outside macOS). Then the app keymap (`Mod-s` flush, `Mod-Enter` follow, `Mod-l` toggle checkbox, `Mod-b`, `Mod-i`, `Mod-k`), then `closeBracketsKeymap`, `completionKeymap`, `searchKeymap`, `historyKeymap`, `defaultKeymap`, `indentWithTab` (`Esc` then `Tab` leaves the editor, §6.14). The app's command keys are also the `editorCommandKeys` facet, which vim mode runs before its own keys |
| 16 | `EditorView.updateListener` | Calls `session.markDirty()` on `docChanged` when the transaction is not annotated `Transaction.remote` |
| 17 | `vimCompartment` (`editor/vimMode.ts`) | Empty, or vim mode when this device turned it on (§6.36): a lazy chunk at `Prec.highest` |

The document is always **LF-only**; there is no `lineSeparator` facet. CRLF files are converted at the session boundary (§6.9), because CodeMirror counts a line break as one position whatever the separator, and `Text.toString()` always joins with `\n`.

**Opening links.** `nav.openLink(target)` handles clicks, `Mod-Enter`, reading-view clicks and the mobile "Follow" button.

1. `resolve(target, source)` from `features/links.ts`, over the tree's `byLowerPath` and `byLowerName` indexes. It implements §5.5.6 and passes the API.md §15.1 vectors, so there is no network round trip per click (on the phone that could go through a DERP relay).
2. If it resolves, `navigate('/n/' + encodeRoutePath(path) + (subpath ? '#' + encodeURIComponent(subpath) : ''))`.
3. If it is unresolved and `createPath(target, source, settings)` is not null, call `POST /api/files {path: createPath, content: "", onConflict: "fail"}` with an `Idempotency-Key`, treat `409 already-exists` as success, then navigate.
4. If it is unresolved without a `createPath`, show the toast "Can't create a note for this link".

`POST /api/links/resolve` stays in the API. Development builds cross-check a sample of client resolutions against it and log any mismatch to the console (a parity check); production builds never call it.

`openTag(tag)` opens search with `tag:#tag`.

**Remote updates.** `applyRemoteText(view, text)` takes the remote content **after** LF normalization and turns `@codemirror/merge` `diff()` output into a minimal change set annotated `Transaction.remote` and `addToHistory: false`, which preserves the cursor, scroll position and undo history. If `view.composing` is true (iOS autocorrect or IME), it retries on `compositionend`, or at the latest after 300 ms.

**Server offsets in CRLF files.** Offsets from the server (backlink mentions, `NoteMeta.links`, search lines) count `\r\n` as two code units. For a session with `eol: 'crlf'`, `features/eol.ts` maps a server offset to a document offset by subtracting the number of `\r\n` pairs before it. A lone `\r` is one position on both sides and needs no mapping.

### 6.7 Reading view

The pipeline is `render/render.ts` from the research, verified:

1. Strip frontmatter. `lineOffset` is the number of frontmatter lines; it is passed in `env` so `data-line` values match the source. The input is the session's LF text.
2. `md = MarkdownIt({html: true, linkify: true, breaks: !settings.editor.strictLineBreaks})`. Obsidian shows a single newline as a line break unless "Strict line breaks" is turned on, so notes written in Obsidian keep their line structure. Plugins: `.use(obsidianPlugin)` (wikilinks with `\|`, embeds, hashtags with the shared character class, never inside a link's label), `.use(obsidianComments)` (drops `%%…%%`, inline and in its multi-line block form), `.use(richMarkdown)` (callouts, math, Mermaid and highlighted-code placeholders, §6.7.1), `.use(mark)` (`==x==` becomes `<mark>`), `.use(tasklist, {disabled: false, label: false})`, `.use(footnote)` and `.use(sourceLinePlugin)`.
3. **Sanitize** with `DOMPurify.sanitize(html, {FORBID_TAGS: ['style', 'form', 'button', 'textarea', 'select'], ADD_ATTR: ['target']})`, plus hooks: remove any `input` that is not a task-list checkbox, strip `position: fixed|sticky` and `z-index` from `style` attributes, and add `rel="noopener noreferrer"` to external links. The view's container uses `contain: paint; position: relative`, so a note imported or clipped from elsewhere cannot draw a full-screen overlay imitating the app (§5.14).
4. **Link resolution** is synchronous: `env.resolve(t)` calls the client resolver over the tree (§6.6), so unresolved links get `is-unresolved` (faded, dashed underline) on the first render, with no flicker.
5. **Embeds** (`render/embeds.tsx`, hydrated after the HTML is inserted):

   | Embed target | Rendering |
   |---|---|
   | image | `<img src="/api/files/raw?path=…&v=<size>-<mtimeMillis>" loading="lazy">`, built from the tree entry, so the URL is content-addressed and the server marks it immutable (§5.14). `\|W` or `\|WxH` sets the size; otherwise the box reserves space (a minimum height, then the image's `aspect-ratio` once known) so later images never make the page jump. Remote `https:` images become click-to-load placeholders when the per-device `loadRemoteImages` preference is off. |
   | audio or video | `<audio controls>` / `<video controls playsinline>`; a video file without a picture (a voice note recorded as WebM, §6.30) becomes an `<audio>` once its metadata says so |
   | pdf | The PDF view's `loadEmbed` (PDF.js, §6.8.3): the pages in a box of the note at page width, scrolled to `#page=N`; the height from `#height=` (or a `WxH` size), otherwise 600 px; a header names the file and opens it at the page shown. v1 used an `<iframe>`, which iOS shows as the first page only. |
   | note (`![[Note]]`, `![[Note#Heading]]`) | Transclusion box showing the rendered note or just that heading's section, **one level deep**. Nested embeds render as links. A cycle guard prevents loops. Header links to the note. |
   | `![[Note#^block]]` | A link (block transclusion is roadmap item 20) |
   | other files | A file chip with a download link |
   | registered view types (`.sheet` spreadsheets, §6.21.7; `.excalidraw` and `.excalidraw.md` drawings, §6.8.1) | The descriptor's `loadEmbed()` (for a spreadsheet, a read-only table of its cached values: `![[Budget.sheet]]`, `#Summary`, `#Budget!A1:C10`, §6.21.7), with `\|W` or `\|WxH` as `width` and `height`. A note that another view shows (a drawing) is embedded through that view at any depth: it transcludes nothing further. |
6. **Interaction** (delegated on the container):
   - A click on `a.internal-link` calls `preventDefault`, then `nav.openLink(data-href)`.
   - A click on `a.tag` opens tag search.
   - External links open in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, added by a DOMPurify hook.
   - **Checkboxes.** A `change` on `input.task-list-item-checkbox` calls `toggleTaskAtLine(text, data-line)` and then `session.applyEdit()`; that is a normal edit, so it autosaves. The task pattern is `^(\s*(?:>\s?)*\s*(?:[-+*]|\d+[.)])\s+\[)`, so tasks inside blockquotes and callouts (`> - [ ]`) work too. Each checkbox carries `data-hash`, a hash of its source line as rendered; a toggle is applied only if the session's line still has that hash, otherwise the view re-renders and the tap is ignored (the line moved during the 150 ms render lag). Checkboxes are disabled unless the session is `clean` or `dirty`. Each gets an `aria-label` from its list item's text.
7. **Properties.** If frontmatter exists, a collapsible read-only "Properties" table is shown above the content. Editing properties uses source mode in v1.
8. **Updating without replacing.** The view re-renders on mode switch and on content changes, debounced by 150 ms, but it never replaces its whole DOM, which would reload PDFs, stop playing media and collapse images while they decode.
   - A change that only flips `[ ]` and `[x]` updates the checkbox's `checked` state and skips rendering.
   - Otherwise `render/patch.ts` renders into a detached fragment and patches the container **per top-level block**, keyed by the block's HTML with `data-line` attributes and task-checkbox state (`checked`, `data-hash`) excluded; the key is stored when the block is inserted, so later changes to the live DOM (hydrated math and diagrams, an opened callout) never change it. Unchanged blocks keep their DOM nodes, including embedded images, iframes, media, transclusions, rendered math and diagrams, and only get their `data-line` and checkbox states updated, so ticking a task inside an opened callout neither closes it nor re-renders anything. No DOM-morphing library is needed.
   - After every patch, `hydrateEmbeds` and `hydrateRich` (§6.7.1) fill the placeholders of the blocks that were inserted; kept blocks are never hydrated again.
   - The scroll position maps between modes through `data-line` and the CodeMirror line block.

#### 6.7.1 Rich Markdown: callouts, code, math, Mermaid (ROADMAP item 3)

Built in `render/rich/`, styled by `styles/rich.css`. Both load with the reading-view chunk; the heavy parts are their own chunks, loaded on first use.

| Feature | Parsing (markdown-it, synchronous) | Rendering |
|---|---|---|
| **Callouts** `> [!type]` | A core rule after block parsing rewrites a blockquote whose first line is `[!type]`, `[!type]+`/`[!type]-` (foldable, open or closed), `[!type\|metadata]`, with an optional Markdown title. Every Obsidian type and alias (`note`, `abstract`/`summary`/`tldr`, `info`, `todo`, `tip`/`hint`/`important`, `success`/`check`/`done`, `question`/`help`/`faq`, `warning`/`caution`/`attention`, `failure`/`fail`/`missing`, `danger`/`error`, `bug`, `example`, `quote`/`cite`); unknown types get the note style and keep their `data-callout`, as in Obsidian. Nested callouts are nested blockquotes. | Direct HTML with Obsidian's class names (`.callout[data-callout]`, `.callout-title`, `.callout-content`), so Obsidian CSS snippets apply. Foldable callouts are `<details>`/`<summary>`: native, keyboard-accessible, no script. Lucide icons as CSS masks (`data:` URLs, allowed by `img-src`). Tasks inside work like any task (`> - [ ]`). |
| **Code** ```` ```lang ```` | Fence renderer: `<pre class="code-block" data-lang>` with the escaped code. | `hydrateRich` highlights with the editor's grammars (`@codemirror/language-data`, by name, alias or file extension) through `@lezer/highlight`'s `highlightCode` and the shared `features/codeTokens.ts` classes. Spans are built with DOM calls, never HTML. Theme-aware through the `--tok-*` variables. Unknown languages stay plain. Every fenced block gets a Copy button (on hover; always shown on touch screens). |
| **Math** `$…$`, `$$…$$` | `@mdit/plugin-tex` (parsing only: `$` must not touch a space on its inner side and the closing `$` must not precede a digit, so `$5 and $10` stays text) plus a rule for `$$…$$` inside a paragraph (display math, as in Obsidian). Placeholders hold the TeX as text. | KaTeX 0.19 with `mhchem` (`\ce{H2O}`): `trust: false` (no `\href`, `\url`, `\includegraphics`, `\html*`), `throwOnError: false` (errors show inline in red), `maxSize`/`maxExpand` bounds, `output: 'htmlAndMathml'`. The output goes through a math DOMPurify config (HTML, MathML, KaTeX's SVG; no links, images or styles) and is cached as a fragment. Fonts and CSS are bundled. |
| **Mermaid** ```` ```mermaid ```` | Fence renderer: `<div class="mermaid-block" data-mermaid>` holding the source. | Mermaid 12.2 with `securityLevel: 'strict'` (labels encoded, no click handlers or links), `secure` keys a diagram's `%%{init}%%` or frontmatter `config:` cannot change (`securityLevel`, `layout`, `themeCSS`, `dompurifyConfig`, limits), the `dark`/`default` theme by app theme (re-rendered when the theme changes), then an SVG DOMPurify config (SVG, its scoped `<style>`, HTML labels in `<foreignObject>`; no scripts, handlers, links, `@import` or remote `url()`/`href`). Errors show inline as text above the source. Wide diagrams shrink to 60 % at most, then scroll sideways. |

**Layout engine.** Mermaid 12 defaults to the ELK layout, whose `elkjs` is EPL-2.0 (not GPL-compatible) and 1.4 MB. `vite.config.ts` aliases `elkjs/lib/elk.bundled.js` to a stub that throws, Mermaid is initialized with `layout: 'dagre'` (Mermaid's classic layout, which Obsidian also uses), and `layout` is a secure key, so `layout: elk` in a diagram is ignored rather than failing.

**Placeholders and patching.** The HTML never depends on which lazy modules are loaded, so a block's patch key is the same on every render, and `hydrateRich` marks what it hydrated (a `WeakSet`): an edit elsewhere never re-renders a diagram or formula. When a block is re-inserted because it changed, hydration uses the already-loaded modules synchronously (KaTeX, loaded grammars, Mermaid's per-theme SVG cache), so it appears without a flash. The placeholder attributes `data-math`, `data-mermaid` and `data-lang` are app-reserved like the embed attributes: the sanitizer strips them from raw HTML, so a note cannot forge a placeholder.

**API for other renderers** (`render/rich/index.ts`; Live Preview, ROADMAP item 10, is the intended user). Each function fills the element it is given, produces the reading view's markup and classes, and never parses note text as HTML:

```ts
parseCalloutHeader(line: string): CalloutHeader | null          // '[!faq]- Why?' → {type:'faq', kind:'question', fold:'-', title:'Why?', metadata:null}
renderCallout(header, title?: Node | string, content?: Node): HTMLElement   // <div|details class="callout">; nodes must be trusted
renderMath(el: HTMLElement, tex: string, displayMode: boolean): Promise<void>  // synchronous once KaTeX is loaded
renderMermaid(el: HTMLElement, source: string): Promise<void>  // .mermaid-svg or .mermaid-error child; follows the theme
highlightCode(code: string, lang: string): Promise<DocumentFragment | null>  // null: unknown language
highlightCodeSync(code: string, lang: string): DocumentFragment | null       // null until the grammar is loaded
hydrateRich(root: ParentNode): void                              // fills placeholders produced by richMarkdown
preloadRich({math?, mermaid?, code?}): void
```

**Sizes** (production build, 2026-10-21). The initial load grows by 2.9 kB (1.5 kB gzip, +0.4 %): the editor's math syntax and the shared token classes, which the session language needs. The reading-view chunk grows from 150 to 164 kB (61 → 65 kB gzip), plus 11 kB of lazy CSS. On first use: KaTeX 293 kB (86 kB gzip) + 30 kB CSS + only the woff2 fonts a formula needs (256 kB for all 20); the highlighter 1 kB plus the grammars the editor already loads; Mermaid about 0.9–1.0 MB (250–280 kB gzip) for its core and one diagram type, more for diagrams with the Langium parser (pie, about 1.55 MB / 385 kB gzip).

### 6.8 Extension points: view registry, pages, panels, commands, events

```ts
// views/registry.ts (re-exported from extensions/)
export interface FileViewProps  { path: string; entry: FileEntry; mode: 'edit' | 'read' }
export interface EmbedProps     { path: string; entry: FileEntry; subpath?: string; display?: string;
                                  width?: number; height?: number;  // from |W or |WxH (added with item 5)
                                  sourcePath: string; depth: number }
export interface FileViewDescriptor {
  id: string;                                   // 'markdown' | 'image' | 'pdf' | 'media' | 'text' | 'unsupported' | later 'sheet' | 'excalidraw' | 'tasks' | 'canvas'
  match(entry: FileEntry): number;              // 0 = no match; highest wins. Convention: length of the matched suffix (".excalidraw.md" beats ".md")
  icon: LucideIcon;
  textual: boolean;                             // true → content via DocumentSession over GET/PUT /api/files (versioned, autosave, conflicts, live sync)
  modes: Array<'edit' | 'read'>;                // which toggles the header shows
  load(): Promise<{ default: ComponentType<FileViewProps> }>;            // lazy chunk
  loadEmbed?(): Promise<{ default: ComponentType<EmbedProps> }>;         // for ![[file]] in reading view
  create?: { label: string; extension: string; initialContent(): string }; // adds "New <label>" to explorer menu + palette
  merge?(base: string, mine: string, theirs: string): string | null;     // optional semantic 3-way merge for conflicts (null = fall back to banner)
  commands?: Command[];                          // contributed to the palette while this view is active
}
```
