The java.time column is for future server-side use only (roadmap item 7, capture to the daily note); note that Java's `YYYY` and `DD` mean something else.

| moment token | Output | dayjs | java.time equivalent |
|---|---|---|---|
| `YYYY` / `YY` | 2026 / 26 | core | `uuuu` / `uu` (**not** `YYYY`) |
| `Q` | 4 | advancedFormat | `Q` |
| `M` `MM` `MMM` `MMMM` | 10 10 Oct October | core | `M` `MM` `MMM` `MMMM` |
| `D` / `DD` | 16 / 16 | core | `d` / `dd` (**not** `DD`) |
| `Do` | 16th | advancedFormat | custom ordinal |
| `d` | 5 | core | custom (Sunday = 0) |
| `dd` `ddd` `dddd` | Fr Fri Friday | core | custom / `EEE` / `EEEE` |
| `w` `ww` `wo` | 42 42 42nd | weekOfYear (+advancedFormat for `wo`) | `WeekFields.of(Locale.US).weekOfWeekBasedYear()` |
| `W` `WW` | 42 42 | isoWeek | `IsoFields.WEEK_OF_WEEK_BASED_YEAR` |
| `gggg` | 2026 | weekYear | `WeekFields.of(Locale.US).weekBasedYear()` |
| `GGGG` | 2026 | isoWeek | `IsoFields.WEEK_BASED_YEAR` |
| `H` `HH` / `h` `hh` | 14 14 / 2 02 | core | `H` `HH` / `h` `hh` |
| `m` `mm` / `s` `ss` | 3 03 / 9 09 | core | `m` `mm` / `s` `ss` |
| `A` / `a` | PM / pm | core | `a` / lower-cased `a` |
| `X` / `x` | Unix seconds / Unix milliseconds | advancedFormat | `getEpochSecond()` / `toEpochMilli()` |
| `LT` `LTS` `L` `LL` `LLL` `LLLL` | 2:03 PM · 2:03:09 PM · 10/16/2026 · October 16, 2026 · October 16, 2026 2:03 PM · Friday, October 16, 2026 2:03 PM | localizedFormat | `DateTimeFormatter.ofLocalized*` |
| `[text]` | literal text | core | `'text'` |
| **Unsupported:** `E` `e` `DDD` `DDDD` | Differs from moment | n/a | Avoid in formats. The settings UI warns when they are typed. |

### 6.13 Quick switcher and command palette

**Quick switcher (`Mod-O`).**

- **Items:**
  - every note (label: basename; detail: folder);
  - one extra item per alias ("alias of X");
  - every other file (label: file name).
- **Ranking:** `fuzzysort.go(q, items, {keys: ['label', 'path'], threshold: 0, limit: 50})`, with notes boosted by +0.05.
- **Empty query:** the 20 most recently opened files on this device (`features/recent.ts`), a star marking the bookmarked ones.
- **Keys:**
  - `Enter` opens the selection.
  - `Shift-Enter` creates a note named exactly the query at the default new-note location (`POST /api/files {onConflict:"fail"}`; a 409 opens the existing note).
  - When no item matches the query exactly, the last row is **Create "query"**.
  - `↑/↓`, `Esc`.
- **Phones:** a full-screen sheet with 48 px rows, opened from the TopBar's switcher/search button.
- **Focus.** The switcher, palette, search field and prompt dialog are bundled **eagerly** (not lazy chunks) and focus their input synchronously inside the tap or key handler (or in `useLayoutEffect` during a synchronous render). iOS opens the keyboard only for focus inside a user gesture.

**Command palette (`Mod-P`).** Fuzzy search over command names. Each row shows the command's hotkey. Recently used commands come first. Selecting a row executes it. Commands contributed by views appear only while that view is active; commands registered with `registerCommand` are always present. On phones it opens from the ⋯ menu and from a MobileToolbar button, as in Obsidian mobile.

| id | Name | Default hotkey |
|---|---|---|
| `app.quickSwitcher` | Open quick switcher | `Mod-O` |
| `app.commandPalette` | Open command palette | `Mod-P` |
| `file.newNote` | Create new note | `Mod-Alt-N` |
| `file.newNoteFromTemplate` | Create new note from template | |
| `file.newFolder` | Create new folder | |
| `file.rename` | Rename current file | `F2` |
| `file.move` | Move current file to… | |
| `file.delete` | Delete current file | |
| `file.reveal` | Reveal current file in explorer | |
| `file.copyLink` | Copy link to current file | |
| `file.download` | Download current file | |
| `editor.toggleMode` | Toggle reading/editing view | `Mod-E` |
| `editor.save` | Save now | `Mod-S` |
| `editor.followLink` | Follow link under cursor | `Mod-Enter` |
| `editor.toggleCheckbox` | Toggle checklist status | `Mod-L` |
| `editor.bold` / `editor.italic` | Toggle bold / italic | `Mod-B` / `Mod-I` |
| `editor.insertLink` | Insert Markdown link | `Mod-K` |
| `editor.insertWikilink` | Insert internal link | |
| `editor.insertAttachment` | Insert attachment… | |
| `editor.find` | Find/replace in current note | `Mod-F` |
| `search.open` | Search in all notes | `Mod-Shift-F` |
| `daily.today` / `daily.prev` / `daily.next` | Open today's / previous / next daily note | |
| `templates.insert` | Insert template | |
| `view.toggleLeft` / `view.toggleRight` | Toggle left / right sidebar | |
| `view.files` / `view.search` / `view.bookmarks` / `view.tags` / `view.recent` / `view.backlinks` / `view.outline` / `view.history` | Show panel | |
| `bookmarks.add` / `bookmarks.remove` | Bookmark current file / Remove bookmark for current file (whichever applies) | |
| `bookmarks.heading` | Bookmark heading at cursor (the nearest heading above it) | |
| `bookmarks.search` | Bookmark current search | |
| `bookmarks.newGroup` | Create new bookmark group | |
| `editor.renameHeading` | Rename heading and update links to it… (the heading at or above the cursor; §6.24) | |
| `history.commitNow` | Commit pending changes now | |
| `nav.back` / `nav.forward` | Navigate back / forward | `Mod-Alt-←` / `Mod-Alt-→` (as in Obsidian; bound at `Prec.highest`, so they also work inside the editor). The browser's own back and forward still work. |
| `trash.open` | Open trash | |
| `settings.open` | Open settings | `Mod-,` |
| `theme.toggle` | Toggle dark/light theme | |
| `app.resync` | Reload vault from server | |
| `capture.quick` | Quick capture (§6.27) | `Mod-Shift-Space` (works in the editor too) |

`Mod` is `Cmd` on Apple platforms and `Ctrl` elsewhere. Browser-reserved combinations (`Mod-N`, `Mod-T`, `Mod-W`, `Mod-Shift-N`) cannot be intercepted, which is why "new note" is `Mod-Alt-N`. Every key can be changed, removed or given to a command without one, per device, under Settings, "Hotkeys" (§6.25).

### 6.14 Keyboard shortcuts (complete v1 set)

| Context | Keys |
|---|---|
| Global | The command hotkeys above. `Esc` closes the topmost overlay or the drawer. `Mod-O`, `Mod-P`, `Mod-Shift-F`, `Mod-,` and `Mod-Alt-←/→` work even while the editor has focus, and so do commands registered with `globalHotkey` (quick add, `Mod-Alt-T`, §6.22), and every key chosen under Settings, "Hotkeys" (§6.25). Off Apple platforms AltGr arrives as Ctrl+Alt: in a text field, a key that types a character with it (AltGr+T is ț in Romanian) types that character rather than running a hotkey. |
| Task list page | `Enter` or `Space` on a task opens its inline editor (`Esc` or Done closes it); `Alt-↑/↓` move it among its siblings; `Alt-→` makes it a subtask of the task above, `Alt-←` moves it out of its parent (§6.22) |
| Editor | CodeMirror defaults: undo `Mod-Z`; redo `Mod-Shift-Z` or `Mod-Y`; indent `Tab`; outdent `Shift-Tab`; find `Mod-F`; next and previous match `Mod-G` / `Mod-Shift-G`; completion `Ctrl-Space`; accept with `Enter`. Plus `Mod-B/I/K/L/E/S/Enter`. **`Esc` then `Tab`** moves focus out of the editor (CodeMirror's tab escape, needed because `Tab` indents); a one-time hint says so the first time the editor gets keyboard focus. With **Vim key bindings** on (Settings › This device, §6.36): vim's keys, with the app's keys above taking precedence; `:w` saves, `:q` leaves the editor. |
| Explorer (focused) | `↑/↓` move; `←/→` collapse and expand; `Enter` opens; `F2` renames; `Delete` deletes (with confirmation); `Mod-Alt-N` creates a new note in the focused folder |
| Quick switcher / palette | `↑/↓`, `Enter`, `Shift-Enter` (create), `Esc` |
| Conflict banner | Buttons reachable with `Tab`; `Esc` dismisses Compare |

### 6.15 File explorer

- **Tree.** It is built from `['tree']`. Folders come first, then files, both sorted with `localeCompare(…, {numeric: true, sensitivity: 'base'})`. An alternative "modified, newest first" sort can be selected and is persisted. Hidden entries never appear. The flattened visible rows are **virtualized** with `VirtualList` (fixed row height per breakpoint), so a `Daily` folder with thousands of notes stays fast; the tags pane and long backlink lists use the same component.
- **Rows.** Each row shows an icon from the registry descriptor, the name (notes without `.md`), and a context menu: right-click on desktop; on touch, a `⋯` button and a **long-press** (about 500 ms of pointer-down without movement; iOS Safari fires no `contextmenu` on long-press). Rows set `user-select: none` and `-webkit-touch-callout: none`. Menu items:
  - New note here, New folder here, and the view `create` items;
  - Rename (inline);
  - Move to… (a folder-picker dialog);
  - Delete (with confirmation);
  - Copy link, File history, Download.
- **Move to… dialog.** Before confirming, it calls `POST /api/move` with `dryRun: true` and shows "N notes will be updated" (and any notes that cannot be updated). This is the rename/move preview.
- **Drag and drop (desktop).** Dropping a row on a folder runs `POST /api/move` without a preview. A toast reads "Moved X · updated links in N notes" with **Undo**. Undo is a reverse move with link updating on: the links resolve again, but their text is regenerated with the current link format, collateral-protection rewrites are not reverted, and it makes its own commits. Touch devices use "Move to…" instead of drag.
- **Dropping OS files** on a folder: the client first checks the tree for a name collision (case-insensitive). If there is one, it asks **Replace** (non-text files only), **Keep both** or **Skip** *before* uploading, so nothing is uploaded twice. It then sends one `PUT /api/files/raw?path=<joinPath(folder, name)>&onConflict=fail|rename|overwrite` per file with an `Idempotency-Key`; with `rename` the server picks the final name and returns it.
- **Rename.** The inline title above the note and the explorer's inline rename both edit the basename only; the extension is kept.
  - It commits on `Enter` or blur; `Esc` reverts; nothing is sent when the name is unchanged.
  - Validation follows the new-name rules (§4.4) and is shown inline before sending. A `/` is rejected ("Use Move to… to change folders"). Server errors are shown as given.
- **New note.** `POST /api/files {path: joinPath(folder, 'Untitled.md'), content: '', onConflict: 'rename'}` with an `Idempotency-Key` (`joinPath('', x) === x`, so the root folder works). The flow is **optimistic**: the tap immediately navigates to a pending view whose inline title input is focused synchronously within the same tap (so iOS opens the keyboard), the POST runs in the background, and the route switches to the created path when it returns. A title committed before then waits for the POST and becomes a rename.

### 6.16 Mobile layout (iPhone Safari first)

| Breakpoint | Layout |
|---|---|
| **Phone** `< 768 px` | Single column. **TopBar:** ☰ (left drawer), title with the connection dot, **+** (quick capture, §6.27), a switcher/search button (quick switcher; its sheet has a "Search in notes" row), edit/read toggle, and ⋯ (Command palette, Backlinks, Outline, History, Bookmark, Rename, Move, Delete, Template, Today, Reload). **Drawer:** full-height, 88 % width, slides in from the left; Files, Search, Bookmarks, Tags and Recent files tabs (plus registered left panels), **New note** and **Today** buttons, and a footer with **Settings**, **Trash** and the **theme toggle**. Right-side panels open as a **bottom sheet** (70 % height), dismissed by dragging its handle, or by swiping down only when its content is scrolled to the top, so scrolling the sheet never closes it. |
| **Tablet** `768–1199 px` | The ribbon is a collapsed icon rail (48 px) that is always visible, so every desktop entry point (switcher, search, Today, template, new note, palette, Trash, Settings) stays one tap away on an iPad. Both sidebars are overlays over the editor, toggled from the rail or the TopBar. |
| **Desktop** `≥ 1200 px` | Ribbon (48 px), left sidebar (default 280 px, resizable 200–480), workspace (readable width 598 px, 46 em of the 13 px UI size, when `readableLineLength`), right sidebar (300 px, resizable). Status bar at the bottom. |

**iOS and touch specifics:**

- **Viewport.** The shell is pinned to the visual viewport through `--vvh` and `--vv-top`, set by `installViewportVars` (research sketch). **`WorkspaceScroll` is the only scroller**, on every breakpoint: CodeMirror sizes to its content (`.cm-editor {height: auto}`, `.cm-scroller {overflow: visible}`), which CM6 supports, still virtualizing against the scroll parent. The inline title and properties therefore scroll away with the text instead of staying pinned above it, which matters when the keyboard leaves only 350–400 px. `html, body {overflow: hidden; overscroll-behavior: none}` turns off the page bounce and pull-to-refresh; ⋯ has a **Reload** item instead.
- **Keyboard open.** `mobileViewport.ts` sets a `keyboard-open` flag when `visualViewport.height < 0.75 × innerHeight`. While it is set, the TopBar collapses (it slides away and returns when the keyboard closes), leaving the space to the text and the toolbar. On phones, `NoteHeader` shows only the inline title, because the TopBar already has the save status, the mode toggle and ⋯.
- **Safe areas.** `env(safe-area-inset-*)` padding on the TopBar, the drawer, the sheet and the toolbar, and `padding-inline: max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-left), env(safe-area-inset-right))` on the editor content and the reading view, so text never runs under the notch or Dynamic Island in landscape.
- **MobileToolbar.** The last grid row, so it sits right above the keyboard, on `(pointer: coarse)`.
  - It is **always mounted**; a CSS class shows it from a debounced (150 ms) "editor focused or toolbar in use" state. Unmounting it on blur would remove a button before its click is delivered.
  - Every button has `tabIndex={-1}` and `onPointerDown={e => e.preventDefault()}`, so tapping it never moves focus: the editor keeps focus, the keyboard stays open, and the button dispatches straight to the `EditorView`.
  - Buttons: `[[ ]]`, `#`, checkbox, backtick, `---`, outdent, indent, undo, redo, **Follow link**, **Command palette**, image, **Record voice note** (§6.30; it puts the keyboard away for the recording panel), and hide keyboard (`blur`).
  - The image button triggers the **app-level** hidden `<input type=file accept="image/*" multiple>` hosted by `AppShell`. The selection to insert at is remembered before the picker opens, because opening the picker blurs the editor; an input inside the toolbar would be lost with its `change` event.
- **Typography and targets.** Editor and input font size is at least **16 px**, so iOS does not zoom on focus. Every tap target is at least **44×44 px**. `touch-action: manipulation` removes the tap delay.
- **No hover-only UI.** Tree rows open their menu with long-press or `⋯` (§6.15).
- **Focus on iOS.** Programmatic focus opens the keyboard only inside a user gesture. Overlays with a text field are eager and focus synchronously (§6.13); New note uses the optimistic flow (§6.15). Nothing focuses an input after an `await`.
- **Back gesture.** Opening the drawer, the sheet or an overlay pushes a history entry, so Safari's edge swipe closes it instead of leaving the note (§6.4).
- **Selection.** The native caret and handles are used (no `drawSelection` on iOS).
- **Lifecycle.** iOS kills SSE in the background. On `visibilitychange` to visible, and on `pageshow` with `persisted` true (the page came back from the back/forward cache), `EventStream` checks `readyState` and reconnects with the `lastEventId` query parameter, and every open session revalidates.

### 6.17 Theming

- Themes are CSS custom properties. The dark values are defined on `:root` itself (so they apply even before any script runs) and repeated on `:root[data-theme="dark"]`; the light values are on `:root[data-theme="light"]`. The `system` preference follows `prefers-color-scheme`.
- Variable names mirror Obsidian's, so future theme snippets port easily.
- **Before first paint.** `<html data-theme="dark">` is static, and the classic script `/theme-boot.js` in `<head>` (§6.1) reads the per-device preference (`notesapp:prefs`), resolves `system`, sets `data-theme`, and updates `meta[name=theme-color]` and the `color-scheme` meta. There is never an unstyled or wrong-theme flash. The toggle updates the same three things at runtime.

| Variable | Dark | Light |
|---|---|---|
| `--background-primary` | `#1e1e1e` | `#ffffff` |
| `--background-secondary` | `#262626` | `#f6f6f6` |
| `--background-modifier-border` | `#363636` | `#e3e3e3` |
| `--background-modifier-hover` | `rgba(255,255,255,0.06)` | `rgba(0,0,0,0.05)` |
| `--text-normal` | `#dadada` | `#222222` |
| `--text-muted` | `#a3a3a3` | `#5c5c5c` |
| `--text-faint` | `#6e6e6e` | `#9a9a9a` |
| `--interactive-accent` / `--accent` | `#8a5cf5` | `#7a4fe0` |
| `--link` | `#a68af9` | `#6b3fd8` |
| `--link-unresolved` | `#a68af9` at 60 % opacity, dashed underline | the same at 60 % |
| `--tag-bg` / `--tag-fg` | `rgba(138,92,245,0.15)` / `#b9a2fb` | `rgba(122,79,224,0.1)` / `#6b3fd8` |
| `--code-bg` | `#2a2a2a` | `#f2f2f2` |
| `--text-error` / `--text-warning` / `--text-success` | `#fb464c` / `#e0ac00` / `#44cf6e` | `#e93147` / `#c48c00` / `#08b94e` |

- **Fonts:** `--font-text: system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", Roboto, sans-serif` and `--font-mono: ui-monospace, "SF Mono", "Cascadia Code", Consolas, monospace`. No web fonts.
- **Contrast** meets WCAG AA for body text in both themes. The faint colour is used only for syntax marks.

### 6.18 Connection and offline indicator

`ConnectionIndicator` sits in the status bar (desktop) or shows as a dot on the TopBar title (phone).

| State | Shown when |
|---|---|
| **Live** (green) | `EventSource` is open and an event of any kind, `ping` included, arrived within 45 s. Pings come every 20 s, so an idle vault stays Live. |
| **Reconnecting** (amber) | `onerror` fired or the stream is stale. The `onerror` handler checks `readyState` **at once**: if it is `CLOSED` (the browser gives up for good on a non-200 response such as serve's `502` during a deploy), the app recreates the `EventSource` with `lastEventId` and backoff (1, 2, 5, 10, then every 30 s) instead of waiting for the 45 s watchdog; if it is `CONNECTING`, the browser's own `retry: 3000` applies. |
| **Offline** (red) | `navigator.onLine === false` or 3 consecutive transient API failures (§6.9). Saves queue. The banner reads "Offline: edits are saved on this device and will sync when the connection returns." |

On recovery, any `resync` is processed, the replay countdown reaches `caughtUp` (§6.3), and then the queued saves are retried.

**History warning.** When `/api/status` reports `git.state: "failing"`, the status bar (desktop) and the ⋯ menu (phone) show "History is not being saved" with the time since `failingSince`. `status.changed` events keep this current.

**Mirror warning** (ROADMAP item 2, §5.8 *Mirror*). The same places show "Mirror is not being updated (for 2 hours)" while `mirror.state` is `failing`, and "Mirror stopped: it has commits this vault does not" while it is `diverged`. On the desktop the warning is a button: its tooltip is `lastError` (plus, when diverged, that NotesApp never force-pushes), and clicking it calls `POST /api/mirror/push`; the phone's ⋯ menu adds **Push to mirror now**. The palette has **Push history to the mirror now** whenever a mirror is configured. The outcome arrives as `status.changed`. A mirror that was set up but is `disabled` by a configuration error (any `reason` other than `not configured` and `history is disabled`, `mirrorMisconfigured`) shows "Mirror is off: <reason>" in the same places and among the status page's problems; there is nothing to push, so the warning is plain text and the ⋯ menu offers no push. Settings › About lists the history size (`disk.repoBytes`), the files kept out of history, and the mirror (remote, last push, next push, or the error).

**New build after a deploy.** Every deploy replaces the bundle, but an open tab, especially an iPhone Safari tab that stays open for weeks, keeps running the old one. Its lazy chunks are gone from the new image, and it speaks the old API contract.

- The page knows the build it loaded (`import.meta.env.VITE_BUILD_SHA`). `state/buildCheck.ts` compares it with `hello.buildSha` and with the `X-Notes-Build` response header.
- On a mismatch, the page reloads itself when no session is dirty or saving (drafts would survive a reload anyway). Otherwise it shows "A new version is available. Reload", and blocks settings writes until the reload, so an old tab cannot write an old-shaped settings file.
- A `vite:preloadError` event (a lazy chunk of the old build is gone) takes the same path.
- In development both values are `dev`, so the check never fires.
- With the service worker (§6.26) the reload first lets the new worker take over (at most 10 s), and a page never reloads itself twice within a minute for the same server build: the banner shows instead.

#### 6.18.1 Status page (ROADMAP item 38)

`/x/status` shows the server at a glance. It is registered in `extensions/builtins.ts` with `registerPage` (route `status`, title *Status*, icon `Activity`), so it gets what every page gets (§6.8): a ribbon button, an entry in the phone drawer, the palette command *Open Status*, and its own lazy chunk (`components/pages/StatusPage.tsx`). Settings › About links to it, and tapping an ntfy alert opens it (§5.18.1).

- **Data.** `useStatus()` (`/api/status`, kept current by `status.changed`) and `useStatusDetails()` (`/api/status/details`, API.md §6.5), refetched every 30 s while the page is open. The details' query key `['status', 'details']` sits under `['status']`, so every invalidation of the status (`status.changed`, `history.committed`, the *Refresh* button) refreshes both. Relative times tick every 30 s.
- **Problems first** (`features/opsStatus.ts`, `statusProblems`). Errors: history failing, less than 1 GiB free, a failed backup. Warnings: the mirror failing, diverged or off because its settings cannot be used, the watcher polling, read-only settings, unwritable folders, files not shown, stale backups, an unusable `ntfy.json`, or an alert that could not be sent. With none, the page says "Everything is working."
- **Cards.**
  - *Server*: version and build, this page's build, uptime, the Java runtime, whether the AOT cache is in use, heap and CPUs.
  - *History*: state and error; commits in the history, during this run, and failed attempts; the last commit and the files waiting for the next one; the repository size; the files kept out of history with their sizes, each opening the file; the mirror. *Commit now* appears while files wait, and *Push to the mirror* while a mirror is configured.
  - *Vault and index*: counts, the index build time, the last rescan, the watcher, files not shown and folders not writable.
  - *Disk*: free of total, with a meter, and whether uploads are accepted.
  - *Backups*: the state, the last run and success, the snapshot, the off-site result and the failures.
  - *App alerts*: whether ntfy is on, alerts sent and held back, the last error, and the markers logged since the server started. *Send a test alert* appears while alerts are on; a toast says whether ntfy accepted it.
  - *Live sync*: the open SSE connections and this device's connection.
- **Layout and themes.** Cards in a responsive grid (`auto-fit`, at least 26 rem wide) that becomes one column on tablets and phones; under 768 px each row's label sits above its value. Buttons and file links are 44 px tall on touch screens. Colours come from the theme tokens (§6.17), so both themes work.

### 6.19 Accessibility basics

- **Landmarks:** `nav` (sidebar), `main` (workspace) and `complementary` (right panel).
- **Explorer:** follows the ARIA tree pattern (`role=tree`/`treeitem`, `aria-expanded`, roving tabindex).
- **Switcher and palette:** follow the ARIA combobox and listbox pattern (`aria-activedescendant`).
- **Dialogs:** use `role=dialog`, `aria-modal`, trap focus, and return focus on close.
- **Live regions:** save status and toasts use `aria-live="polite"`; the conflict banner uses `role="alert"`.
- **Focus and motion:** a visible `:focus-visible` ring; `prefers-reduced-motion` disables drawer and sheet animations.
- **Focus after navigation:** after a keyboard-initiated navigation to a note, focus moves to the editor (or the reading view's container); after a pointer navigation, the note's name is announced through a polite live region. Focus never stays stranded on the switcher's trigger.
- **Editor escape:** `Esc` then `Tab` leaves the editor (§6.14).
- **Icons:** every icon-only button has an `aria-label` and a tooltip.
- **Touch targets in Compare:** under `(pointer: coarse)`, the merge view's per-chunk accept and reject controls are enlarged to 44 px.
- **Checkboxes** in the reading view get an `aria-label` from their list item's text (§6.7).

### 6.20 Error UX

`ApiError` maps `code` to friendly text. Examples:

- `invalid-name`: "Names can't contain # ^ [ ] | or %%."
- `case-collision`: "A file with the same name (different case) already exists."
- `too-large`: "This file is too large to edit here; download it instead."
- `history-unavailable`: "History is not available on the server" (with the `reason`: disabled, importing, or commit failing).
- `permission-denied`: "The server cannot write to this folder. It may have been created as another user over SSH." (with the `path`)
- `insufficient-storage`: "The server is low on disk space."
- `identity-not-allowed`: "This device's Tailscale login is not allowed to use NotesApp."
- `settings-read-only`: "Settings were written by a newer version of NotesApp and are read-only here."

Any other error shows the problem's `detail`. 5xx problem errors show a toast with the `incidentId` for log lookup. Transient failures (§6.9) never show an error toast; they show the Offline state. An unexpected render error is caught by an `ErrorBoundary` per view, which offers **Reload view**.

### 6.21 Spreadsheets (`.sheet`, ROADMAP item 6)

Built (6a, 6b, 6c): the file format, its parser and serializer, the formula engine adapter and the cell-level merge in `frontend/src/sheets/`; the grid view that uses them in `frontend/src/views/sheet/` (registered with `registerView`, §6.8; §6.21.5); embeds in notes and search indexing (§6.21.7); number formats (§6.21.8); charts (§6.21.9); and CSV and Excel import and export (§6.21.10). `.sheet` is a text kind (§4.2) with versioned saves, conflicts, history and live sync; the only server part is `SheetDocumentIndexer`, which feeds search and backlinks.

| Module | Role | Bundle |
|---|---|---|
| `a1.ts` | A1 references: columns A..XFD, rows 1..1048576 (Excel's and Google Sheets' limits), normalization, row-major order | main |
| `format.ts` | Types, `parseSheet()` (validate, repair or refuse), `serializeSheet()` (stable layout), `cellInput`, `cellText`, sheet-name rules | main |
| `numfmt.ts`, `input.ts`, `display.ts` | Number formats (§6.21.8), typed input with the formats it implies, how a cell shows (in its format: the cell's, else the row's, else the column's) | main |
| `target.ts` | What an embed's or link's subpath names: a sheet, a named range or a range (§6.21.7) | main |
| `merge.ts` | `mergeSheetText(base, mine, theirs)`: the descriptor's `merge()` | main |
| `engine.ts` | `Workbook`: HyperFormula adapter, cached values, undo and redo, remote replace | **lazy chunk** (764 kB, 163 kB gzipped) |
| `loadEngine.ts` | `loadSheetEngine()`, the only way in to `engine.ts` | main |
| `refs.ts`, `tsv.ts`, `edits.ts` | Moving formulas (`shiftFormula`, `renameSheetInFormula`), the TSV clipboard format, and fill, clear, copy and paste as cell edits (§6.21.5) | main |
| `charts.ts`, `csv.ts` | What a chart shows of its range (§6.21.9); CSV in and out (§6.21.10) | with the view's or the embed's chunk |
| `xlsx.ts` | Excel workbooks in and out through SheetJS (§6.21.10) | **lazy chunk** (490 kB, 160 kB gzipped), loaded only by an import or export of a workbook |
| `activeSheet.ts`, `views/sheet/register.ts` | The view descriptor and its palette commands | main |
| `views/sheet/*` | The grid view: Glide Data Grid, formula bar, tabs, dialogs, charts pane, import and export, the session binding | **lazy chunk** (361 kB, 117 kB gzipped, plus 16 kB of CSS) |
| `views/sheet/SheetEmbed.tsx`, `SheetChart.tsx` | The embed (`loadEmbed()`) and the SVG charts it shares with the grid view | **lazy chunks** (3 kB and 8 kB, plus 3 kB of CSS); no engine, no grid |

Embeds, search and merges need neither the engine nor the grid, because every formula's result is cached in the file.

**Licence.** HyperFormula is used under its GPL-3.0 option, so the repository is `GPL-3.0-only` (`LICENSE`, `package.json`, `pom.xml`; decided 2026-10-17). `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists every shipped package and its licence; it is generated by `scripts/third-party-notices.mjs` from the frontend lockfile and the backend's resolved runtime dependencies, and is regenerated after dependency changes.

#### 6.21.1 File format

```json
{
  "type": "notesapp-sheet",
  "version": 1,
  "engine": "hyperformula@3.4.0",
  "sheets": [
    {
      "id": "s1",
      "name": "Budget",
      "cells": {
        "A1": { "v": "Item" },
        "B1": { "v": "Cost" },
        "B2": { "v": 1450 },
        "B10": { "f": "=SUM(B2:B9)", "c": 1937.5 },
        "D1": { "f": "=SEQUENCE(2)", "c": 1 },
        "D2": { "c": 2 },
        "E1": { "f": "=1/0", "c": { "e": "#DIV/0!" } },
        "F1": { "v": 0.125, "fmt": "0.0%" }
      },
      "cols": {
        "A": { "w": 180 },
        "B": { "w": 110, "fmt": "$#,##0.00" }
      },
      "rows": {
        "1": { "bold": true }
      },
      "frozen": { "rows": 1, "cols": 0 },
      "charts": {
        "c0mgv1a2b3x7f2": { "type": "bar", "range": "A1:B9", "title": "Costs", "headers": true, "labels": true }
      }
    }
  ],
  "named": {
    "Total": "Budget!$B$10"
  }
}
```

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `type`, `version` | Always `"notesapp-sheet"` and `1`. A higher version is refused (read-only until NotesApp is updated). |
| `engine` | `name@version` of the engine that computed the `c` values. `""` means no single engine run did (a merged file); see §6.21.3. |
| `sheets[]` | In tab order. `id` is a stable identity for merges (new sheets get random ids such as `s4k2x9q`, so sheets added on two devices never collide); formulas refer to sheets by `name`, which is unique without regard to case. |
| `cells` | Sparse, keyed by canonical A1 references. `v` is a literal (text, number or `true`/`false`), `f` a formula starting with `=`, `c` the cached result of `f`, `fmt` the cell's number format (§6.21.8; written after `c`). An error result is `{"e": "#DIV/0!"}`; an empty result is `null`. A cell with only `c` holds a value an array formula spilled into it; a cell with only `fmt` is formatted and empty. |
| `cols`, `rows` | Keyed by column letters and by 1-based row numbers: `w` (width) or `h` (height) in CSS pixels, `fmt` (number format), `bold`. |
| `frozen` | Frozen rows and columns. |
| `charts` | The sheet's charts (§6.21.9), keyed by id, one per line: `type` (`bar`, `line` or `pie`), `range` (an A1 range of this sheet), `title`, `headers` (the first row names the series), `labels` (the first column labels the categories). Written only when the sheet has charts, so files without charts keep the layout of earlier versions. Ids are `c`, the creation time in base 36 and a random tail: they sort in the order the charts were made, which is the order they show in, and charts made on two devices never collide. |
| `structure` | A random marker that changes with every insertion or deletion of rows or columns in the sheet (§6.21.3). Written after `charts`, only once set, so other files keep their layout. |
| `named` | Named ranges and constants: name → expression without the leading `=` (`Budget!$B$10`, `0.2`, `Budget!$B$2*2`). |

**Unknown keys survive.** Keys this version does not know, at every level (file, sheet, cell, column, row, chart), are kept in an `extra` record and written back after the known ones, so a tab running older code never strips what newer code added (code from before 6c keeps a cell's `fmt` and a sheet's `charts` this way). A known key with an unusable value (`"w": "wide"`, a chart `type` of `donut`) is kept the same way, and is written back until the field is set; a `charts` value that is not an object is kept aside and written back while the sheet has no charts.

**Stable layout.** `serializeSheet()` always writes: the top-level keys in the order above; sheets in tab order; cells in row-major order (`A1, B1, AA1, A2, A10`); columns by index; rows by number; named ranges by name without regard to case; one cell, column, row or named range per line, as `{ "v": 1, "c": 2 }`; an empty collection as `{}`; LF line endings and a final newline. Writing the same model twice gives the same bytes, so a one-cell edit is a one-line diff and git history stays readable.

**Parsing: repair or refuse, never lose.** `parseSheet()` returns the model and a list of repairs, or an error. A repair is made only when nothing is lost; anything the parser cannot place is refused, and a refused file is left untouched for the user to fix as text.

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| Empty or whitespace-only file | A new one-sheet workbook (repair) |
| Leading BOM | Ignored |
| Not JSON, not an object, another `type`, an unknown or newer `version` | Refused |
| A key twice in one object (`JSON.parse` would silently keep only the last), or the key `__proto__` | Refused |
| `sheets`, `cells`, `cols`, `rows` or `named` of the wrong type; a cell key that is not a cell reference (after normalization); two keys for one cell (`a1` and `A1`); a cell that is a list; a named range that is an object or a list | Refused |
| `b2`, `$B$2`, `ab` (a column), `007` (a row) | Normalized to `B2`, `AB`, `7` |
| Missing `version`, `engine` or `sheets`; an empty sheet list | `1`; `""` (recalculate); one empty sheet |
| A number as a sheet name; missing, duplicate or other non-text sheet ids and names | Stored as text; assigned deterministically (`s<n>`, `Sheet<n>`, `Name (2)`), so every device repairs alike |
| A bare value as a cell (`"A1": 5`); a `null` cell or value | `{ "v": 5 }`; dropped (nothing to lose) |
| A formula without `=`; an empty formula | `=` added; dropped |
| Both `v` and `f` | The formula is used; the value is kept aside and written back |
| An unusable `v`, `f`, `w`, `h`, `fmt` or `bold`, or chart `type`, `range`, `title`, `headers` or `labels` | Kept aside verbatim (not shown or calculated) |
| `charts` that is not an object | Kept aside verbatim; no charts shown |
| A chart that is not an object | Refused |
| An unreadable `c` | Dropped: it is derived data, recalculated on the next engine run |
| A named range given as a number or boolean, or with a leading `=` | Stored as expression text |
| Unusable `frozen` values | `0` (a view setting, not data) |

**Typed input** (`input.ts`, `cellInput`): `=…` is a formula; a number (`42`, `-1e3`, `.5`, `007`) is a number; `TRUE`/`FALSE` in any case are booleans; a leading `'` forces text; an empty string clears the cell; percentages (`12.5%`), amounts with a currency symbol (`$1,450.50`, `-€5`), numbers with thousands separators (`1,450`), dates (`2026-10-17`, `10/17/2026`, with an optional time) and times (`14:30`, `2:30 PM`) are numbers that bring the format they imply (`0.0%`, `$#,##0.00`, `#,##0`, `yyyy-mm-dd`, `m/d/yyyy`, `h:mm`, §6.21.8), set on the cell when no format applies to it yet; anything else is text, kept exactly as typed. A cell formatted as plain text (`@`) takes everything as text. Two-digit years 00–29 are 2000–2029 and 30–99 are 1930–1999; dates before 1900 stay text. `cellText` is the inverse for the formula bar: dates and times show as ISO text (`2026-10-17 14:30`), percentages as percentages, and text that would read back as something else with a leading `'`.

#### 6.21.2 Formula engine

- **HyperFormula 3.4.0**, pinned exactly. The roadmap named 3.5.0, but it was published on 2026-10-09, less than the two weeks a new dependency must have been public; 3.4.0 (2026-08-10) is the newest that qualifies, under the same licence. Moving to a later version is a deliberate change of `SHEET_ENGINE` (below), and every sheet recalculates once on its next open.
- **Configuration** (`engineConfig()`): `licenseKey: 'gpl-v3'` (without it every function returns `#LIC!`; `rebuild()` checks the key is valid), its own undo history off, Excel's row and column limits, and ISO dates first among the date formats `DATEVALUE` reads. HyperFormula's defaults already match Google Sheets where it matters: day 0 is 1899-12-30 without the 1900 leap-year bug, text comparison ignores case, criteria accept wildcards. `TRUE` and `FALSE` are defined as named expressions for `TRUE()` and `FALSE()`, because HyperFormula has no boolean literals and `=VLOOKUP(x, A:B, 2, FALSE)` must work; this also keeps users from defining ranges with those names.
- **Engine identity.** `ENGINE_ID` is `hyperformula@<version>`. `SHEET_ENGINE` in `format.ts` (what new files carry, without loading the engine) must equal it; a test fails when an upgrade changes one but not the other, because an upgrade recalculates every sheet. A configuration change that alters results must change the identity too.
- **Text stays text.** Cell text goes to the engine with a leading `'`, so `"007"`, `"=1+2"`, `"TRUE"` or `"2026-10-17"` typed as text are never read as numbers, formulas, booleans or dates.

**`Workbook`** (`Workbook.open(file)`) holds a deep copy of the model and a HyperFormula instance. The model is the source of truth; the engine is a calculator built from it and can always be rebuilt from it.

- **Cached values.** After every change, each formula's result is written to its cell's `c`, and array results to the content-less cells they spill into (stale spill cells are removed). The model is therefore always ready to serialize.
- **Stale caches.** `open()` reports `stale` when the file's `engine` differs from `ENGINE_ID` (another engine, another version, or `""` after a merge), a formula has no `c`, or the spilled cells differ. The values are then already recalculated in memory and `engine` rewritten; the view saves once. A different value alone does not count, so volatile functions (`NOW()`, `RAND()`) never make opening a file dirty.
- **Edits** (`setCell`, `setCells`, `setFormats`, `moveCells`, `insertRows`, `deleteRows`, `insertColumns`, `deleteColumns`, `addSheet`, `renameSheet`, `removeSheet`, `moveSheet`, `setFrozen`, `setColumn`, `setRow`, `addChart`, `setChart`, `setNamed`) validate their input and throw on invalid names or references. A cell's format and unknown keys stay when its content changes or is cleared; a `CellEdit` may set or remove the format with the content (typed `10%`, fill, paste). Renaming a sheet makes formulas and named ranges that refer to it follow the new name (the engine rewrites them; the workbook adopts the engine's text only where it differs in substance, keeping the user's own spelling elsewhere). Removing a sheet turns references to it into `#REF!`; undoing the removal brings them back.
- **Patches.** Every change is a list of slot changes, each with its value before and after: a cell's content (value or formula, format and unknown keys; never its `c`), a sheet's name, panes and unknown keys, the sheet order, a column, a row, a chart, a named range, a top-level unknown key. A cell change that leaves the value and formula alone (a format) never reaches the engine, and the cell keeps its cached value, spilled values included. A patch is applied to the model first; then the engine is reconciled with the model's new state (sheets removed, renamed through temporary names so two sheets can swap names, added; the touched cells and names set). Sheets are added before, and removed after, everything else in a patch, so a patch and its inverse both apply whatever order their changes are in. More than 1000 changed cells, or any failure of the incremental update, rebuilds the engine from the model instead (rows go in as blocks; a 20,000-cell sheet loads in about 0.2 s).
- **Undo and redo** are the workbook's own: a stack of up to 200 patches, inverted to undo. `transact(fn)` makes several edits one step with one notification; a new edit clears the redo stack.
- **Remote changes.** `replace(next, {undoable})` diffs the model against a parsed file and applies the patch. With `undoable` (Take theirs) it is one undo step. Otherwise (another device's change, an automatic merge, §6.8) the history is cleared if any entry touches a changed slot (a change anywhere in a sheet counts as touching that sheet, so undoing its creation could not carry the other device's edits away); an undo can therefore never overwrite another device's edit. After a remote rename the other device's formula text is kept and put back into the engine, because both devices must compute the same file. `replace` also returns `stale`, but a view acts on it only for a change of its own making (its own merge); otherwise two open devices could trade cache-only saves forever.
- **Notifications.** `subscribe()` receives `{cells, layout, origin}`: the changed cells per sheet (content or value; `null` after a rebuild, when anything may have changed), whether sheets, columns, rows, panes or names changed, and whether the change came from an edit, undo, redo or replace.
- **Errors.** `errorMessage(sheet, ref)` gives the engine's explanation of an error result, for a tooltip; `problems` lists named ranges the engine rejected (invalid, or defined twice in different case), which stay in the file untouched.
- **A HyperFormula quirk.** An array formula blocked by a value (`#SPILL!`) does not spill again when the value is cleared. The workbook tracks blocked array formulas and sets them again after each change, so they spill where they now can.

#### 6.21.3 Merging (the descriptor's `merge()`)

`mergeSheetText(base, mine, theirs)` is a 3-way merge over the same slots as the patches. Per slot: equal sides agree; otherwise the side that differs from the base wins; if both differ from the base and from each other, it is a conflict and the function returns `null`, so the conflict banner (§6.10) appears only when the same cell, sheet property, column, row or named range changed differently on both devices.

- **Cells** are compared by content (`v`, `f`, `fmt`, unknown keys), never by `c`: two devices that each changed a different input both recalculated the same totals, which is not a conflict. When both sides changed a cell, its parts merge separately: a value typed on one device and a format set on the other are both kept; two different values (or formats) conflict.
- **Charts** merge per chart id, like columns and rows. Formats and charts are not calculation inputs, so changing only them keeps a side's exact cached values.
- **Sheets.** A sheet added on one side is kept, in its place after the sheet it followed there. A sheet removed on one side is removed if the other side left it alone, and is a conflict if the other side changed anything in it. The sheet order of the side that reordered wins; two different reorders conflict. Two sheets (or named ranges) added on both sides with names differing only in case conflict.
- **Inserted or deleted rows and columns** move cells, which a merge per cell cannot follow (a cell added below an inserted row on the other side would land on the wrong row). Each insertion or deletion changes the sheet's `structure`, and a side whose `structure` changed merges only with a side that changed nothing but cached values; otherwise the banner decides.
- **Renamed sheets.** A sheet renamed on one side (and not on the other) is renamed in the other side's formulas and named ranges, and in the base for comparing, before anything merges: a formula typed on the other device against the old name follows the sheet, as it would have on the device that renamed it, instead of showing `#REF!`; and a formula the rename rewrote merges with the other side's change to it.
- **Cached values.** If the merged calculation inputs (sheet names, cell values and formulas, named ranges) equal one side's, that side's `c` values and `engine` are taken: they are exact. Otherwise the best values at hand are kept and `engine` is set to `""`, so the next engine run recalculates everything and the device that saved the merge writes correct values.
- **Text.** When the merge equals one side, that side's text is returned unchanged (even if it is not in the stable layout); otherwise the merged file is serialized. A side that cannot be parsed gives `null`.

#### 6.21.4 Wiring into the view

- `registerView({id: 'sheet', match: e => e.path.toLowerCase().endsWith('.sheet') ? 6 : 0, textual: true, modes: ['edit'], merge: mergeSheetText, create: {label: 'spreadsheet', extension: 'sheet', initialContent: () => serializeSheet(newSheetFile())}, commands: SHEET_COMMANDS, loadEmbed: () => import('./SheetEmbed'), ...})` in `views/sheet/register.ts`, called from `extensions/builtins.ts`, which also registers the global command "Import spreadsheet from CSV or Excel…" (§6.21.10). Everything it imports is in the main bundle; `load()` brings in the view's chunk and `loadEmbed()` the embed's.
- **The route's subpath.** `/n/Budget.sheet#Summary` opens that sheet, `#Budget!A1:C10` (or a named range) opens its sheet with the range selected and scrolled to (`resolveTarget`, §6.21.7). Links and embeds use it.
- **A binding per session** (`views/sheet/useSheetDocument.ts`, `SheetBinding`) keeps the session and a `Workbook` in step outside React; the view subscribes to its snapshot: `loading`, `failed` (the engine chunk did not load), `refused`, or `ready` with the workbook and a version that changes with every change of it.
  - **Open:** once the session has loaded, `await loadSheetEngine()`, parse the session's text, `Workbook.open()`. Opening writes only for `stale` caches (with `markDirty`, so it is saved like an edit); repairs are written with the next edit, never on open.
  - **Edits** (and the workbook's own undo and redo) call `markDirty(() => workbook.serialize())`, so serialization stays lazy (§6.8).
  - **Text from the session** (§6.8: another device's change, Take theirs, a merge, a restored draft, restored text): when `remoteChangeCount` moves, parse `getText()` and `replace()` it, `undoable` as `remoteChangeUndoable` says. Recalculated caches are saved (`stale`) only when the session holds unsaved text at that moment. That is the case for this device's own merge or restored text, and never for another device's change, so two open devices never trade cache-only saves.
  - **Refused text:** the parser's message is shown and the file is left alone; the view opens it in the text editor to be fixed (§6.21.5). New text from the session (an edit from outside the view, such as a restored text, included, §6.8) is tried again at once; text fixed in the editor is tried on "Open as spreadsheet", so a large file is not parsed per keystroke.
  - Bindings, and with them each workbook's undo history, stay cached for three files that are not shown (as notes keep their `EditorState`, §6.3); older ones are destroyed. `serialize()` needs no engine, so a dirty session can still save the model of a destroyed workbook.
- Conflicts go through `mergeSheetText` in the session (§6.8) and reach the banner only when the same cell changed on both sides. Take theirs is one undo step in the grid.

#### 6.21.5 The grid view

**Grid.** [Glide Data Grid](https://grid.glideapps.com) (MIT): canvas-rendered and virtualized, fast on a phone. It is pinned at **6.0.4-alpha24** (2025-10-08), not the stable 6.0.3 the roadmap named: 6.0.3 declares React 16 to 18 as peers, and the 6.0.4 line has declared React 19 since alpha10; alpha24 is its latest release. Its peer dependencies (lodash, marked, react-responsive-carousel) are installed with it, all MIT. The grid, its CSS and `styles/sheet.css` load with the view's chunk. The lockfile holds the transitive `resolve` at 1.22.12, because 1.22.13 was published less than two weeks before.

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| Header | The inline title (editing it renames the file and keeps `.sheet`; files other than notes are titled without their extension) and the save status |
| Toolbar | Undo, Redo, Bold rows (Bold columns when whole columns are selected), Fill down, Fill right, Freeze (a menu), Column width, Rows and columns (a menu: insert above, below, left, right; delete), Decrease and Increase decimal places, Number format (§6.21.8), Insert chart (§6.21.9), Named ranges, Import CSV or Excel, Download as CSV or Excel (a menu, §6.21.10). 44 px targets on touch devices; it scrolls sideways on a phone. |
| Charts pane | The active sheet's charts, below the grid, scrolling sideways, each with Edit and Delete; it folds away (folded at first on a phone, so the grid keeps its room) and hides with the keyboard (§6.21.9) |
| Formula bar | The name box (the selection: `B3`, `A1:C4`; type a cell, a range, `Sheet!range` or a named range to go there) and the active cell's formula or value. Enter commits and moves down (Shift+Enter up), Tab right (Shift+Tab left), Esc reverts, leaving the field commits; an edit always writes the cell (and sheet) it started on. An error result shows the engine's explanation (`errorMessage`) beside it. |
| Grid | Glide over the active sheet: A1 column letters, row numbers, frozen columns and rows, column widths and row heights from the file, bold rows and columns |
| Sheet tabs | Switch; add (`Sheet<n>`); rename (double-click, F2 or the menu, checked with `sheetNameProblem`); move left or right (menu) or drag (desktop); delete (confirmed, one undo step; formulas that used the sheet show `#REF!`). The active tab's menu button opens its menu, so nothing needs right-click or long-press. |

**Cells.** Every cell is a Glide text cell. `displayData` is the value as shown (`display.ts`: in its number format, §6.21.8, or else the "Automatic" format of Google Sheets, up to 15 significant digits for whole numbers and 10 for fractions, scientific notation from 1E+15 and below 1E-06). `data` is what an edit starts from (`cellText`: the formula, or the value as typed input reads it back, dates as dates). Numbers sit right, booleans and errors in the middle, text left; error results use `--text-error`, and a format's `[Red]`, `[Green]` and `[Blue]` the theme's error, success and link colours. Commits go through typed input (`typedEdit`, §6.21.1), which sets the format a typed value implies on a cell without one.

**Editing.** On desktop, Glide's cell editor opens on a double-click, Enter, F2 or typing (F2 is one of Glide's activation keys, so it never reaches the app's Rename file hotkey). The editor is a lazy chunk of Glide's; the view loads it when an editable grid mounts (through the `glide-overlay-editor` alias in `vite.config.ts`, the same file Glide imports), so the first edit's keys are not lost while it downloads. A press on the grid gives it the keys (Glide focuses itself only when the press selects another cell), as do Add sheet and a click on a tab. On touch devices (`pointer: coarse`) cells are edited in the formula bar, at a 16 px font so iOS does not zoom: a tap selects a cell, a tap on the selected cell focuses the bar. The focus moves in that tap's `touchend`, a user gesture (so iOS opens the keyboard), and its default is prevented, because the mousedown a browser emulates after a tap would otherwise take the focus back. Enter then commits and moves to the next cell with the keyboard still up (`enterkeyhint="next"`). While the keyboard is open on a phone, the title, toolbar and tabs give way to the formula bar and the grid (`.keyboard-open`, §6.16).

**Pointing at cells in a formula** (`referencePicking.ts`), as in Google Sheets and Excel. While a formula is typed, in Glide's editor or in the formula bar, with the caret where a reference can go (after `=`, `(`, `,`, an operator or `:`, outside a string), a click on a cell inserts its reference instead of ending the edit, a drag inserts a range (`B2:B5`), and another click replaces the reference just inserted; the pointed-at range is outlined in the grid. The press is taken at the window before Glide sees it, so the edit, the focus and the selection stay. On touch devices a tap (not a scroll) does the same, and its `touchend` default is prevented so the keyboard stays up. With the caret anywhere else, a click ends the edit as before.

**Frozen rows and columns.** Glide freezes columns, but it has no frozen top rows. Frozen rows are therefore drawn in its sticky header (`drawHeader`), below a band of column letters (26 px, 32 px on touch devices): they stay at the top and scroll sideways with their columns, frozen columns included. The grid's own rows start at the first unfrozen row, and the row numbers continue from there (`rowMarkers.startIndex`). A frozen cell is selected by a click or tap, edited in the formula bar (double-click, a second tap, Enter, F2 or typing), cleared with Delete, and left with the arrow keys, which also lead from the first grid row up into the frozen rows. Two Glide behaviours are filtered out: a press on the frozen band would select the whole column, and Glide selects a cell by itself when it gets the focus without a selection, which is its state while a frozen cell is selected. Frozen rows take at most half the grid's height on the current screen; the others stay in the grid, and the file keeps its value. A range selection does not extend into the frozen rows (see Limits below).

**Selections and keys.** Rectangular ranges; a click on a column letter or a row number selects whole columns or rows. Mod-Z and Mod-Shift-Z (or Mod-Y) undo and redo through the workbook's own history, one step per action (a paste, a fill, a resize). Mod-D and Mod-R fill down and right (with a single row selected, from the row above, as in Google Sheets). Mod-B bolds rows. Delete clears the selection; a cell's formatting and unknown keys stay (§6.21.2).

**Fill.** The fill handle (desktop) and Fill down and right repeat the source block over the target (`edits.ts`). Formulas move with `shiftFormula` (`refs.ts`): relative references (`A1`, `B$2`, `C:C`, `3:3`) shift, absolute parts stay, strings, quoted sheet names, function names and named ranges are untouched, and a reference pushed off the sheet becomes `#REF!`. Values are copied as they are (no series yet).

**Clipboard.** The view handles `copy`, `cut` and `paste` itself while the focus is in the grid and nothing is being edited (Glide's own handling is off), so frozen cells work too. A copy writes the values as shown as TSV (`tsv.ts`: a field with a tab, a line break or a quote is quoted, as Google Sheets, Excel and LibreOffice write it); a copy of whole columns is cut to the used cells. The copy also keeps the cells' contents in memory, so a paste of the same text brings its formulas, shifted to the new place; values an array formula spilled are copied (and filled) as the values they show. Any other text is read cell by cell as typed input. A block is repeated across a selection it divides evenly.

**Cut and paste moves**, as in Google Sheets and Excel. A cut changes nothing yet: the cut cells are outlined with dashes (Glide's `highlightRegions`) until the paste, and Escape drops the cut. Pasted in the same spreadsheet while the cut cells still hold what was cut, they move (`Workbook.moveCells`, one undo step): contents, formats and unknown keys go to the new place (on any sheet) and the old place is emptied where the two do not overlap; references to the moved cells and ranges, in every formula and named range and in the moved formulas themselves, follow them (absolute ones too, `moveReferences` in `refs.ts`); every other reference keeps pointing where it did, so a moved `=SUM(A1:A2)` still sums A1:A2; a chart of a moved range follows it; values an array formula spilled move as values unless their formula moves too. A cut pasted in another spreadsheet, or whose cells changed before the paste, pastes as a copy. Pasting again copies the moved cells from their new place.

**Inserting and deleting rows and columns.** The Rows and columns menu (toolbar), a right-click on a cell, a row number or a column letter (which selects what it is on first, unless it is in the selection), and palette commands insert as many rows or columns as are selected, above, below, left or right, or delete the selected ones (`Workbook.insertRows` and friends, one undo step, as in Google Sheets): the cells after them move with their formats, as do row heights and column widths; references to the sheet in every formula and named range follow (`restructureReferences` in `refs.ts`): ranges that span inserted lines grow, ranges that lose lines shrink, and references whose cells are all deleted become `#REF!`; chart ranges and frozen rows and columns follow too. An insertion that would push a used cell off the sheet is refused.

**Columns and rows.** Dragging a column's edge resizes it, live while dragging, and saves `cols[X].w` on release. On a phone the edge is too small to drag, so the Column width dialog (a number and a slider, or Reset) sets the selected columns. Bold toggles `rows[n].bold`, or `cols[X].bold` for whole columns. The Freeze menu freezes rows or columns up to the active cell, or unfreezes them. Row heights (`rows[n].h`) are shown; editing them comes later.

**Named ranges.** A dialog lists every name with its range or value (editable; a click on the name goes there), defines new ones (offering the selection as an absolute reference, such as `Budget!$B$2:$B$9`) and deletes them. Invalid names, and expressions that do not parse, are refused with the reason (`Workbook.setNamed`); names the engine rejected in the file are listed as problems.

**Size.** The grid shows at least 1,000 rows and 26 columns, and 100 rows and 4 columns beyond the used cells. It grows when it is scrolled to its edge or the name box goes beyond it.

**Theme.** A canvas cannot read `var()`: `sheetTheme()` resolves the theme's CSS variables (§6.17) into Glide's theme, and a `MutationObserver` on `data-theme` reads them again when the theme changes.

**Commands.** The descriptor's `commands` (active while the view shows the open file, §6.8) reach the mounted view through `sheets/activeSheet.ts`: Undo and Redo spreadsheet edit, Fill down, Fill right, Bold, Freeze rows, Freeze columns, Unfreeze, Set column width…, Add sheet, Insert rows above and below, Insert columns left and right, Delete the selected rows and columns, Named ranges…, Number format…, Increase and Decrease decimal places, Insert chart…, Import CSV or Excel into this spreadsheet…, Download sheet as CSV and Download spreadsheet as Excel (.xlsx).

**New spreadsheet.** The descriptor's `create` adds "New spreadsheet here" to the explorer's row menu and "Create new spreadsheet" to the palette. Both create `Untitled.sheet` with one empty sheet through the optimistic new-note flow (§6.15): a title typed meanwhile becomes the file's name, and Enter moves on to the grid (`InlineTitle`'s `onEnter` focuses it inside the key press, renamed or not). `.sheet` files show the `FileSpreadsheet` icon in the tree.

**Files that cannot be edited.** A file the parser refuses opens in the text editor (the `text` view) below the parser's message, untouched, with "Open as spreadsheet" to try again after a fix. A file a newer NotesApp wrote (a higher `version`) is not this version's to fix: its text is shown read-only with the message to update NotesApp. A file the engine fails to calculate is shown like a refused one. A file that is not valid UTF-8 (`lossy`, §4.2) is shown read-only.

**Limits.** Frozen cells are edited in the formula bar rather than in place, and a range selection stops at the frozen rows. Fill copies values and formats (no series). Row heights are shown but cannot be changed yet. Copy and paste need a keyboard or the browser's own clipboard events; there is no Paste button for phones yet. Sorting and filtering a range in place are not built yet (the `SORT`, `SORTBY` and `FILTER` functions are). Formats other than number formats (colours, alignment, borders) and floating charts are not in scope.

#### 6.21.6 Tests and measurements

`a1.test.ts`, `format.test.ts`, `merge.test.ts` and `engine.test.ts` cover the parser's refusals and repairs (every row of the table above), unknown keys round-tripping at every level, the exact serialized layout and its stability, one-cell edits as one-line diffs, typed input; merges of every slot kind, conflicts, cached-value handling and unreadable sides; and an engine round trip with common functions, text that looks like numbers, cross-sheet references with quoted names, named ranges and constants, every error type, spills, engine mismatch and staleness rules, edits, sheet renames and swaps, undo and redo of every change kind, large edits, rebuild after a failed update, and remote replaces with history pruning and undoable Take theirs.

The grid view adds `refs.test.ts`, `tsv.test.ts` and `edits.test.ts` (moving formulas, absolute parts, quoted sheet names, strings, functions and `#REF!`; TSV quoting both ways; fill, clear, copy and paste, own copies and other text, tiling), `gridModel.test.ts` (grid size, widths, frozen rows that fit, cell contents, selections with frozen rows), `useSheetDocument.test.ts` (the binding with the real engine and session over the mock vault: opening, stale saves, lazy serialization, another device's change never saved back, Take theirs as one undo step, a cell-level merge without the banner, refused and fixed files, the cache, an engine chunk that fails to load), `register.test.tsx` (the descriptor, its commands, the theme from CSS variables) and `SheetView.test.tsx`, where Glide is replaced by a stand-in that renders cells and keeps its props: the formula bar, Glide's editor, Delete, undo, fill, copy and paste through clipboard events, frozen cells, column widths, freezing, bold, the name box, sheet tabs, named ranges, live updates, palette commands, refused and read-only files, and the touch flow (tap, `touchend`, Enter to the next cell). `DocumentSession.test.ts` covers views with their own model and semantic merges (§6.8).

6b and 6c add `numfmt.test.ts` (the Automatic format, serial dates, rounding on 15 significant digits, every kind of format code, colours, sections, kinds, decimal places, the presets), `input.test.ts` (percentages, amounts, grouped numbers, dates and times, what stays text, plain-text cells, the formula bar's text reading back), `format.test.ts`, `engine.test.ts` and `merge.test.ts` additions (cell formats and charts in the file, kept-aside values, formats and charts through edits, undo, sheet removal and remote replaces, a format on a spilled value, per-part cell merges, per-chart merges), `edits.test.ts` additions (formats in fill, copy and paste), `target.test.ts` (subpaths, `renameSheetInFormula`), `charts.test.ts`, `csv.test.ts` (quoting, separators, encodings, typed fields), `xlsx.test.ts` (a round trip through SheetJS keeping values, formulas, cached results, formats, spills, widths, heights and names; `_xlfn.`; long sheet names; what an import leaves out; files that are no workbook), `importExport.test.ts` (imports into the open workbook as one undo step with renamed clashing sheets and their formulas, as a new file), `SheetChart.test.tsx` (bars, lines, pies, legends, tooltips, palette order, what cannot be drawn), `SheetEmbed.test.tsx` (with HyperFormula and Glide replaced by modules that fail to load: tables in their formats, sheets, ranges, named ranges, charts, limits, clicks, live updates, and the reading view's placeholder hydrated through `loadEmbed`), and `SheetView.test.tsx` additions (the Number format dialog on cells and whole columns, decimals, a typed percentage, inserting, editing and deleting a chart, importing CSV into a new sheet and a new file, downloads as CSV and .xlsx, opening at the route's sheet or range). On the server, `SheetDocumentIndexerTest` and `SheetIndexingTest` (§6.21.7).

After review: `engine.test.ts` adds names such as `constructor`, a small file with a far-away cell (opened in milliseconds), moving cells, inserting and deleting rows and columns, and implied formats, each checked against a fresh open of the saved file; `merge.test.ts` adds sheet renames against new formulas, the `structure` marker and prototype-named keys; `refs.test.ts` adds `moveReferences`, `restructureReferences` and `renameSheetsInFormula`; `edits.test.ts` spilled values and cuts; `numfmt.test.ts` and `input.test.ts` linear time on long input; `DocumentSession.test.ts` and `useSheetDocument.test.ts` undo after an automatic merge and a merge save refused by history; `referencePicking.test.ts` and `SheetView.test.tsx` cut and paste, rows and columns, pointing at cells (mouse and touch), F2, the title's Enter and the grid's focus.

Bundle: the view's chunk is 361 kB (117 kB gzipped) plus 16 kB of CSS (and Glide's 4 kB cell editor, loaded on the first edit); the embed's 3 kB and the charts' 8 kB; SheetJS's 490 kB (160 kB gzipped) loads only for workbooks. The main bundle grows by the number formats, typed input and subpath resolution (about 12 kB minified).

Measured on the development PC (Node, so a phone is slower) with a 20,000-cell sheet (5,000 rows of values, formulas, text and running totals; 744 KiB): parse 42 ms, open and calculate 185 ms, one edit that changes 5,000 dependent results 19 ms, serialize 26 ms, a 3-way merge 380 ms.

#### 6.21.7 Embeds, links and search (6b)

**Embeds.** The descriptor's `loadEmbed()` (§6.8) is `views/sheet/SheetEmbed.tsx`, which the reading view hydrates like any registered view's embed (§6.7; Live Preview's embeds, ROADMAP 10b, go through the same `loadEmbed`). It reads the file through the `['file', path]` query (refreshed by `file.changed`, so it follows other devices), parses it with `parseSheet` and draws a read-only table of cached values in their number formats, bold rows and columns, column widths and error colours. Neither the engine nor the grid loads (a test replaces both with modules that fail to load).

| Embed | Shows |
|---|---|
| `![[Budget.sheet]]` | The first sheet's used range (from A1 to the last cell that shows something), and its charts below |
| `![[Budget.sheet#Summary]]` | The sheet named `Summary` (any case), and its charts |
| `![[Budget.sheet#Budget!A1:C10]]`, `![[Budget.sheet#B2:D4]]` | A range (without a sheet: on the first sheet) |
| `![[Budget.sheet#Total]]` | A named range |

`resolveTarget` (`target.ts`) tries a sheet name first, then a named range, then a range (`parseRangeText`), so a sheet called `B2` wins over the cell. An embed shows at most 100 rows and 26 columns and says so (`Showing A1:Z100 of A1:Z500. Open the spreadsheet for the rest.`); the table scrolls sideways inside the embed, so a wide sheet never widens the note on a phone. A file that is empty, refused by the parser, or has no such target says so in place. `![[x.sheet|Caption]]` replaces the title. A file without cached values (an import, or a merge, before its first open) shows its formulas' cells empty until it is opened once.

**Clicks.** A click anywhere on the embed opens the spreadsheet at its target (`openPath(path, subpath)`, so `/n/Budget.sheet#Budget!A1:C10`, §6.21.4), unless text is being selected; the title is a real link, so Ctrl or Cmd-click opens a new tab. The embed handles its own clicks and stops them there, so it behaves the same in any host and the reading view never follows the title link a second time.

**Search and backlinks** (server, `sheets/SheetDocumentIndexer`, through the `DocumentIndexer` of §6.8). Each cell's shown value is one block, in row-major order: its text or number, the cached `c` of its formula (the frontend caches every result, so no engine runs on the server), or a value an array formula spilled into it; sheet names and chart titles are blocks too. A `[[Note]]` in a cell, or computed by a formula, is therefore a backlink with the cell's text as its context, and a `#tag` a tag, as in a note; one block per cell keeps markup in one cell (a backtick, `%%`) from reaching into the next. Formulas, error results, number formats and the JSON around the values are not indexed: searching `SUM` or `frozen` finds nothing. Numbers are indexed as the Automatic format writes them (`1937.5`, `1E+21`), not in their number format. Files that are not readable sheets (not JSON, another `type`, a newer `version`) are listed but not content-indexed; an empty file is an empty document. As for every document, a rename of a linked note reports the sheet as `not-rewritable` instead of rewriting its cells, sheets never count as notes, and they are never offered as unlinked mentions.

#### 6.21.8 Number formats (6c)

**Where a format lives.** A cell's `fmt`, else its row's, else its column's (`effectiveFormat`); none is the Automatic format. Format codes are Excel's and Google Sheets' (`numfmt.ts`, no dependency), so they survive an .xlsx round trip:

| Codes | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `0`, `#`, `?` | Digit placeholders: a required digit, an optional one, one padded with a space. Whole parts fill from the right (`000-0000`); fractions drop trailing zeros at `#` |
| `,` | Between digits: thousands separators; after the last digit: divides by 1,000 (`0.0,,"M"`) |
| `.`, `%`, `E+00` | Decimal point; percent (times 100); scientific notation |
| `"text"`, `\x`, `_x`, `*x` | Literal text, an escaped character, a space the width of `x`, a fill (one space) |
| `;` | Sections: positive; negative (written without a sign); zero; text |
| `@` | The text of a text value; as the whole format, plain text: typed input stays text |
| `y`, `m`, `d`, `h`, `s`, `AM/PM`, `[h]`, `.00` | Years, months (`m` after `h` or before `s` is minutes), days and weekdays, hours (12-hour with `AM/PM` or `A/P`), seconds, elapsed time, fractions of a second |
| `General` | The Automatic format, possibly with literals (`General" kg"`) |
| `[Red]`, `[Green]`, `[Blue]`, `[$€-407]` | Colours (the theme's error, success and link colours); a currency symbol with a locale |

Numbers round half up on their 15 significant digits, as spreadsheets show them (`1.005` to two places is `1.01`). Dates are serial days from 1899-12-30 (HyperFormula, Google Sheets, Excel after February 1900); a number before day 0 or after 9999 shows in the Automatic format. Conditions (`[>100]`) are ignored, fractions (`# ?/?`) and other colours are not supported. Booleans and errors ignore formats; text uses a format's text section.

**Setting formats.** The Number format dialog lists Google Sheets' formats (Automatic, Plain text, Number, Percent, Scientific, Financial, Currency, Currency rounded, Date, Date (US), Date (long), Time, Date time, Duration), each with a sample, and a custom format with a live preview. It applies to the selection as one undo step: whole columns or rows get a column or row format (and their cells lose their own, so the new format shows everywhere); other selections, up to 10,000 cells, get a format per cell; Automatic on a cell whose row or column has a format stores `General`, which wins. Decrease and Increase decimal places add or remove a `0` after the point in every section; on Automatic they start from the decimals the value shows. Typed input brings formats (§6.21.1). So does a formula typed or pasted as text into a cell without a format: it gets the format its result implies (`Workbook.impliedFormat`, in the same undo step), the engine's dates, date-times, times, percentages and amounts (`=TODAY()`, `=DATE(…)`, `=EDATE(…)`, `=NOW()`, `=TIME(…)`, `=5%`) in Google Sheets' default formats, or, for plain arithmetic on one cell, that cell's format (`=A1+7` on a date is a date, `=F1*2` on a percentage a percentage; a date times two, or one date minus another, stays a number). As in Excel the format is the cell's from then on, so embeds, search and exports show it without the engine. Fill and pastes of this app's own copies bring the cells' formats, and copies put values on the clipboard as they show (`$1,450.00`), which pastes back as a number with its format.

#### 6.21.9 Charts (6c)

**Model.** A sheet's `charts` (§6.21.1) describe bar (vertical), line and pie charts of a range of the same sheet; they are stored, merged per chart and undone like everything else, and drawn from the cached values, so the grid's pane and embeds draw them without the engine.

**Data** (`charts.ts`). Columns are series and rows categories, as in Google Sheets. With `headers` the first row names the series; with `labels` the first column labels the categories (the slices of a pie); otherwise series are named after their column and categories numbered by row. Cells that hold no number (text, errors, empty) are gaps. Insert chart guesses both options from the range (text above the values, text in the first column), charts the selection, or the sheet's used range when a single cell is selected, and offers a live preview; a range on another sheet is refused. At most eight series (the palette's colours are never reused) and 500 categories are drawn, with a note of what was left out; a pie takes the first series' positive values, the eight largest at most with the rest summed as Other.

**Drawing** (`SheetChart.tsx`, plain SVG, no library). The validated categorical palette of the data-visualization guidelines, in its fixed order, stepped separately for the dark and the light theme (`styles/sheetChart.css`; the light steps that fall below 3:1 against the surface are relieved by the legend, the tooltips and the sheet itself as the table view). Bars are at most 24 px wide with a 4 px rounded data end and a 2 px gap between neighbours, grouped per category; lines are 2 px with ringed 8 px markers (up to 60 categories); pie slices are separated by the surface colour. One value axis with round ticks including zero, compact labels (`1.5K`, `25%` for percent formats) and hairline grid lines; category labels thin out to fit. A legend appears for two or more series and for every pie (with shares); a single series names the chart when it has no title. Every mark has a tooltip with its category, series and value as the sheet shows it. Charts measure their width, so they fit a phone.

**In the view.** A pane below the grid shows the active sheet's charts as cards that scroll sideways (on a phone, one per screen width), each with Edit (the same dialog) and Delete (confirmed, undoable). It folds away, starts folded on a phone, and hides while the keyboard is open. Charts do not float over the grid as in Google Sheets.

#### 6.21.10 Import and export (6c)

| Action | Where | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| Import CSV or Excel | Toolbar, palette (sheet open) | A file picker (`.csv`, `.tsv`, `.txt`, `.xlsx`, `.xlsm`, `.xls`, `.ods`), then a dialog: into new sheets of this spreadsheet (one undo step) or a new spreadsheet file next to it; for CSV the separator (detected) and "Convert text to numbers, dates and formulas" (on: typed input with formats; off: every field is text) |
| Import spreadsheet from CSV or Excel… | Palette, anywhere | The same, always into a new `.sheet` file in the new-note folder, with the defaults |
| Download sheet as CSV | Toolbar menu, palette | The current sheet's used range from A1, values as they show, RFC 4180 (comma, CRLF, quotes doubled), UTF-8 without a BOM; `Budget.csv`, or `Budget - Summary.csv` for a workbook of several sheets |
| Download as Excel (.xlsx) | Toolbar menu, palette | The whole workbook |

**CSV** (`csv.ts`). UTF-8 with or without a BOM, UTF-16 with a BOM, or Windows-1252 (Excel's "CSV" on Windows); the separator that occurs most outside quotes in the first lines (comma, semicolon or tab; `.tsv` is tab). Quoted fields keep separators, quotes and line breaks. A sheet named after the file (made unique) receives the cells.

**Excel** (`xlsx.ts`, a lazy chunk). SheetJS Community Edition **0.20.3** (Apache-2.0), installed from `https://cdn.sheetjs.com/xlsx-0.20.3/xlsx-0.20.3.tgz` as the ROADMAP requires: the lockfile pins the tarball by its `sha512` integrity hash, Dependabot ignores the package, and the npm registry's `xlsx` (frozen at 0.18.x with published advisories) is never used. It contains no `eval` or `new Function`, so the CSP stays as it is (§5.14). A file named `.xlsx` must start like a workbook (a zip or an OLE file), since SheetJS would read any text as a table.

| Survives an import and an export | Not carried over |
|---|---|
| Values, booleans; formulas (Excel's `_xlfn.` and `_xlws.` prefixes removed on import and added on export for newer functions); cached results, errors included (the file's `engine` is `""` after an import, so the first open recalculates and saves once); number formats (a column's format is written on its cells); column widths and row heights; array formulas (written as dynamic arrays over the cells they spill into; imported as a formula whose values spill); workbook-level named ranges | Styles other than number formats (SheetJS CE writes no styles), merged cells (shown unmerged, reported), comments, hyperlinks, frozen panes, charts, sheet-scoped names (reported); a literal error value becomes text |

Excel allows 31 characters in a sheet name: longer names are cut on export (made unique), and formulas and names that refer to them follow (`renameSheetInFormula`); an import into the open workbook renames sheets whose names are taken (`Data (2)`) the same way (all renames at once, `renameSheetsInFormula`, so `Sheet1` → `Sheet1 (2)` and `Sheet1 (2)` → `Sheet1 (3)` never chain), and leaves out named ranges whose names exist, saying so. Functions that HyperFormula has and Excel lacks show `#NAME?` in Excel until recalculated there with an equivalent.

### 6.22 Tasks UI (ROADMAP item 4)

The frontend of structured tasks, in `frontend/src/tasks/`. The contract is API.md §20 and the server's design §5.20. Built (phases 4a and 4b): the list index, the Today, Upcoming, Completed and Filter pages, each list as a list or as a board (Kanban), the task detail sheet with its rule editor, quick add, live updates, the `tasks` file view, `![[List.tasks]]` embeds, **Convert checkbox to task**, and the "Tasks linking to this note" panel. Reminder delivery (phase 4c) comes with item 9: reminders are already stored, edited in the detail sheet and moved with a recurring task, so item 9 adds the scheduler and the notification routes, and nothing here changes.

**Registration** (`tasks/register.ts`, called at the end of `registerBuiltins`). Everything goes through the extension points of §6.8:

| Point | Registration |
|---|---|
| `registerPage` | Menu pages (ribbon, drawer, palette): `tasks/today` (Today's tasks), `tasks/upcoming` (Upcoming tasks), `tasks` (Task lists, the index). `hidden`: `tasks/completed` and `tasks/filter` (each with a palette command of its own, `page.tasks/completed` and `page.tasks/filter`, so the ribbon stays short), and `tasks/list/:id` (one list). Every task page has a row of tabs to the others (`TasksNav`). |
| `registerView` | `tasks`, for `.tasks` files (match 6): `load` the file view, `loadEmbed` the embed; `textual: false`, `modes: ['read']` (below) |
| `registerPanel` | `tasks`, right side: "Tasks linking to this note" |
