| `registerCommand` | `tasks.quickAdd` on `Mod-Alt-T` with `globalHotkey` (it works while writing in a note); on a list page it adds to that list. `tasks.convertCheckbox`, "Convert checkbox to task", offered (its `when`) while the cursor of a note's editor is on an open checkbox |
| `registerOverlay` | `tasks`: the quick-add dialog (eager) and the detail sheet (lazy) |
| `registerEventHandler` | `task.created`, `task.changed`, `task.deleted`, `list.changed` (`TASK_EVENT_NAMES`) |

The pages, the panel, the detail sheet, the file view and the embed are lazy chunks, and so are `chrono-node` (in the quick-add parser chunk) and `rrule` (the `recurrence` chunk: readable rules, presets, the rule editor and the roll-forward of a completion, which the main chunk loads on demand through `loadRecurrence`). The main chunk gets only the store, the sync wiring, the quick-add dialog shell, the convert command and `tasks.css`.

**The task store** (`tasks/store.ts`). One normalized copy of every task and list the page has seen, rather than one cache per view, so a change shows in every view at once (a task completed in the note panel leaves Today too) and an optimistic change has one place to live.

- *Known state.* Responses (`mergeResponse`, `setLists`, `mergeTasks`), SSE events and this tab's write results (`upsert`, `remove`, `upsertLists`, `removeList`) write it. Each event or result stamps the tasks it touched with a generation; a response only writes, and only removes, tasks whose stamp is older than its request, so a GET that started before an event can never undo it (the race the tree also has, §6.3).
- *Views are filters* over the store (`tasks/model.ts`) that mirror the server's rules of API.md §20.2: `inToday`, `inUpcoming`, `inListView`, the status, tag (nested ones too) and priority filters (`statusesOf`, `matchesFilter`), `scheduleComparator` for the order of Today and Upcoming (date with all-day before times, priority, list order, manual order) and `compareCompleted` for the log. A response covers its view's rule (`scopeOf(params, response)`), so a task the store holds that the rule selects but the response lacks is gone on the server (a hand edit, another device) and is removed. A search covers only what it returned; a page of the completed log covers the tasks completed before its `before` and, unless it is the last page, after its last task (others completed at that same instant may be on the next page).
- *Optimistic layers.* A write adds an overlay of field patches (`null` hides a task; a new task is a full placeholder with a `pending-` id) on top of the known state. When the write settles, its result is applied and the overlay dropped; on failure, dropping the overlay is the rollback, and what shows is the server's state as last known, including any event that arrived meanwhile. Overlays stack in order, so dropping an earlier one keeps a later one.
- React subscribes through `useSyncExternalStore`; the merged view is memoized per version.

**Loading** (`tasks/queries.ts`). Pages load views with TanStack queries (`['tasks', 'view', params]`, `['tasks', 'lists']`) whose data is only the response's metadata (total, problems): the tasks go into the store. Events keep the store current, so the queries never go stale on their own (`staleTime: Infinity`); they refetch when invalidated (live updates below, the resync, a failed write). Today loads `view=today&date=<local date>` (the date follows midnight and the page coming back, `useToday`), Upcoming `view=upcoming&date=<local date>&days=14`, a list page (and the file view and the embed) `view=list&list=<id>&status=all`, the index `view=list` (the open tasks of every active list, for the counts), the Filter page `view=list` with its filters, and quick add `GET /api/tasks/lists`. The Completed log is an infinite query (`['tasks', 'completed', filter]`) of pages of 50: subtasks completed with their parent share its `completedAt`, so the next page asks for `before` one millisecond after the last time received (tasks seen twice merge in the store), and a page that cannot move on (more than 50 tasks completed in the same millisecond) ends the log rather than loop.

**Writes** (`tasks/mutations.ts`).

- *Field-level PATCH.* An edit sends only the changed fields, with `base` = the values the user saw: for a text field, its value when the editing began; otherwise the task as shown when the change was made. Fields already as requested are not sent. A conflict is therefore only a real one (the same field changed elsewhere), as on the server (§5.20).
- *One task, one line.* Writes to the same task are sent one after another, so the `base` of a second edit is what the first one wrote, never a value the server has not seen yet.
- *Idempotency.* Each write has one `Idempotency-Key`, reused by its Retry.
- *Failures*, after the rollback: `409 task-conflict` writes the server's task into the store (the field shows the other device's value) and offers **Use mine**, which sends the change again over the server's current value; `404` removes the task (a delete counts it as done); `409 malformed-file` names the file, with **Open file**; a transient failure offers **Retry** in a toast that stays until dismissed, and the write is sent again by itself (same idempotency key) when the connection indicator returns to live (§6.18); anything else is an error toast (§6.20). Every failure other than a conflict also refetches the views shown. Text being edited is never lost to a failure (`ui/textField.ts`): a title or notes draft is let go only once the server confirms it, one save of a field at a time and always based on the value the server last confirmed; a failed save keeps the text in the field, marked unsaved (notes stay a textarea rather than rendered), sends it again on the next blur or pause in typing and when the connection is back, and survives the editor closing until the field is shown again.
- *Completing* closes the task and its open subtasks at once. A recurring task instead moves to its next occurrence at once, computed as the server will (`recurrence.rollForward`, a mirror of `Recurrence.next` and `rollForward` on rrule: from the due date skipping the occurrences already missed, or from the completion day; the other date and the reminders shifted by as many days; `COUNT` counted down; closed subtasks reopened), so a task done on Today leaves Today and shows on its next day in Upcoming before the server answers, and the server's result then replaces the guess. A rule this client cannot evaluate shows no change until the answer; one on its last occurrence closes. The request carries the local date and the `due` and `start` seen, so a doubled request never rolls a recurring task forward twice, and a second tap while the first is sent is ignored (it would otherwise complete the next occurrence). The toast says when the task is next due and offers **Undo**: reopening, or, for a task that rolled forward, a PATCH of its dates, reminders and rule back.
- *Creating* shows a placeholder at the end of its siblings when the list is known (the Inbox by its path, `Tasks/Inbox.tasks`), replaced by the created task.
- *Moving* computes the fractional key between the new neighbours with a port of the server's algorithm (`tasks/fractionalIndex.ts`, checked against the same vectors), so the task lands where it was dropped before the server answers; with unusable neighbour keys (a hand edit) it waits for the server's renumbering.
- *Results* are applied like `file.changed` for their list files: the tree entries, the `['file']` copy, an open session of the list file (`onRemoteChanged`: it adopts the content, or shows the conflict banner when it has unsaved text) and the content queries (backlinks, tags, search).
- *Lists and sections* are not optimistic (the server names files and ids). Renaming a list renames its file through the move service, which may rewrite links to it in other notes; dirty notes are saved first, and since `TaskListWriteResult` does not list those rewrites, the tree is fetched again afterwards and open notes revalidate (§6.3), so none saves over a rewrite. Deleting a list saves and closes an open session of its file first; the file goes to the trash.

**Live updates** (`tasks/sync.ts`, API.md §20.8). Task events go straight into the store. Changes that come without task events (a hand edit, a save of the file as text, a restore from history or the trash, a move, a deleted file) are noticed in the tree: SSE events and this tab's own results both keep `['tree']` current, so a subscription to its cache compares the versions of all `.tasks` files with those the views last saw, and refetches the task views (debounced, one second) when any differs. This tab's own task writes acknowledge their files first, so they cause no refetch; on other devices a task operation arrives both as events and through the refetch, which is harmless. `list.changed` with `origin: null` (the archive rollover) also refetches. After `hello {resync}` and `resync` the event stream invalidates every query, the task views included.

**Pages.**

- *Today*: Overdue, then Today, each in the server's order, with the list (color and name) and, for a subtask, its parent on each row. **Add task** opens quick add with today as the date.
- *A list*: the tasks without a section, then each section (collapsible, with Add task, Rename, Move up and down, and Delete; on the board by section the same moves read left and right, through `POST /api/tasks/lists/{id}/sections/reorder`). Subtasks are indented under their parent, three levels at most (`MAX_DEPTH`), and collapsible. Completed tasks are hidden unless **Show completed** is on; a closed task with an open subtask stays visible so the tree stays whole. A list written by a newer NotesApp (`writable: false`) is shown read-only, with a banner.
  - *Inline editing.* A tap on a task opens an editor in its row: title, notes, due date and time, priority and tags, each saved as its own PATCH (the title on Enter or blur; the notes after 1.5 s without typing, on blur, and when the editor goes away). On a phone the row becomes a grid while it is open, so the fields below the title take the row's whole width.
  - *Reordering.* Dragging the handle (pointer events with `touch-action: none`, so it works with touch) shows a drop line; how far the pointer moved sideways decides the depth, between the depth of the task below and one more than the task above, less the dragged task's own levels of subtasks (`ui/drag.ts`, `project()`). A drop sends `POST /api/tasks/reorder` with the parent, the section and the sibling to follow; the page scrolls near its edges, and Esc cancels. The keyboard and the row menu do the same in single steps: `Alt-↑/↓` among siblings, `Alt-→` into the task above, `Alt-←` out of the parent, and **Move to section**. The row menu (and a card's, on the board by section) also has **Cancel task**, with Undo.
- *Task lists*: Today with its count, then every active list with its open and overdue counts (a read-only list says so), the files that could not be read (`problems`, with links to open them), and the archived lists on request. Each list has Rename, Color (a palette of nine colors that read on both themes, or none; `ui/ListColorDialog.tsx`), Move up and down, Archive, Open list file (its JSON) and Delete.
- *Upcoming* (`ui/UpcomingPage.tsx`): the 14 days from today, each with a heading (Today and Tomorrow named, then the weekday and date), its tasks in the server's order and an add button that opens quick add on that day; days without tasks keep only their heading. Above them, a sticky **week strip** of seven days (this week, or the next with its arrows) shows each day's number of tasks and scrolls to a day when tapped. Overdue tasks are not repeated here; they are on Today.
- *Completed* (`ui/CompletedPage.tsx`): finished and cancelled tasks, archived ones included, newest first, grouped by the local day they were completed (Today, Yesterday, then the date, with the year when it is not this one), page by page (**Show older**), for every list or one. Unchecking a task reopens it, and it leaves the log.
- *Filter* (`ui/FilterPage.tsx`): a list (or every active list), a tag (with the tags of the tasks and of the vault as suggestions; nested tags match), any set of priorities, and open or all tasks. The filter is the page's address (`/x/tasks/filter?tag=home&priority=2,3`), so it can be bookmarked, or linked from a note as a Markdown link. Results are grouped by list, each in schedule order.
- *A list as a board* (Kanban): a **List | Board** switch in a list's header, remembered per list on the device (`ui/layoutPref.ts`, local storage only; the default is the list), and **By section | By status** for the columns. Below.

**The board** (`board/`, ROADMAP items 4 and 15). Built in two layers, so item 15's boards reuse the first:

- `board/Board.tsx` is generic: columns (`id`, `title`, `items`, header actions, a footer, an accent color, `locked` for a column that takes no drops) of cards the caller renders, and one callback, `onMove({itemKey, from, to, index})`, where `index` is the card's place in the target column counted without the card itself. It does the layout, dragging and keyboard moves only. The geometry is pure (`board/boardDrag.ts`: the column under the pointer, else the nearest one that takes drops; the index from the cards' middles; a drop where the card already is; `Alt-↑/↓` within a column and `Alt-←/→` to the next column that takes drops, at the same height). Dragging uses pointer events, as the list page does: the card's handle starts a drag with any pointer (it has `touch-action: none`, so a finger drags the card instead of scrolling the board), and a mouse can also drag the card itself once it has moved 5 px (a click still opens it, and the click that ends a drag is swallowed). The card follows the pointer, a line shows where it lands, the board scrolls sideways near its edges and the workspace up and down near its own, and Esc cancels. Columns are measured on every move, so scrolling while dragging stays exact.
- `board/taskBoard.ts` turns a list into columns and a move into a write, purely. *By section*: tasks without a section first (only when there are some, or the list has no sections), then each section in order; the cards are the top-level tasks in manual order, closed ones only with **Show completed** (or while they have an open subtask), and a card shows its subtasks as "2/5". A move between or within section columns places the task after the card it now follows (`POST /api/tasks/reorder` with the section, as the list page's moves). *By status*: To do in the list's own order, section by section, then Done and Cancelled by completion time, newest first (Cancelled shown even when empty, as the place to drop a task to cancel it). A move into another column sets the status: Done through `complete` (so a recurring task rolls forward and stays in To do, and subtasks follow), To do through `uncomplete`, Cancelled through a PATCH (with Undo; a recurring task stops repeating, and subtasks stay as they are); within To do the task takes the section of the card it now follows (or precedes, at the top); within Done and Cancelled, ordered by time, a move changes nothing.
- `ui/TaskBoard.tsx` renders the cards (check circle, title with links and tags, the meta line, a menu with Details, **Move to "Section"** or **Mark as …** for each other column, and Delete) and the section actions (add, rename, move left and right, delete; an **Add section** column at the end). Every write is the list page's, so it is optimistic, ordered per task, and undoable where the list's is.

**The file view and embeds** (`ui/TaskFileView.tsx`, `ui/TaskListEmbed.tsx`, the `tasks` view of `registerView`).

- *Opening a `.tasks` file* shows its list, as its page does (`ui/ListScreen.tsx` serves both, by list id), with a **Source** button for the JSON: the `?view=source` address shows the text view of §6.8 with its conflict banner, so the file keeps its autosave, history and conflict handling, and **Show as list** goes back. Open list file in the menus opens the source directly. A file that is not a usable list (malformed, a copy that repeats a list id, an archive file, or one not indexed yet) opens as its source with the reason. The view is `textual: false` with a single mode, so the shell adds no conflict banner or mode toggle of its own while the list shows; the source brings its own.
- *Links to a task.* `[[Inbox.tasks#^<task id>]]` (an Obsidian block reference to the list file; API.md §20.9) opens the file view, which opens that task's details, highlights its row and scrolls to it (a closed one is shown even with completed tasks hidden). The details are fetched by id, so the link still works after the task moved to another list.
- *Embeds.* `![[Home.tasks]]` in a note is hydrated by the reading view like any registered view's embed (§6.7 #5): `loadEmbed` mounts the embed in a React root of its own with the query client and no router, which is also how Live Preview's embed widgets will mount it (ROADMAP item 10b; Live Preview on its own branch renders no embeds yet). It shows the list's open tasks with their section headings and subtasks, live (it reads the same store, so a change from anywhere shows at once), **Show completed**, and an **Add task** field at the bottom. Checking a task completes it through `complete`, exactly as on the list page (recurrence, subtasks, Undo); a title opens the details; the heading opens the list. `![[Home.tasks#Errands]]` shows one section (and adds to it), `![[Home.tasks#^<id>]]` one task with its subtasks (and adds subtasks). The embed stops click propagation for its own links, so the reading view's delegated handlers never see them, and renders only task titles, never task notes, so embeds cannot nest. A file that cannot be read says why.

**Convert checkbox to task** (`tasks/convert.ts`). The command takes the line under the cursor of the active note's editor, when it is an open checkbox (`- [ ] text`, any list marker, also inside quotes and callouts), and:

1. reads the Obsidian Tasks plugin's emoji fields out of the text (📅 due; ⏳ scheduled, else 🛫 start, as `start`; 🔺⏫ high, 🔼 medium, 🔽⏬ low; 🔁 a rule in words, read by rrule's text parser; ➕ ✅ ❌ dates dropped), so a vault that used that plugin keeps its dates; a rule that cannot be read stays in the title, so nothing written is lost;
2. starts following the line (a tracked range, §6.8), then creates the task with `POST /api/tasks` without a list, so the server puts it in the Inbox and creates `Tasks/Inbox.tasks` when it does not exist (API.md §20.4);
3. once the task exists, replaces the checkbox and its text, after the list marker, with a link to the task in the vault's link style (`[[Inbox.tasks#^<id>|Title]]`, or a Markdown link; the title without link syntax as its text), but only if the line is still exactly the text it was (it may have moved: lines added above or below, here or on another device). Otherwise the note is left alone and the toast says the task was added and the line kept.

The task is created before the note changes, so a failed request (offline, refused) leaves the line as it was, and a tab closed in between leaves at worst a task and its line both present, never neither. The edit is an ordinary local edit (undo, autosave). The toast offers **Open**.

**The detail sheet** (`ui/TaskDetail.tsx`, a `Modal` of the `sheet` variant: full screen on phones). Title; status (To do, Done, Cancelled: Done completes as the check does, Cancelled closes the task for good); due and start dates with optional times; priority; repeat (below); reminders (stored only, with a note that delivery comes with item 9); tags; related notes (a fuzzy note search); list and section; notes; subtasks (open one, or add one by its title); the dates and Delete. The notes are shown rendered by the reading-view pipeline (`renderNote`, so DOMPurify sanitizes them, §6.7), with links resolved from the list's file, and are edited as Markdown text. A task the store does not know is fetched (`GET /api/tasks/{id}`); one that no longer exists says so. Archived tasks and read-only lists show every field disabled (an archived task can still be reopened).

**Recurrence** (`ui/RepeatField.tsx`, `tasks/recurrence.ts`, ROADMAP item 4 "Recurrence"). Rules read as text through rrule's `toText()` everywhere a task shows (the meta line of rows and cards, the menu, the editor), with two corrections to its English: `COUNT`, which here is the number of occurrences left (API.md §20.6), reads "3 more times", and `BYMONTHDAY=-1` "on the last day". The Repeat menu has presets that follow the task's date (every day, every weekday, weekly, every two weeks, monthly and yearly on its day), the current rule when it is none of them, and **Custom…**, which opens the rule editor; **from the due date** or **from completion** is a second menu. The editor has three ways to the same rule, and switching carries the rule over:

- *Choose*: every N days, weeks (on chosen weekdays), months or years (in a chosen month), monthly and yearly on a day of the month (or the last) or on the first to fourth or last weekday ("the second Tuesday", `BYDAY=2TU`), ending never, after N times (`COUNT`) or on a date (`UNTIL`). `draftFromRule` reads a stored rule back into these controls, from the task's date for the parts it leaves out; a rule it cannot show (BYYEARDAY, BYWEEKNO, times, several months) opens as an RRULE instead, so nothing is silently simplified.
- *In words*: rrule's text parser, as quick add uses it ("every 2 weeks on monday and friday").
- *RRULE*: the raw value, checked as the server will check it (`validateRule`: the parts of the subset, numbers, `COUNT` or `UNTIL`, and at least one occurrence), with the server's message when it is refused.

The rule's text shows under the controls as you change them, and below the field the next three dates show from the task's date ("Then Oct 26, Oct 29, Nov 2").

**Quick add** (`quickadd/`). The dialog is part of the main chunk, so its field takes focus inside the key press or the tap on the floating "+" that opened it (iOS opens the keyboard only then); the parser (`quickadd/parse.ts`, with chrono-node and rrule) loads as the dialog opens, or earlier when a task page opens. Parsing, as you type:

1. `[[links]]` are found first; nothing inside them is read as anything else, and they stay in the title, where they are backlinks (API.md §20.9).
2. `!high`, `!med` (or `!medium`), `!low` and `!!!`, as whole words, set the priority.
3. `#tags` (the note rules, API.md §15.6) become `tags` and leave the title.
4. `@List` takes the longest active list name that follows, case-insensitive (so `@Home Renovation` works); an unknown name is one word and becomes a **new list**, created first on save. A subtask ignores it: it stays in its parent's list.
5. `every …` is a recurrence: the phrase grows word by word while rrule's `parseText` keeps adding to the rule (it ignores trailing words, so "every month tomorrow" must not swallow "tomorrow"), and stops at the syntax above. Only `DAILY` to `YEARLY` are kept; the rule is stored without `RRULE:` and with `FREQ` first.
6. Dates and times come from chrono-node's casual English parser, run on the text with the parts above blanked out, forward-dated: a time that is certain gives `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm`, otherwise the date only. A preposition left dangling before it ("by", "on", "at", "due", "before") leaves the title too.
7. A recurrence without a date gets its first occurrence from today as its due date (shown as "(first)").

Each part shows as a chip, and its × keeps the part in the title as plain text (the dismissal is remembered for that text). Context chips come from where quick add was opened: the list and section of a list page, today on the Today page, the note of the note panel (as `links`), or the parent task. The dialog closes at once and the task appears optimistically; when the server refuses it (other than being offline, which offers Retry), quick add opens again with the typed text, so nothing typed is lost.

**The note panel** (`ui/TasksPanel.tsx`). The lists to load come from the note's linked backlinks (a list file is a backlink source, API.md §20.9) and from the tasks already in the store, so a note that no task links to costs no task request. A task is shown when its `links` hold the note, or a `[[link]]` or Markdown link in its title or notes resolves to it from the list's file (the client resolver, §6.6). Open tasks come first, completed ones on request; **Add task for this note** opens quick add with the note linked.

**Phone and themes.** The task pages use the page layout of §6.16. On touch devices every control is at least 44 px; the drag handle, the check circle and the menu button take the row's height, and the subtask toggle moves to the right of the title, so the check circles of siblings stay aligned without a spacer column. Phones get a floating "+" for quick add instead of the header's Add task (hidden while the keyboard or the editing toolbar is up); dialogs are full-screen sheets. Colors are the theme tokens of §6.17 (priority: low `--tok-function`, medium `--text-warning`, high `--text-error`); a list's own color is used for its dot only, as `#rrggbb` or a color name; a closed task's date is shown faint, never as overdue. List icons are not shown yet: lucide's dynamic icon loader would add a chunk per icon. The task pages' tabs scroll sideways on a phone. On the board, a phone shows one column at a time (85% of the width, snapping, so the next one peeks in) and the card handles are 44 px; the board takes the whole width of the workspace on a desktop. The Upcoming week strip stays at the top while the days scroll. Embeds use the interface's small text size inside a note, and 44 px controls on touch.

**Tests.** `fractionalIndex.test.ts` (the shared vectors), `model.test.ts` and `model.views.test.ts` (view rules, Upcoming, the filters, the completed order, tags, links, rows), `store.test.ts` (reconciliation, the response race, layers and rollback), `mutations.test.ts` (optimistic writes, rollback, conflicts and Use mine, one task in order, Retry and the resend on reconnect, completion with Undo that puts the subtasks back (and a roll-forward's Undo), the optimistic roll-forward and the ignored second tap, create (also into an Inbox the server made, or a list the page does not know), delete, move), `recurrence.test.ts` (toText, presets, the server's subset, the editor's draft round-trips, the roll-forward against API.md §20.6's examples, the preview), `sync.test.ts` (events, the tree watcher), `queries.test.ts` (scopes, the completed log's paging), `board/boardDrag.test.ts` and `board/taskBoard.test.ts` (geometry, keyboard moves, columns, what a move means), `convert.test.ts` (checkbox lines, emoji fields, link text, the line followed through local and remote edits, a line changed meanwhile, a failed create) with `editor/tracked.test.ts`, `quickadd/parse.test.ts` and `QuickAdd.test.tsx`, `ui/drag.test.ts`, and component tests of the pages (Upcoming, Completed, Filter, the board in `ListPage.test.tsx`), the file view, the embed (hydrated through the view registry, as a note shows it), the panel, the detail sheet with the rule editor, the status and drafts kept through a failed save, cancelling from the row menu and the status board, section moves, the list color, a subtask's parent fetched on Today, and the registration.

### 6.23 Bookmarks and recent files (ROADMAP item 12)

**Bookmarks** sync through the vault (§5.21, API.md §21); **recent files** stay on each device.

- **State** (`features/bookmarkStore.ts`). The query `['bookmarks']` holds the server's list, read through the page's own `QueryClient` (so a star can show anywhere, provider or not), and `bookmarkWriter` lays this tab's unsaved edits over it: `useBookmarks()` and `currentBookmarks()` return that view. The list is prefetched when live sync starts, so commands and menus know what is bookmarked.
- **Saving** (`BookmarkWriter` in `features/bookmarks.ts`). Every change is an *edit*, a function from the current list to the next. It shows at once; one `PUT` is in flight at a time, carrying every edit made so far on top of the cached list and its version; edits made meanwhile go in the next `PUT`. A `409` (another device, or the server's re-keying) refetches the list and applies the same edits again, up to four times, so two devices bookmarking different notes both keep theirs. Any other failure drops the edits (the view falls back to the server's list) and shows an error toast. The edits a response covers are removed before the response is cached, so the view never applies them twice.
- **Live sync.** `bookmarks.changed` invalidates `['bookmarks']`. After a move or a restore the server sends it with `origin: null` (§5.21), which is how the moving tab learns that its bookmarks were re-keyed.
- **Entry points.**

  | Where | What |
  |---|---|
  | Note header (desktop and tablet) | A star toggles the open file's bookmark |
  | TopBar ⋯ (phone) | **Bookmark** / **Remove bookmark** |
  | Explorer row menu | **Bookmark** / **Remove bookmark** for a file or folder |
  | Outline panel | A star per heading, shown on hover and always on touch (`subpath: "#Heading"`) |
  | Search panel and phone search page | A star beside the result count bookmarks the query |
  | Bookmarks panel toolbar | Bookmark the current file, New group, Collapse all groups |
  | Palette | The `bookmarks.*` and `view.bookmarks` commands (§6.13); no default hotkeys, as in Obsidian |
  | Drag and drop (desktop) | An explorer row dropped on the Bookmarks panel is bookmarked where it lands |

- **Bookmarks panel** (left sidebar; a drawer tab on phones). Rows follow the explorer's look: an icon by kind (the file's view icon, a heading, a block, a folder, a search), the title or the target's name, and a faint detail (the folder, or the note of a heading). Groups have a chevron and collapse; collapsed groups are remembered per device (`localStorage notesapp:bookmarkGroupsCollapsed`, keyed by `ctime` and title, because Obsidian's groups have no ids). File and folder targets resolve ignoring case, as links do; a target that is not in the tree is shown **missing** (faint, struck through) and opening it offers **Remove bookmark** in a toast; it lights up again when the target comes back (a trash restore). Opening a file goes to the file (at its heading), a folder reveals it in the explorer, a search opens the search panel (phones: the search page) with the query, and an Obsidian `url` bookmark opens in a new tab. The row menu (right-click; long-press or ⋯ on touch) has **Open**, **Rename…** (a display title; empty gives the target's name back), **Move up** / **Move down**, **Move out of the group**, **Move to “group”**, and **Remove bookmark** (a group: **Remove group**, which keeps its bookmarks in its place). On desktop, rows drag to reorder (a drop on a group moves into it). Keyboard: arrows, Enter, ←/→ on groups, F2 renames, Delete removes, the ContextMenu key opens the menu.
- **Recent files** (`features/recent.ts`, `localStorage notesapp:recentFiles`): up to 50 `{path, at}` entries, newest first, recorded when a file view opens. Renames and moves re-key them (own `MoveResult`s and `entry.moved`), deletes through the app drop them, and entries whose file is gone are hidden. The first format (`notesapp:recent`, paths only) is moved over once. Other tabs follow through the `storage` event. The **Recent files panel** (left sidebar; a drawer tab on phones) lists them with the time each was opened, a star on bookmarked ones, and a row menu with **Open**, **Bookmark** / **Remove bookmark** and **Remove from recent files**; its toolbar clears the list (after a confirmation; no file is touched). The quick switcher's empty query shows the same list (§6.13), and `[[` completion ranks recent notes first.

### 6.24 Updating heading links after a rename (ROADMAP item 21)

- **Offer** (`views/markdown/useHeadingRenameOffers.ts`, edit mode only). When the text has rested for 1.5 s, the outline (`headingsFromState`) is compared with the outline the last decision was made on. `detectHeadingRename` (`features/headingRename.ts`) reports a rename only when both have the same headings at the same levels and exactly one heading's text changed beyond case and spacing; anything else (a heading added or removed, several edited, or any change that came from the server, which `DocumentSession.getRemoteChangeCount()` counts) just becomes the new baseline. For a rename the note is saved and a **dry run** asks what would change; when links or heading bookmarks point at the old text, a toast (20 s) reads "Heading renamed to “New”. Update 3 links in 2 notes that pointed at “Old”?" with **Update links**. Typing on in the heading replaces the offer with a new one; a rename nothing points at is accepted silently.
- **On purpose:** the palette command **Rename heading and update links to it…** (`editor.renameHeading`) asks for the new text of the heading at or above the cursor, edits it in place (keeping the `#` marks and any closing `#`s, or the setext underline) as one undoable change, and updates the links.
- **Applying.** The note is flushed, then `POST /api/notes/rename-heading`; the result is applied like a move's `linkUpdates` (`applyHeadingRenameResult`: tree versions, `['file']` queries, open sessions refetch quietly), heading bookmarks arrive through `bookmarks.changed`, and a toast sums it up ("Updated 3 links in 2 notes and 1 bookmark to “New”"; notes that could not be updated are listed).

### 6.25 Customizable hotkeys (ROADMAP item 29)

Every command's key can be changed per device: a laptop and a desktop keyboard differ, and the phone has none.

- **Storage** (`features/hotkeyPrefs.ts`): `localStorage notesapp:hotkeys`, a map from command id to a key spec (`Mod-Shift-b`) or `null` (no key). A command without an entry keeps its default: its `hotkey`, or the `editorHotkey` the editor binds. Binding a command to its default removes the entry. Other tabs follow through the `storage` event; `useHotkeyOverrides()` re-renders the palette, the ribbon tooltips and the note header's tooltips.
- **Global handler** (`features/hotkeys.ts`). A key runs the available command whose key on this device it is. **Always-global** commands (switcher, palette, search, settings, back, forward; by id, so they stay global on a new key), commands registered with `globalHotkey` (quick add, §6.22) and **every key chosen on this device** run in the capture phase, before the focused widget, so they work in the editor and in text fields too (not in modal dialogs, except the always-global and `globalHotkey` ones). In a text field, a character typed with AltGr (Ctrl+Alt off Apple platforms) is never taken for a key (§6.14). Default keys of other commands run in the bubble phase outside text fields, as before. A command whose default key the editor binds (`editorHotkey`: bold, italic, link, checkbox, follow link, find) is left to the editor until this device gives it another key.
- **Editor keys step aside.** The editor's keymap wraps each default (`Mod-B`, `Mod-I`, `Mod-K`, `Mod-L`, `Mod-E`, `Mod-S`, `Mod-Enter`, `Mod-F`, `Mod-Alt-←/→`) in `ifDefault(commandId, …)`, which does nothing once the command was given another key, so the old key really is free.
- **Settings, "Hotkeys"** (`components/pages/HotkeysSection.tsx`, route `/settings/hotkeys`): every command, sorted by name, with its key (a customized one is outlined, with its default named), a filter by name or key, and per row **Change** (the next key combination is recorded; `Esc` cancels; the global handler ignores keys aimed at the recorder), **Remove** and **Reset**, plus **Reset all**. A key needs `Mod`, `Alt` or a function key. A key another command uses shows "Ctrl+Alt+1 is used by “…”" with **Use it here instead** (that command loses its key) and **Cancel**. Keys the browser keeps (`Mod-N`, `Mod-T`, `Mod-W`, `Mod-Shift-N/T/W`, `Mod-Q`, `Mod-R`) and editing keys (`Mod-Z`, `Mod-Y`, `Mod-Shift-Z`, `Mod-C`, `Mod-V`, `Mod-X`, `Mod-A`) are refused with the reason.
- The editor's own built-in keys (undo, indent, completion, search panel navigation) are not commands and keep their keys.

### 6.26 Installable app and offline mode (ROADMAP item 8, 2026-10-21)

The app installs to the iPhone Home Screen (Safari, Share › Add to Home Screen) and as a desktop app (Chrome or Edge), opens without a connection, shows recently opened notes, and lets them be edited offline. Edits sync through the ordinary version check when the server is reachable again, so an offline edit can produce a conflict banner but never an overwrite.

**Manifest and icons.** `public/manifest.webmanifest`: `display: standalone`, `start_url` and `scope` `/`, dark `theme_color`/`background_color`, icons `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, a maskable `icon-maskable-512.png` (glyph inside the 80 % safe zone) and `favicon.svg`; all are rasterized from `favicon.svg`'s geometry. `index.html` links the manifest and keeps the iOS tags (`apple-mobile-web-app-capable`, `black-translucent` status bar, `apple-mobile-web-app-title`, `apple-touch-icon.png`). The status bar overlaps the page, so every top-level surface pads by `env(safe-area-inset-*)` (§6.16); the desktop layout does too, for an installed iPad.

**Service worker: hand-written** (`src/sw/sw.ts`, decisions as pure functions in `src/sw/core.ts`), built after the app by `vite-plugin-sw.ts`, which lists the build output and compiles the precache list and a version (a hash of the build SHA and every precached file) into one classic script, `dist/sw.js`. ROADMAP item 8 named vite-plugin-pwa 2.0.0; it was not used because the worker's update flow is tied to the app's own build check and unsaved-edit state, which neither of its registration modes fits (`autoUpdate` reloads pages on `controllerchange`, dirty or not; `prompt` duplicates the "new version" banner), and its Workbox build tooling adds a second bundler (Rollup, Babel) to a Vite 8/Rolldown build for about 200 lines of worker.

| Request | Answer |
|---|---|
| Navigation to a client route (the `SpaController` list, plus `/capture`) | Network first. The cached `index.html` when the network fails, takes more than 4 s, or answers 502/503/504 (serve during a deploy). |
| Other navigations (a raw file opened in a tab, downloads) | Not touched |
| A precached file (exact URL, no query) | From the shell cache, else the network |
| `/api/files/raw?…&v=` as an image (content-addressed, no `Range`) | Cache first in `notesapp-media`, at most 300 entries; off when the device's offline copies are off |
| `POST /capture/share` navigation (share target, §6.27) | Kept in Cache Storage; `303` to `/capture?share=<id>` |
| Everything else, the whole API included | Not touched: API responses stay `no-store` and never come from a cache |

- **Install** precaches every built file except source maps, `robots.txt` and Excalidraw's fonts: the shell and every lazy chunk, CodeMirror's language modes included, so nothing the app may load is missing offline. With drawings, spreadsheets, tasks and rich Markdown merged that is about 560 files and 16 MB (about 5 MB compressed; it was 165 files and 2.6 MB on its own branch), downloaded once per device and then only as files change. Excalidraw's self-hosted fonts (§6.8.1; about 230 files and 12 MB, mostly CJK subsets loaded for the characters a drawing shows) are left out (`isExcalidrawFont`): the browser's HTTP cache keeps the ones a drawing used (immutable, versioned path), and a drawing offline falls back to a system font for characters never shown before. Hashed bundles still in the previous shell cache are copied, not downloaded again. Any failure fails the install and the previous worker stays in charge. Then `skipWaiting()`.
- **Activate** deletes older `notesapp-shell-*` caches and claims the open pages.
- Registration (`src/pwa/serviceWorker.ts`) only in production builds, with `updateViaCache: 'none'`; the server sends `sw.js` with `no-cache` and `Service-Worker-Allowed: /`, and the manifest as `application/manifest+json` with `no-cache` (§5.14). An installed app can stay open for weeks without navigating, so it also checks for a new worker when it comes back to the foreground, at most hourly. A development page unregisters any worker a preview left behind.

**Deploys** (with §6.18). A page that sees a new `buildSha` first asks for the new worker and waits up to 10 s for it to take over (`prepareReload`), then reloads; navigations are network-first anyway, so the reload gets the new `index.html` even while the old worker is active. If the page still comes back on the old bundle (the network was too slow and the cached shell answered), it does not reload again for the same server build within a minute: it shows the "new version" banner, whose Reload does the same hand-over, so a reload can never loop. An old tab whose lazy chunk is gone takes the existing `vite:preloadError` path. Verified with a deploy simulated under a running page: it reloaded once onto the new bundle and the new worker, and the old shell cache was gone.

**Offline reading** (`src/offline/`, IndexedDB database `notesapp-offline`, separate from the drafts database so neither schema moves with the other).

- `notes`: a copy of each note opened on this device (as read, and as saved by this tab), at most 100 notes and about 20 MB of text, least recently opened first out. While the server is reachable, copies follow the tree: deleted notes' copies go, moved ones follow the client move map, outdated ones are re-read.
- `meta.tree`: the last tree from the server (written 15 s after changes, and when the page is hidden).
- A session opens its note through `loadFile` (`offlineStore.openNote`): the server, or the copy when the request fails transiently. **The copy's version stays the session's base**, so an edit made offline is saved with it (API.md §4): equal on the server, it saves; changed there, the 409 is the ordinary conflict banner. A note without a copy shows "Can't reach the server, and this device has no offline copy of this note".
- At startup, when `GET /api/tree` has not answered after 2.5 s (at once when the browser says it is offline), the snapshot stands in, with a banner saying from when. The event stream then starts without an id, so its `hello` asks for a resync, which fetches the real tree and revalidates every open note.
- Once the stream has caught up, sessions opened from a copy revalidate against the tree (a stale copy, clean, catches up silently).
- Settings › This device: **Keep recent notes for offline reading** (on by default; it also controls the image cache). Off removes the copies; drafts and queued captures are unsaved data and stay. This is the device-level answer to §5.14's wish that note text not persist in the disk cache of shared computers.

**Offline editing: the save queue is the drafts.** A draft (§6.9) already holds the text and the version it was based on, in IndexedDB, and survives the page. An open tab retries its own sessions' saves once the stream has caught up (§6.3). iOS kills an installed app in the background often, though, leaving its edits only in drafts that no live tab owns. When the stream catches up, `offline/draftReplay.ts` takes the newest such draft per note in the tree (one tab at a time, Web Locks):

| Draft | Action |
|---|---|
| Based on the note's current version, no pending Keep mine | `PUT {content, baseVersion: draft's}`; 200 removes it (older drafts of the note go to the discarded store) |
| Based on an older version, or a Keep mine was pending | Nothing is written. If the server's text equals the draft's, the draft is dropped; otherwise the note is listed in a **Review** banner. Opening it restores the draft into the ordinary conflict banner (Keep mine, Take theirs, Compare) |
| `PUT` answered 409 (changed between the tree and the save) | Listed for review, the draft stays |
| Transient failure | Stops; the next catch-up tries again |

Sessions of this tab in conflict but not on screen are listed in the same banner. Verified in the browser: edits made with the server stopped synced on restart; an edit to a note changed on disk meanwhile came back as the conflict banner, with the other version committed to history; a closed tab's edit was replayed by the next page load.

**Indicators.** The offline banner (§6.18) also counts waiting captures; the snapshot banner; the Review banner; the save pill shows Offline on each note with unsaved edits.

### 6.27 Quick capture (ROADMAP item 7, 2026-10-21)

Capture a thought, a link or a photo into `Inbox.md` (vault root) or today's daily note, in one tap on the phone and one key on the desktop, with or without a connection. The server's part is API.md §8.5–8.6 (`POST /api/capture`, `POST /api/capture/file`): it appends one list item with the device's time and creates the note when needed.

- **Entry points.** The command `capture.quick` ("Quick capture", `Mod-Shift-Space`, registered with `globalHotkey` like quick add, §6.8, so it works inside the editor and text fields), the ribbon's inbox button, the drawer's **Capture** button, and a **+** in the phone's TopBar. All open the eager capture dialog, whose text field takes the focus inside the opening tap (§6.13). The same form is the `/capture` page, rendered outside the shell: the share target, the manifest's "Quick capture" shortcut, links with `?text=&url=&title=&target=`, and the desktop app's capture window (ROADMAP item 41, which registers `Ctrl+Shift+Space` system-wide).
- **The form.** Text, **Inbox / Today** (remembered per device), a shared link shown as a removable chip, photos (one entry each, the text as the first one's caption), Save (`Mod-Enter`). "Today" is decided on the device: the request carries the local date, time and IANA zone.
- **Offline queue** (`offline/captureQueue.ts`, store `captures` in `notesapp-offline`). Every capture is stored first, files included, then sent, so one made offline, or whose tab dies mid-request, is not lost. Each keeps the `Idempotency-Key`, date and time it was made with; the queue is sent in order when the stream catches up, when the browser goes online and at startup (one tab at a time). A transient failure leaves the rest waiting; a refusal (for example `too-large`) is kept aside and listed in Settings › Quick capture with Copy, Try again and Discard. A refusal of the capture just submitted stays in the form with its text instead.
- **The capturing tab's own note.** The server appended, so the response carries no text, and the tab's own events are not echoed (API.md §16.4). Before sending, the queue flushes dirty sessions (at most 3 s), so the target note is clean here; `applyCaptureResult` then treats the response like another device's change: the session re-reads the note quietly.
- **Web Share Target** (Android, and installed desktop Chrome or Edge). The manifest's `share_target` posts `title`, `text`, `url` and image `files` (multipart) to `/capture/share`. The service worker answers that navigation itself: it keeps the share in the Cache Storage cache `notesapp-share` and redirects to `/capture?share=<id>`, which pre-fills the form (a link found inside the text becomes the URL; a title that only repeats the text or the link is dropped) and forgets the share once captured. Nothing is posted to the server, so the CSRF rule is never bypassed; without a worker the server answers that post with `303` to `/capture?share=lost`.
- **iPhone Share Sheet.** iOS Safari does not support `share_target` for Home Screen apps, so an iOS Shortcut ("Add to Notes", Show in Share Sheet) calls `POST /api/capture` over the tailnet with the header `X-Client-Id: ios-shortcut-capture` (the CSRF rule: every mutation needs a client id; a Shortcut sends no `Origin`) and a JSON body of `text` (the Shortcut Input), `target`, and `date`/`time` from two *Format Date* actions (`yyyy-MM-dd`, `HH:mm`). A second Shortcut posts photos to `/api/capture/file`. Signed `.shortcut` files cannot be generated outside macOS, so Settings › Quick capture spells out the steps with copyable values instead; the request passes the Host and identity allowlists like the browser does (the phone is a user-owned device of the allowed login).

**Risk.** The server remembers idempotency keys for an hour within one run; a capture whose response was lost just before a restart could, when retried, be appended twice. It is a visible duplicate line, never a loss.

### 6.28 Graph view (ROADMAP item 16)

Two views over the same data, both lazy chunks registered in `extensions/builtins.ts`:

- **Global graph** `/x/graph`: `registerPage({route: 'graph', title: 'Graph', icon: GitFork})`, so it has a ribbon button, a drawer entry and *Open Graph* in the palette. The page fills the workspace (`.graph-page` is absolutely positioned in `WorkspaceScroll`, above the desktop status bar).
- **Local graph**: `registerPanel({id: 'graph', side: 'right', title: 'Local graph'})`, a right-sidebar tab (the bottom sheet on phones, also in the phone ⋯ menu), and the command *Open local graph*. It follows the open note; a depth slider (1–5) and a settings popover (incoming, outgoing and neighbour links, tags, attachments, existing files only, arrows) sit above it, and a button opens the global graph.

**Data** (`features/graph/`). `useGraphData()` reads `['graph']` (`GET /api/graph`, API.md §11.4). The tree query is patched by every SSE event and every own write (§6.3), so the hook subscribes to the query cache while a graph view is mounted and, when `['tree']` or `['settings']` changes, invalidates `['graph']` at most every 800 ms. The refetch sends `since`, and an `unchanged` answer returns the cached object, so an edit that changed no link re-renders nothing. `model.ts` turns the response into nodes with stable ids (the path, `?` + lower target for unresolved links, `#` + lower tag for tags) and builds a **view**: notes filtered by folder, tag and the search filter; attachments, unresolved links and tags as nodes on request (tags linked to their notes); orphans hidden on request; for the local graph a breadth-first walk to the chosen depth along the allowed directions (tags both ways), without links between nodes at the same depth unless *neighbour links* is on. Both directions of a pair become one link with direction bits, which the arrows use.

**Queries** (`query.ts`) use the search grammar of API.md §12.1 (words, phrases, `tag:`, `path:`, `file:`, `-`), evaluated on the device against names, paths, aliases and tags, accent- and case-insensitively. Note text is not searched, so filters and color groups are instant and apply to attachments, unresolved links and tag nodes too. A node takes the color of the first group whose query matches it, as in Obsidian.

**Options** (`options.ts`, `store.ts`) use the names and ranges of Obsidian's `.obsidian/graph.json` (`search`, `showTags`, `showAttachments`, `hideUnresolved`, `showOrphans`, `colorGroups`, `showArrow`, `textFadeMultiplier`, `nodeSizeMultiplier`, `lineSizeMultiplier`, `centerStrength`, `repelStrength`, `linkStrength`, `linkDistance`) plus a folder and a tag filter, so an imported vault's settings carry over (ROADMAP item 24, §6.29). They are per-device preferences in `localStorage` (`notesapp:graph`, `notesapp:local-graph`), saved 400 ms after the last change; the local graph uses the global graph's groups, display and forces. The settings panel has Obsidian's four collapsible sections (Filters, Groups, Display, Forces) and *Restore defaults* (which keeps the groups); on phones it is a bottom sheet over the graph, closed by default.

**Layout** (`forces.ts`, `runner.ts`, `layout.worker.ts`, `layoutClient.ts`). d3-force 3.0.0 (ISC) with Obsidian's four forces: many-body repulsion (Barnes–Hut, θ 0.9, looser above 2,000 and 5,000 nodes, bounded range), link springs (d3's 1/smaller-degree strength times the slider), and x/y centering. It runs in a **module Web Worker** in time slices and posts positions as transferable `Float32Array`s tagged with a graph generation, so stale positions of a previous view are ignored. Without workers (tests, a worker that fails to load) the same runner runs on the page. Nodes keep their position by id when the view changes (a filter, a live update); a new node starts next to a placed neighbour. Graphs above 3,000 nodes settle in about 150 ticks instead of 300.

**Rendering** (`components/graph/renderer.ts`, `GraphCanvas.tsx`). A 2D canvas at up to 2× device pixels. Each frame is one path for all links of a style, one path per node color, and labels in screen space; off-screen nodes and links are skipped. Node radius grows with the square root of the degree; labels fade in with the zoom (moved by *text fade threshold*) and are capped at 600 per frame, the highest-degree ones first; the hovered node and its neighbours are always labelled. A 10,000-note frame draws in about 4 ms. Positions and the view transform live in refs and are drawn on animation frames, so React does not re-render per frame. Colors come from CSS variables on `.graph-view` (`--graph-node`, `--graph-line`, …) for both themes, read again when the theme changes.

**Interaction.** Wheel and trackpad pinch zoom around the pointer; dragging the background pans; dragging a node holds it in the layout; hovering (or pressing, on touch) highlights the node, its links and its neighbours and dims the rest; a click or tap opens it: a file opens, an unresolved link creates its note as following the link would (`openLink` from a note that links to it), a tag opens the search for it. Touch uses pointer events: one finger pans or drags a node, two fingers pinch-zoom around their midpoint (Safari's own gesture events are cancelled), `touch-action: none` on the canvas. Zoom in, zoom out and fit buttons are 44 px on touch screens; the canvas is focusable, with `+`, `-`, `0` (fit) and the arrow keys. Until the user moves the view, the graph is re-fitted as it settles. The canvas reports its zoom and hovered node in `data-zoom` and `data-hover`, for tests.

### 6.29 Compatibility report (ROADMAP item 24)

`/x/compat` (`components/pages/CompatPage.tsx`), registered with `registerPage({route: 'compat', title: 'Compatibility report', icon: FileCheck})` and linked from Settings › About. It reads `['compat']` (`GET /api/compat`, API.md §11.5) on every visit, with a *Check again* button; nothing refreshes it live.

- **Summary**: errors, warnings and notices (occurrence counts), the vault's size, when it was checked; whether `.obsidian/` exists (and that it is never changed), and the files there that could not be read.
- **Obsidian settings**: one row per Obsidian setting (file › key and value, the NotesApp setting, its current value, and *In effect*, Obsidian's value, or *Not used* with the reason). When a row differs, **Apply Obsidian's settings** saves `proposedSettings` with `PUT /api/settings` and `baseVersion: settingsVersion`; a `409` (the settings changed since the report) refreshes the report and asks to check it again. Disabled while the settings are read-only.
- **Graph view**: when `graph.json` has options, **Use them on this device** turns them into graph options (`optionsFromObsidian`: colors `{a, rgb}` become `#rrggbb`, unknown keys are dropped) for this device (§6.28).
- **Plugins**: the enabled core and community plugins, with *Supported*, *Partly*, *Planned* (and the roadmap item), *Not supported* or *Unknown*, and a one-line note.
- **Findings** in three sections (syntax and plugins in notes, links, files), each a collapsed card with its severity icon, count, file count and roadmap item; expanded, it explains the finding and lists the occurrences, each opening the note at its line (`/n/<path>?line=N`, §6.4). Under 768 px the settings table stacks each row; occurrences are 44 px tall on touch screens.

### 6.30 Voice notes (ROADMAP item 33)

Record in the app, save the audio as an attachment, embed it where the cursor was, and, when the server has a transcription service (§5.24), put the transcript under the embed. The code is in `features/voice/` (`recorder.ts`, `voiceNotes.ts`) and `components/overlays/VoiceRecorderPanel.tsx`.

**Starting.** **Record voice note** (command palette, `when` a Markdown note is open) and the microphone of the phone's editing toolbar (§6.16, which then puts the keyboard away so the panel has the room) call `startVoiceNote(session)` inside the tap: iOS lets the level meter's `AudioContext` run only when it is created in the gesture. A note can have one recording at a time. Without `MediaRecorder` or `getUserMedia` (an insecure origin), the toast says why; a refused microphone says where to allow it.

**Recording** (`VoiceRecorder`). `getUserMedia({audio})` with echo cancellation, noise suppression and automatic gain; `MediaRecorder` at 64 kbit/s with a 1-second timeslice, so a recording cut short by the phone keeps what it had. The format is the first the browser supports of AAC in MP4 (iOS Safari, and Chrome where it can: plays on every device), Opus in WebM, Opus in Ogg, then the browser's default. The file is `Recording YYYYMMDDHHmmss.<ext>` in local time, as Obsidian's audio recorder names it: `.m4a` for MP4, `.webm`, `.ogg`. A screen wake lock keeps the phone awake while recording (where the browser has the API). When the microphone goes away by itself (another app takes it, the phone locks), the recording ends and what was recorded is saved, with a toast.

**The place.** At the start, an upload widget (§6.6 #10, verb "Recording") marks the cursor (after a selection, which stays). It is a position in the editor state, never text, so it follows every edit made while recording, on this device or another; a note never shown in an editor during this page load gets the recording at its end. The note's session is held until the recording and its transcript are in, so autosave, drafts and conflicts work whatever the user opens meanwhile, and a recording in progress counts as unsaved work (the `beforeunload` prompt).

**The panel** floats above the workspace and the phone's toolbar: the time (pauses excluded), a level meter, *Into <note>*, a **Transcribe** checkbox (shown when the server can; per device, also in Settings › This device), and **Discard** (asks first after 10 seconds), **Pause**/**Resume** and **Done**. Toasts move above it.

**Saving.** **Done** stops the recorder; the mark's position becomes two widgets, "Uploading Recording …" and, when transcribing, "Transcribing Recording …". The audio goes up as an attachment (`POST /api/attachments`, source = the note); its `markdown` replaces the first widget on a line of its own, and the second widget moves after it (the batch order of §6.6 #10). A failed upload keeps the recording in memory: the toast's **Retry** sends it again, at the cursor.

**Transcribing.** `POST /api/transcriptions` with the attachment's path. The text replaces its widget as a quote, `> ` on every line, starting on a new line and ending a block: a blank line follows unless the next line already is one (Markdown would otherwise draw the next line into the quote). The quote is ordinary note text, so search finds the recording by its words, the outline and history see it, and Obsidian shows it too. No speech recognized: a toast, nothing inserted. A failure (`503 transcription-failed`, the server unreachable) shows the server's reason with **Retry**, which finds the recording's embed in the note again wherever the text moved it. **Transcribe recording on this line** (palette) transcribes any audio or video embed on the cursor's line, for recordings made before the service existed or synced from elsewhere.

**Playback** is the reading view's (§6.7): `<audio controls>` for `.m4a` and `.ogg`; a `.webm` (a `video` kind, §4.2) starts as `<video>` and becomes `<audio>` once its metadata shows no picture, in embeds and in the media view.

**Server side** is §5.24; the `Permissions-Policy` allows the microphone for the app's own origin (§5.14).

### 6.31 Ask your vault (ROADMAP item 18)

The conversation view of §5.25, in `features/ai/`, registered through the extension points (§6.8) by `registerAi()` (called from `builtins.ts`):

- **Where.** A right panel *Ask* (`registerPanel`, icon `Sparkles`): a column on the desktop, the bottom sheet on tablets and phones; and the page `/x/ask` (`registerPage` *Ask your vault*: a ribbon button, an entry in the phone drawer, "Open Ask your vault"), which is how the phone uses it, since the sheet is small with the keyboard up. `openAiView()` picks the panel, or the page on phones. Both render `AiChat` (a lazy chunk with `ai.css`).
- **Commands** (palette): *Ask a question about your notes*; while `settings.ai.enabled` is on, *Summarize current note*, *Suggest links, tags and tasks for current note*, *Summarize current folder*, *Summarize selection* (the editor's selected text, every range) and *Summarize this week's daily notes* (Monday to Sunday of this week in the browser's time zone, with the daily-note format and folder of §6.12, the notes that exist). The same actions are chips above the input.
- **Asking.** `run.ts` adds a turn to the current conversation and streams into it: `aiClient.streamAi` posts with `fetch` (`EventSource` cannot post), reads the body with `sse.ts`, an incremental `text/event-stream` parser, and applies `start` (the notes sent), `delta`, `restart` (a fallback model starts over: the text so far is dropped), `done` and `error`. Refusals before the stream (`ai-unavailable`, `ai-excluded`, `ai-budget-exceeded`) become a short explanation in the turn. **Stop** aborts the fetch, which also stops the answer on the server. Several turns may stream at once.
- **Include the open note.** A checkbox (on by default) sends the open note as `activePath`; the page uses the note last opened on this device (`notesapp:lastPath`).
- **Answers** render with the reading-view pipeline (`renderNote`, §6.7, at depth 1 so no note is transcluded, remote images always as placeholders) into `.markdown-rendered`. A click on a citation resolves it over the tree (from the vault root) and opens the note (closing the sheet); a link that does not resolve shows "… is not a note in this vault" and **never creates a note**, unlike a link in a note. Tags open tag search. Each answer lists the notes sent (each opens the note; *excerpt* marks a cut note), how many matching notes were excluded or did not fit, the model, the estimated cost, and Copy. `max_tokens` and `refusal` stops are explained.
- **Conversations per device** (`conversations.ts`, zustand): in `localStorage` under `notesapp:ai:conversations`, never on the server. A turn keeps its prompt as sent, its label, the answer, the notes and the outcome; follow-ups send the answered turns as `history` (API.md §24.1). The newest 30 conversations of at most 40 turns and 1.5 MB are kept; a page closed mid-answer reads back as *stopped*. The toolbar has *New conversation* and *Conversations on this device* (reopen, delete one, delete all).
- **Privacy in the UI.** Before the first question on a device, a notice says that the question and the text of the notes it needs are sent to Anthropic, and which folders never are (*Got it* stores `notesapp:ai:noticeSeen`). The footer always says "Sent to Anthropic when you ask" (or "Fake answers" on a development server), the model and the month's spend against the budget. While the feature is off, not configured or over budget, the view says why (with a link to Settings, or the key file's path) and the input is disabled.
- **Settings › Ask and summaries** edits `settings.ai`: the privacy explanation, *Use Claude* (off by default), the provider (Claude Code with the subscription, or the Anthropic API), the model (Sonnet 5.5 recommended, Opus 5.5, Haiku 5.5), *Never send* (one path per line), the context per request, the monthly budget, and the status from `GET /api/ai/status`: whether the server has a key (by file name), and this month's estimated spend and requests.
- **Layout.** The panel is a flex column whose messages scroll above the input; on the page the workspace scrolls (§6.16) and the input is sticky at the bottom with the safe-area inset. Both follow a streaming answer unless the reader scrolled up. Inputs are 16 px and chips and buttons 44 px on touch screens; the input is a textarea that grows, Enter sends and Shift+Enter breaks the line (`enterkeyhint="send"`).

**Tests** (Vitest): `sse.test.ts` (any split, CRLF, comments), `aiClient.test.ts` (the request, the events, problems, a cut-off stream, aborts), `conversations.test.ts` (storage, history, limits, damaged data), `actions.test.ts` (the week, citations) and `AiChat.test.tsx` (registration, the off and unconfigured states, the notice, a streamed answer whose citation opens the note, history and the include toggle, an unresolved citation creating nothing).

### 6.32 Calendar (ROADMAP item 14)

The code is in `frontend/src/calendar/`: `model.ts` (the first day of the week, six-week month grids, week numbers, the date-grid keys, the tasks of each day), `repeats.ts` (coming occurrences of repeating tasks), `prefs.ts` (per-device options), `api.ts` (API.md §25), `useCalendarData.ts`, `routes.ts`, `actions.ts`, `register.ts`, and `ui/` (`MonthGrid`, `DayDetails`, the page and the panel, `useSwipe`, `labels.ts`). `register.ts` registers, through the extension points (§6.8), the page `/x/calendar` (ribbon, drawer), the right panel **Calendar** (the bottom sheet on phones), the command *Open Calendar: this week*, and handlers for note events. Days are local calendar days (`YYYY-MM-DD`), like task dates, never instants.

- **What a day shows.** Its daily note (a dot), the notes a frontmatter property dates on it (squares), its tasks (open ones as rings, red when overdue) and the coming occurrences of repeating tasks (a dashed ring). Wide grids (a container query at 560 px, the page on a tablet or desktop) show up to three titles per day instead of the rings: dated notes, then tasks (closed ones struck through), then repeats in italics, and "+n more". A day's accessible name says all of it ("Today, Wednesday, October 21, 2026: daily note, 1 note, 2 open tasks, 1 done").
- **Daily notes are found by formatting each day** with the daily-note format and folder (`dailyNoteEntry`, case-insensitive over the tree), never by parsing names, so any moment format works, week-based ones (`gggg-[W]ww`) included. Opening a day uses the procedure of API.md §15.2 (`openDailyNote(day)`, which *Open today's daily note* now also uses): open the note, or create it from the daily-note template rendered for that day at the current time of day (`atTimeOf`), as Obsidian does: `{{date}}` is that day, `{{time}}` now.
- **Tasks** come from the task store (`GET /api/tasks?view=list&status=all`, kept current by SSE, §6.22): every task of every active list, by its due date, else its start date, as Upcoming groups them; open ones first, each part in schedule order. `FlatTaskRow` gives each its check, and *Add task* opens quick add with the day as due date.
- **Repeating tasks.** A task that repeats from its due date also shows, faintly and without a check, on the later days its rule produces, after today and after its own date (`occurrencesBetween` in `tasks/recurrence.ts`, one pass of rrule with COUNT and UNTIL honoured, at most 2,000 occurrences read from the rule's start). Tasks that repeat from their completion are not projected: their next date depends on when they are done. rrule stays in the calendar's lazy chunks.
- **Notes dated by a property** come from `GET /api/notes/by-date` (API.md §25), one request per visible range (the grid's 42 days, or the week). `calendar.DatedNoteService` scans the index's parsed frontmatter (`VaultIndex.notes()`, a snapshot taken under the read lock; records are immutable) for the asked keys (`date` by default, case-insensitive): a date, a date-time, a wikilink to a day, or a list of them. Templates and drawings are left out, and a day's own daily note is not listed again. The scan reads frontmatter already parsed in memory, so there is no separate date index. The answer is not pushed: note events (`file.*`, `entry.moved`, `folder.deleted`, not for non-note files) refetch it, debounced by 500 ms, as for habits.
- **The page** (`/x/calendar?date=2026-10-21&view=week`): the focused day and the view are the address, so a day can be linked and reloading keeps the place; without `date` the page follows today, across midnight too. *Month* shows six weeks, so the grid keeps its height, with the focused day's details beside it (below it on a phone); *Week* shows the seven days one under another. Previous, Today and Next move by a month or a week. A tap focuses a day; a tap on the focused day, a double click or Enter opens its daily note.
- **The panel** is a compact month like Obsidian's Calendar plugin: a click on a day opens its daily note at once (closing the sheet on phones); it follows the daily note in view, outlined, and shows the focused day's notes and tasks below. Its month name opens the page at that day.
- **Keyboard and touch.** The grid is a WAI-ARIA date grid: one day is in the tab order (a roving tabindex); arrows move by a day or a week, Home and End to the ends of the week, Page Up and Page Down by a month, with Shift by a year, and the grid follows the focus into another month. On touch screens a quick sideways stroke of at least 48 px turns the month (`useSwipe`; the grid has `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom`, so vertical strokes still scroll, and the click that ends a swipe is swallowed). A new month slides in from its side unless reduced motion is asked for. Tap targets are 44 px on coarse pointers.
- **Options** (per device, `localStorage` `notesapp:calendar`, like the theme): the first day of the week (the language's by default, from `Intl.Locale` week info, else CLDR's territory table), week numbers (ISO 8601 when weeks start on Monday, else week 1 holds January 1), and the frontmatter properties that date a note (up to five, comma-separated).

**Tests** (Vitest): `model.test.ts`, `repeats.test.ts`, `prefs.test.ts`, `register.test.ts` (the debounced refetch), `recurrence.test.ts` (`occurrencesBetween`), `daily.test.ts` (`dailyNoteEntry`, `atTimeOf`, `openDailyNote` rendering the template for another day), `CalendarPage.test.tsx` (the month with notes, tasks, dated notes and repeats; opening and creating a day's note from the template; the date-grid keys across months and years; buttons and swipes; the week view; completing and adding tasks; options) and `CalendarPanel.test.tsx` (following the daily note in view, one-click open and create, months, opening the page and dated notes). JUnit: `DatedNoteServiceTest` (values and properties) and `CalendarIntegrationTest` (the endpoint over HTTP, edits, bad requests).

### 6.34 Flashcards (ROADMAP item 34)

The code is in `frontend/src/flashcards/`. The pure parts are `parse.ts` (cards in notes), `ids.ts` (identity), `reviews.ts` and `schedule.ts` (answers and FSRS), `deck.ts` (decks and counts), `session.ts` (a review session), `stats.ts` and `store.ts` (records and the outbox); `ui/` has the page. `register.ts` registers the page, the commands and the event handlers (§6.8). ts-fsrs 5.4.2 (MIT) loads with the page's lazy chunk, never in the main bundle.

**Syntax** (the Obsidian Spaced Repetition plugin's, so a vault works in both apps):

| Written | Cards |
|---|---|
| `Question::Answer` | One; a leading list marker or checkbox is dropped |
| `Question:::Answer` | Two, one each way (siblings) |
| Lines, a line `?`, lines | One. The question and the answer form a paragraph: it ends at a blank line (fenced code in the answer may hold blank lines). |
| Lines, a line `??`, lines | Two, one each way |
| `==deletion==` in a paragraph | One per deletion, with the others shown. `==x==^[hint]` shows the hint in the blank; `==x==[^1]` asks every deletion with the same number together (the plugin's classic clozes). Paragraphs with `::` cards are not read for clozes. |

- **Decks** are flashcard tags (`config.tags`, default `#flashcards`, matched case-insensitively, nested tags included): the note's frontmatter tags first, then each line of tags sets the deck for the cards after it, and tags at the start of a card's first line apply to that card only (the plugin's question-specific tags). Cards before any such line take the note's first flashcard tag. With `foldersAsDecks` the deck is the note's folder (`''` shows as "Vault root"). A deck's counts include its sub-decks.
- **Ignored:** fenced code, `$$` math, `%%` comments, HTML comments (the plugin's way to suspend a card), `::` and `==` inside inline code or a wikilink, and the plugin's `<!--SR:…-->` comments. The headings above a card are kept as its context ("Trivia › Capitals").
- **Identity.** A card's id is `c` plus a 53-bit hash (cyrb53) of its kind and its normalized question (white space collapsed, SR comments dropped). A forward card has the same id written on one line or on several; a reverse card hashes its own question; a cloze hashes the paragraph's plain text with the deletion's text and occurrence (or its group number), so adding a deletion keeps the other deletions' ids. Nothing is written into the note, moving or renaming the note keeps every id, and the same card written in two notes is one card.
- **Edited questions.** Editing a question makes a new id. Adoption keeps the history: a card without a record takes the record of a card that is gone from the same note when its `front` hint is at least 75 % similar (Levenshtein), and the card's next answer carries `from`, so the server moves the record (API.md §34.4). The records of cards in no note can be pruned from the statistics page.

**Scheduling.** FSRS through ts-fsrs with the vault's settings (desired retention, maximum interval, learning and relearning steps, fuzz; short-term steps on). A card's FSRS card is its stored state when that covers its answers, else the replay of its answers in order (cached per record); a `reset` answer starts the card over as new. ts-fsrs seeds its fuzz with the review time and the card, so a replay on another device gives the same due date. The study day starts at `dayStartsAt` local time (Anki's 4 a.m.): a review card is due when it falls due before the next study day starts, a learning card when it is due within 20 minutes (learning ahead). New cards count against `newPerDay` and reviews against `reviewsPerDay`, across every device, from the day's answers.

**Records on the device** (`store.ts`). The server's records are the `['flashcards', 'state']` query, and this device's answers not yet confirmed are laid over them, so the page never waits for the network. Answers and undos go through an **outbox** kept in `localStorage` (a few hundred bytes each). It sends them in order, consecutive answers in one request, and retries with backoff (2, 4, 8, 16, 30 s) after transient and storage failures, and at once when the event stream has caught up or the browser is back online; an operation the server refuses is dropped with a toast. Undoing an answer still in the outbox removes it there; one already sent is undone on the server. `flashcards.changed` patches the query. Note events (`file.*`, `entry.moved`, `folder.deleted`) refetch the source notes a second later, only while the page is open; parsed notes are cached by version.

**The page** (`/x/flashcards`, one ribbon and drawer entry; `?view=review|stats|settings`, `&deck=`, `&note=`):

- **Decks.** Today's count and **Study now** on top, then the deck tree with new, learning and due counts (blue, orange and green, as in Anki), capped by what today's limits leave. A row studies its deck. Without cards, the page shows the syntax.
- **Review.** Built for the iPhone. The card fills the workspace below a 48 px bar (back, the deck, the remaining counts, undo, and a ⋯ menu with Edit in note, Skip for now and Reset progress) and a progress line. A tap on the card shows the answer; **Show answer**, then the four answer buttons (each with the interval it would schedule), sit at the bottom, 56 px tall, clear of the home indicator. A basic card shows the answer under the question; a cloze replaces its blank with the highlighted deletion. Cards render through the reading-view pipeline (§6.7), so links, tags, images and embeds work, and a link opens its note.
- **The queue** has learning cards due now first, then reviews (most overdue first) with new cards (in note order) spread evenly among them; siblings are never shown back to back. A card answered *again* comes back in the session when due; when only such a card is left, the session waits for it, or shows it on request. Answers on other devices and edited notes reach the queue when the next card is picked; the card on screen never changes under the thumb.
- **Keys:** Space or Enter show the answer, then answer Good; 1 to 4 answer Again, Hard, Good, Easy; U or Mod-Z undo; E edits; S skips; Escape ends the session.
- **Statistics.** Today (answers, time, share of *again*), the day streak, retention over 30 days (review answers that were not *again*, young and mature apart at Anki's 21 days), the cards by stage, and two single-series bar charts: answers per day for 30 days, and cards due in the next 30 days.
- **Settings.** The fields of API.md §34.3; only the changed fields are sent.
- **Commands:** Open Flashcards, Review flashcards, Review flashcards in this note (all its due and new cards, without daily limits), Show flashcard statistics.

### 6.35 Habits (ROADMAP item 35)

The code is in `frontend/src/habits/`: `model.ts` (habits from notes, schedules), `progress.ts` (streaks, rates, calendars), `frontmatterEdit.ts` and `write.ts` (note edits), `actions.ts` (check-ins, new habits, changes), `useHabits.ts`, `register.ts`, and `ui/` (the page and the panel).

**A habit** is a note in `Habits/` or tagged `#habit` (API.md §35 has the frontmatter). Its name is `title` or the note's name; its colour is `color`, else one of the charts' eight categorical hues picked from its path, so every device shows the same. Its daily-note property is `property`, else the name in lower case with dashes. A day is done when the note's `entries` list it or that day's daily note has the property set to `true`, `yes`, `done`, `x`, a check mark or a number above 0.

**Schedules** (`schedule`): `daily` (the default), `weekdays`, `weekends`, a list of days (`mon, wed, fri`, also as a YAML list), or `N/week` (also "3 times a week"). Anything unreadable is daily.

**Progress** (local days, `YYYY-MM-DD`):

- **Streaks.** For a day schedule, the current streak counts done days back from today (from yesterday while today is still open); a scheduled day that was missed ends it once more than `maxGap` such days follow each other (Habit Tracker 21's gap tolerance), and days the schedule skips neither count nor break it, though a done day still counts. An N-a-week habit counts weeks (Monday to Sunday) that met the target; the current week counts once met.
- **Rate.** The share of scheduled days done over the last 30 days (four full weeks, each capped at its target, for N a week), from the habit's start: its `created` date, else its first check-in.
- **Calendar.** 53 weeks, Monday on top, the latest week on the right (a phone opens on it and scrolls back). For one habit a day is done (the habit's colour), missed (an outline), not scheduled (faint), or before the start.
